## [New Metric Measures Whether LLM Chain-of-Thought Matches Internal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38972) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces CoT-Interpretability Alignment (CIA), a metric that measures the agreement between an LLM's chain-of-thought (CoT) traces and its internal reasoning strategies as detected by interpretability tools. Evaluating CIA on two-hop question answering, hint intervention, and integer multiplication across three LLMs, the authors find only limited alignment (44.8–75.9%), and show that post-training with task accuracy plus parametric faithfulness rewards can substantially improve CoT faithfulness while maintaining or improving task accuracy. Chain-of-thought traces are widely used as a window into how LLMs reason, so evidence that they often diverge from internal computation undermines trust in model transparency and AI safety auditing. By providing both a measurement framework and a training pathway to improve faithfulness, this work could influence how future reasoning models are evaluated and aligned. The CIA metric compares CoT traces against internal reasoning strategies detected by interpretability tools, and the post-training experiments use both task accuracy and parametric faithfulness signals as rewards. The authors also provide rich analysis of generalization patterns, and code and data are available at https://github.com/yihuaihong/CIA-minimal-repro.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought prompting asks a large language model to write out intermediate reasoning steps before giving a final answer, which often improves accuracy and is treated as a form of explanation. However, prior work such as Anthropic's faithfulness studies has shown that these written traces can be unfaithful—models may reach the same answer through different internal computations, or the stated reasoning may not causally affect the output. Interpretability tools aim to probe a model's internal representations and strategies, and this paper combines such tools with CoT evaluation to quantify what it calls parametric faithfulness.

## [Video2World Benchmarks Coding Agents on Video-to-Simulation Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04432) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Video2World, a benchmark of 222 reconstruction instances derived from 189 robot and human demonstration videos, which evaluates coding agents on autonomously building interactive simulated environments from embodied videos. Testing 9 frontier coding-agent systems showed task success jumping from below 5% to over 15% starting with Claude Opus 5, though large gaps to human-assisted reconstruction remain. Automating video-to-simulation could dramatically scale embodied AI training data by removing the manual environment construction and calibration bottleneck, affecting robotics and agent research. The finding that better visual fidelity does not always yield higher task success echoes the broader gap between perceptual realism and factual correctness in generative models. Video2World measures reconstructed worlds along three axes: geometric fidelity, dynamic fidelity, and functional correctness, capturing spatial perception, physical reasoning, and executable interaction. The benchmark formulates autonomous video-to-simulation as a software engineering task where an agent observes an embodied video, constructs the simulated environment and robot behavior, and iteratively refines results through execution feedback.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Embodied AI refers to AI systems embedded in a physical body that perceive the environment through sensors and act via actuators, and training such systems typically requires large amounts of simulated interaction data. Building interactive simulators from real-world observations is a promising way to scale this data, but current pipelines still rely heavily on manual environment construction and calibration. Coding agents are AI systems capable of autonomously writing and refining code, and this work tests whether they can automate the entire video-to-simulation process end to end.

This sparse conditional computation lets a model hold far more total parameters than it actually computes per token, improving efficiency. On-device inference means running the model locally on hardware such as GPUs or specialized AI chips rather than in the cloud, which reduces latency and keeps data private.

This critique could redirect research toward more rigorous evaluation and temper expectations about XAI as a trust-building tool. The paper frames faithfulness as the degree of approximation between an explanation and the model, and notes that computing it rigorously is either infeasible or computationally expensive with existing metrics. Over the past decade, evidence has suggested that XAI often promotes overreliance on incorrect predictions rather than genuine trust.

## [Equal-Budget Study Finds Simple List Scheduler Beats MCTS for DAG Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12542) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A preregistered controlled study compared Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) against a randomized-restart list scheduler for DAG workflow scheduling under an identical budget of 2048 evaluated candidate schedules. At that budget, MCTS was 2.88% worse than the restarts (95% CI [-3.62%, -2.15%]), and swapping in textbook UCT reduced but did not reverse the deficit (-0.26%, 95% CI [-0.71%, +0.23%]). This is a valuable negative result that challenges the widely proposed assumption that MCTS is the superior approach for DAG workflow scheduling, showing that a much simpler method can win when given equal search effort. It has practical implications for scheduling research and system builders, since it suggests that expensive tree-search machinery may not pay off in this domain. The deficit is not budget starvation: on a separate exploration pool, restarts self-improve by 1.85% while MCTS gains only 0.24%, and 700 of 720 of MCTS's first results are merely the warm start it was handed. The studied selection rule locks deterministically onto the first-expanded child, keeping trials correlated, and the causal link to makespan remains an inference; results are confined to a synthetic domain with 8/16/32 tasks, 2-8 processors, and budgets up to 2048.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm that builds a search tree via random simulations, and it is often proposed for scheduling directed-acyclic-graph (DAG) workflows, where tasks with dependencies must be assigned to processors. List scheduling is a much simpler greedy algorithm that orders jobs and assigns them to machines in that fixed order. Most prior studies never gave the simpler method the same search effort, so this study equalizes the budget to make a fair comparison.

## [EvoSyn Evolves Synthesis Space via Learner Feedback for Agent Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12593) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced EvoSyn, a learning-feedback-driven multi-agent framework that synthesizes executable and verifiable trajectories for interactive agents by evolving the generation space based on learner feedback. Experiments on τ²-Bench and CAR-Bench show EvoSyn improves average task success by 13.3 points over the strongest synthesis baseline while producing higher-quality and structurally broader synthetic experience. This addresses a feedback-to-decision gap in closed-loop learning, where observed weaknesses of a task agent do not directly specify how future data synthesis should change. By turning learner failures into explicit generation controls, it could improve how LLM agents are trained with synthetic experience, benefiting researchers working on agentic reinforcement learning and data synthesis. EvoSyn organizes synthesis around a revisable state specifying what interaction factors can be deliberately generated and where synthesis effort should be allocated, with a Designer allocating synthesis directions and a Planner grounding them into executable experience. The framework supports both within-space adaptation toward weak or under-covered regions and synthesis-space expansion that promotes newly discovered factors into explicit generation controls.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Training interactive LLM agents increasingly relies on synthetic experience, but useful data must be executable, verifiable, and provide learning value for the current agent. Existing methods incorporate environment feedback, data validation, or learner failures, yet observed weaknesses often do not translate directly into changes in how future data is synthesized. EvoSyn is a multi-agent system that closes this loop by revising and expanding its synthesis space based on learner feedback.

## [SpireBench: Benchmarking LLM Agents on Full Slay the Spire Runs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12596) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SpireBench, a new benchmark in which 20 large language models play the complete commercial deck-builder Slay the Spire at its highest difficulty. Frontier models beat heuristic baselines and match the average human player, but fall far behind experts, with the gap widening as runs lengthen. Most agentic benchmarks are short and episodic, making long-horizon strategic reasoning hard to measure; SpireBench offers an unsaturated, dissectable testbed where early decisions determine long-term success. Its findings suggest current frontier models still lack robust long-term planning and risk management, which matters for anyone deploying agents on multi-step real-world tasks. The harness is the first to give a model the complete original game, and a comprehensive self-consistent renaming of every game term shows part of performance rests on prior knowledge of the game. Models do not improve from memories they write across runs, which are often wrong and overly cautious, and all models misjudge risk by preserving health rather than spending it to grow stronger.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Slay the Spire is a roguelike deck-building game in which players construct a deck of cards, fight through a branching map of enemies, and make irreversible decisions whose consequences only appear much later in a run. Long-horizon planning in LLM agents refers to the challenge of making sequences of interdependent decisions with delayed feedback, which is much harder than the short, episodic tasks most agent benchmarks test. SpireBench uses this game as a controlled environment to separate stored knowledge, reasoning, and luck in agent performance.

<a id="item-54"></a>
## [Behavioral Self-Rewarding Refines Binary Rewards for SWE Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12598) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Behavioral Self-Rewarding (BSR), a reward-refinement method that reuses the policy itself to compare observable debugging behaviors among trajectories from the same task with the same verifier outcome. BSR aggregates pairwise preferences into task-local scores and converts them into bounded auxiliary rewards, preserving the verifier-defined pass/fail ordering while adding reward contrast. Binary verifiable rewards cannot distinguish trajectories with substantially different debugging quality, so BSR provides additional supervision from already-collected trajectories without new environment interactions. Experiments on SWE-bench Verified and SWE-bench-Live show consistent improvements across Qwen3-14B and Qwen3-32B configurations, suggesting the policy's own comparative judgments can be a complementary learning signal for training software engineering agents. BSR only compares trajectories that share the same task and the same verifier outcome, so it never overrides the correctness signal from executable tests; it converts pairwise trajectory comparisons into bounded auxiliary rewards. The method is evaluated on SWE-bench Verified and SWE-bench-Live with Qwen3-14B and Qwen3-32B, and it is an arXiv preprint without community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) trains models using objective, programmatically verifiable signals such as executable unit tests, which provides reliable supervision for software engineering agents but yields only binary pass/fail feedback. SWE-bench Verified and SWE-bench-Live are benchmarks for evaluating software engineering agents on real-world GitHub issues, where an agent must produce a patch that passes hidden tests. Self-rewarding methods let a model judge its own outputs to create training signals without a separately trained reward model.

## [CLEAT: Value-Guided Clarification Training for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12655) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CLEAT (CLarification through valuE-guided Agent co-Training) is a new framework that co-trains a clarification-value controller alongside the task policy, using paired rollouts from the same interaction history to estimate the residual query value of asking a clarifying question. With Qwen3-4B, it achieves the highest mean score in all ten evaluation settings across four benchmarks, with gains over the strongest baseline reaching 13.2 percentage points on UserGym Travel and 6.5 points on IN3 underspecification judgment accuracy. Clarification is only useful when an agent can convert the user's answer into better decisions, so this work addresses a core bottleneck in interactive intent alignment for LLM agents operating under underspecified instructions. By making clarification guidance adapt as the policy learns, CLEAT points toward agent designs that ask fewer but more valuable questions, which could improve reliability in tool-use and human-AI interaction scenarios. The framework formulates residual query value as the task utility gained by asking rather than proceeding under the current policy, and estimates it via paired rollouts that share the same interaction history. Ablations show that co-training improves the task policy even when the controller is removed at inference, and that adapting clarification guidance to the evolving policy yields further gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents can execute complex tasks via tool calls, but users often leave goals and constraints unstated, causing incorrect actions and task failures. Prior work has framed clarification as a decision problem, for example modeling tool-argument uncertainty as a POMDP with an Expected Value of Perfect Information objective. CLEAT builds on this line by learning clarification value jointly with the task policy rather than treating question-asking as a fixed heuristic.

<a id="item-56"></a>
## [MagicBeanStalk Escapes RLVR Zero-Advantage Trap via Recursive Data Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12659) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12659) from NVIDIA and University of Washington researchers introduces MagicBeanStalk, a top-down decomposition and bottom-up recursive data-synthesis algorithm that grows seed problems into policy- and budget-calibrated training problems. Continual RLVR training on these synthesized problems improves gpt-oss-120b from 61.2 to 69.8 (+8.6) and DeepSeek v4Flash from 72.4 to 77.9 (+5.4) across 8 math benchmarks, far exceeding the +1.1 gain from training on the full 78K source corpus. This work identifies a fundamental inefficiency in RLVR data scaling: roughly 88% of problems in a large open-source math corpus fall into a zero-advantage trap where every rollout either succeeds or fails, providing no learning signal. By showing that recursive synthesis of calibrated hard problems can dramatically outperform simply scaling or filtering real data, it offers a practical new direction for RLVR data collection and could reshape how future reasoning models are trained. The zero-advantage trap is characterized experimentally as a function of the problems, target policy, and training rollout budget; filtering can only select existing problems and discards the hardest ones. MagicBeanStalk preserves the seeds' reasoning blueprints while generating substantively new problems, and in finance it synthesizes over 14K verifiable problems from only 340 solutions, improving SecQue by 6.9 and FinanceBench by 2.4 points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method that fine-tunes language models using automatic, rule-based checkers (e.g., math answer verification) instead of learned reward models or human raters, and it underpins reasoning models like DeepSeek-R1 and OpenAI's o-series. In RLVR, a model samples multiple rollouts per problem; if all rollouts succeed or all fail, the advantage signal is zero and no gradient update occurs—this is the zero-advantage trap. Data-centric scaling has focused on collecting or generating more verifiable problems, but corpus size alone does not guarantee a useful learning signal for a given policy and rollout budget.

<a id="item-57"></a>
## [Aligning Unimodal Encoders with Few Paired Anchors via Distilled Optimal Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12781) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12781) proposes a method that aligns two independently pretrained unimodal encoders using only N ≈ 100–500 paired anchors while exploiting abundant unpaired data. It represents each item by its similarities to the anchors, matches the two point clouds with a single entropic optimal transport problem over the entire pool, and distills the resulting plan into two lightweight encoders so the correspondence becomes inductive. Cross-modal alignment normally requires millions of paired examples, so this work could substantially lower the data cost of building image-text or molecule-text systems. It is especially relevant for domains such as chemistry or low-resource languages where paired data is scarce but unpaired data is plentiful. The method's marginal constraints counteract hubness, the phenomenon where a few points become nearest neighbors of disproportionately many others, and the authors relate this constraint to one-step hub corrections from bilingual lexicon induction. Pairing is required only for the anchors, so no item outside the anchor set needs both modalities observed; experiments on Flickr30k, MS-COCO and ChEBI-20 show gains over existing approaches at the same anchor budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Optimal transport is a mathematical framework for finding the cheapest way to move one probability distribution onto another, and its entropic variant adds a regularization term that makes the problem easier to solve at scale. Bilingual lexicon induction is the classic NLP task of learning word translations between languages from limited supervision, and it has long dealt with hubness when matching embedding spaces. This paper combines these ideas to align independently trained unimodal encoders, such as an image encoder and a text encoder, without large paired datasets.

<a id="item-58"></a>
## [EvoBelief: Falsifiable World Models for Self-Improving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12789) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoBelief introduces a self-evolving world model that represents environment knowledge as falsifiable hypotheses, allowing an LLM agent's beliefs to evolve during deployment without fine-tuning the underlying language model. On ALFWorld without actor-side transition memory, it improves success over a matched Frozen World Model by 23.9% with Claude Haiku 4.5 and 31.3% with GPT-5.4, and in DiscoveryWorld's Reactor Lab it completes 83.3% of Normal-tier and 58.3% of Challenge-tier instances versus 0% for all evaluated baselines. This work points to a complementary axis of self-improvement for LLM agents: evolving testable, revisable environment knowledge rather than updating model parameters, which could improve robustness and continual learning in deployment. It matters for agent developers because it offers a way to recover from stale or incorrect beliefs and to reuse learned rules across tasks without retraining. Unexpected outcomes and decision-relevant unknowns trigger hypothesis formation, and later observations or controlled experiments provide independent validation before a rule can guide predictions or plans within its supported scope; counterexamples narrow or retire rules as the environment changes. The language model remains frozen, and each rule stays linked to its evidence and the decisions it changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are systems that use a large language model to perceive an environment, make decisions, and act, often in interactive benchmarks such as ALFWorld, MiniGrid, and DiscoveryWorld. A world model is an internal representation of how the environment changes in response to actions, and it is typically used for planning and prediction. Self-evolving agents usually improve their policies, reflections, or skills from experience, but their beliefs about environment dynamics can still be incomplete, incorrect, or outdated. EvoBelief instead treats those beliefs as falsifiable hypotheses that can be validated, narrowed, or retired during deployment.

## [VEPA: Verified Evolving Formal Policy for LLM Agent Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12813) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VEPA introduces a framework that represents LLM agent guardrails as formal policies of executable symbolic rules over typed agent events, and uses an LLM guided by deployment feedback to propose policy patches that fix newly observed failures while preserving previously correct behavior. A deterministic verifier accepts only structurally valid and conflict-free policies, and an evidence-aware authority layer activates a verified rule only when it has sufficient supporting evidence. This addresses a critical gap in AI safety by combining the determinism and auditability of formal verification with the adaptability needed for lifelong LLM agents operating over sensitive resources and mutable system states. It could enable safer deployment of agents in high-stakes settings where inconsistent or ambiguous LLM-based guardrail decisions are unacceptable. VEPA consistently outperforms baselines across ASSEBench-Safety, ASSEBench-Security, and ATBench while expanding the scope of deterministic formal-policy enforcement, and these gains hold under more challenging deployment conditions including mixed streams, corrupted feedback, and longer horizons. The evidence-aware authority layer ensures uncertain updates cannot directly affect guardrail decisions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents increasingly operate over sensitive resources such as private documents and mutable system states, creating serious safety and security risks. Runtime guardrails are meant to make inspectable, reproducible, and deterministic allow-or-refuse decisions before potentially irreversible actions are executed, but safety requirements are rarely complete before deployment. Existing adaptive guardrails learn from deployment experience but apply policies through LLM inference, leading to inconsistent decisions, while formal guardrails enforce policies deterministically but lack mechanisms to adapt to newly observed risks.

## [Paper Proposes Formal Mathematical Definition of AI Creativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12845) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.12845) proposes formal postulates and a conceptual mathematical definition of creativity for AI applications, grounded in Boden's three criteria of newness, value, and surprise. The authors validate their creativity formula in two domains—evaluating specific chess moves and assessing paintings by artists such as Picasso and Renoir—showing it captures creative patterns across both. Computational creativity has long lacked a conceptually grounded yet empirically applicable formal definition, and this work attempts to unify psychological theory with a measurable mathematical structure. If adopted, such a definition could give researchers and developers a shared, quantitative way to evaluate creativity in AI systems across art, science, and games. The proposed formula is built on three fundamental criteria—newness, value, and surprise—whose specific measurements are adapted to each domain, meaning the same mathematical structure is reused while the operationalization of each criterion changes. As a theoretical arXiv preprint, the work offers initial validation on chess and painting but no broader empirical benchmarking or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Computational creativity is the field that aims to model, simulate, or replicate creativity using computers, and it has traditionally struggled to define creativity in rigorous computational terms. Boden's influential framework holds that a creative idea must be new, valuable, and surprising, but prior work has often offered either informal conceptual definitions or formal definitions without validation. This paper tries to bridge that gap by turning Boden's criteria into a single mathematical formula and testing it in concrete applications.

## [RGR Regularization Mitigates Capability Degradation in LLM Self-Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12855) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12855) finds that On-Policy Self-distillation (OPSD) can progressively degrade previously acquired capabilities in LLMs, even as overall performance improves, and proposes Representation Geometry Regularization (RGR) to mitigate this. RGR preserves intra-cluster compactness and inter-cluster separation in hidden representations and can be integrated into existing OPSD methods without changing their core optimization procedures. This work exposes a hidden trade-off in self-improvement training: models can gain new skills while silently losing old ones, which is critical for continual learning and deployment of LLMs. The proposed RGR offers a lightweight fix that could make self-distillation pipelines more reliable for researchers and practitioners. The degradation is linked to deteriorating representation geometry, specifically reduced intra-cluster compactness and diminished inter-cluster separation in hidden representations. Experiments across multiple OPSD methods and benchmarks show RGR generally alleviates degradation while retaining capability improvements, and code is available at https://github.com/supermachinelearner/RGR.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: On-Policy Self-distillation (OPSD) is a self-improvement paradigm where the same LLM acts as both teacher and student under different conditioning contexts, using on-policy rollouts to align outputs and reduce exposure bias. Representation geometry refers to the structural properties of hidden-layer embeddings, such as how tightly samples of the same class cluster together (intra-cluster compactness) and how well different classes are separated (inter-cluster separation). Regularization techniques that preserve such geometric properties have been explored in other machine learning settings but are newly applied here to LLM post-training.

## [CToM Framework Trains LLMs to Anticipate User Responses and Self-Revise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12872) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12872) introduces Collaborative Theory of Mind (CToM), a framework that trains large language models to draft a response, predict how the user will reply, and use that prediction to internally revise before responding. Combined with group-relative policy optimization (GRPO), CToM raises final accuracy on a collaborative math reasoning task from 74.1% to 79.6% over naive GRPO, while cutting user replies by 8% on coding and 17% on writing. LLMs remain weak at extended multi-turn collaboration, often proposing solutions before understanding user needs or failing to correct mistaken assumptions after feedback. CToM directly targets this gap by making user-response prediction a training signal, which could improve both the quality and efficiency of human-AI collaboration in reasoning, coding, and writing workflows. During training, the user's actual reply to each draft serves as the prediction target, and the assistant minimizes the negative log-likelihood of that reply; this prediction objective is combined with GRPO to optimize task performance and interaction efficiency jointly. Ablations show that adding prediction supervision reduces user replies by 8% on coding and 17% on writing under the same reward, and behavioral analyses link more accurate predictions to larger self-revision gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Theory of Mind (ToM) refers to the ability to reason about the mental states of oneself and others, and recent work has assessed how well LLMs possess this capacity. Multi-turn dialogue research shows that LLM-based systems often struggle to track dependencies across turns and maintain consistency as conversations lengthen. GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm, originally developed for improving reasoning in LLMs, that optimizes policies by comparing groups of sampled responses rather than relying on a separate value model.

## [Seq-Drifting Enables One-Step Text Generation via Drift Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12879) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers from Yee Whye Teh's group propose Seq-Drifting, a framework that learns a direct map from Gaussian noise to full text sequences in a single forward pass by applying drift to continuous token representations. It achieves a generative perplexity of 36.01 on LM1B in one step and performs competitively on translation, summarization, and reasoning tasks. Diffusion language models currently require multiple sampling steps to produce high-quality text, which limits inference speed; Seq-Drifting's one-step generation could significantly accelerate DLMs and offer a new paradigm beyond autoregressive and iterative denoising approaches. If validated, this could affect how future language models are designed for latency-sensitive applications. Unlike existing DLMs that derive supervision at each position directly from the observed token, Seq-Drifting builds a drift field whose position-wise targets adapt to the current generated context, using attraction toward contextually plausible token representations and repulsion to prevent collapse across samples and repetition within sequences. The code is available at https://github.com/jyliu-ai/Seq-Drifting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models (DLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising a sequence in parallel rather than predicting tokens one at a time like autoregressive models such as GPT. Drifting Models, introduced in recent work, formulate generative modeling as a training-time evolution of the pushforward distribution via a drift field, naturally enabling one-step generation at inference. Seq-Drifting extends this drifting paradigm from images to text by operating on continuous token representations, addressing the key limitation of multi-step sampling in DLMs.

## [GeoSpatialBench Tests Multimodal LLMs on Geospatial Reasoning and Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12881) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced GeoSpatialBench, a new benchmark that evaluates multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on an escalating progression of spatial cognition, from spatial relation understanding and geometric reasoning to constrained spatial editing. Grounded in real-world urban data from six geographically diverse cities, it covers 28 topological, directional, metric, and ordering relations across structured GeoJSON and map image modalities, with ground truth verified via GIS operations. Existing spatial reasoning evaluations rely mostly on static question-answering over small-scale or synthetic scenes, so they rarely test whether models can actually modify spatial configurations under explicit geometric constraints. GeoSpatialBench exposes a stark spatial gap that matters for embodied AI, robotics, and any system that must build grounded world models of physical environments. Evaluation across frontier MLLMs shows structured GeoJSON consistently outperforms map images, multimodal combinations offer no reliable gain over GeoJSON alone, and models handle qualitative topological and ordering relations reasonably well but drop on quantitative metric relations. Notably, editing performance on ordering relations declines relative to QA, and most failed edits violate at least one specified spatial constraint.

GeoJSON is a standard JSON-based format (RFC 7946) for encoding geographic features such as points, lines, and polygons, and is widely supported by mapping libraries. Benchmarks are standardized test suites used to compare model capabilities, and this work adds one focused on geospatial cognition and editing rather than static QA.

## [OGPO: Offline Goal-conditioned Policy Optimization for Recoverable VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12914) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose OGPO, an offline goal-conditioned policy optimization framework that improves vision-language-action (VLA) models by relabeling demonstrations with semantic key states and constructing goal-conditioned process rewards. Experiments on LIBERO and MetaWorld show consistent gains over standard supervised fine-tuning, and evaluations on LIBERO-Plus and the new LIBERO-ReAct benchmark demonstrate stronger robustness and goal-directed recoverability under distribution shifts. VLA post-training has largely relied on trajectory-level supervised imitation, which biases policies toward demonstration-specific action paths and offers little guidance once execution deviates. OGPO shifts post-training from action imitation to key-state reachability learning, potentially making robotic manipulation policies more robust and recoverable without additional online interaction. OGPO adaptively relabels offline trajectories with semantic key states and optimizes the policy to reach these task-progress states using goal-conditioned process rewards, requiring no additional online interaction. The authors also introduce LIBERO-ReAct, a mid-execution perturbation benchmark designed to evaluate recovery from off-demonstration states.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take visual observations and natural language instructions and directly output low-level robot actions, pioneered by Google DeepMind's RT-2 in 2023. They are typically built by fine-tuning a vision-language model on large-scale datasets pairing observations and instructions with robot trajectories. Post-training of these models usually uses supervised imitation over demonstrations, while offline goal-conditioned reinforcement learning and process reward models provide related techniques for learning from pre-collected data with dense, step-level signals.

## [AI Shopping Assistants Distort Retail Assortment Planning, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12917) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12917) models retail assortment planning when an AI intermediary shortlists products before customers choose, using both deterministic segment-specific rankings and stochastic Plackett-Luce mediation. Using real product rankings from leading AI models, the authors find that ignoring AI mediation causes mean revenue losses of 2.52%–11.37%, while a simple deterministic plug-in based on fitted AI utility ordering limits losses to at most 0.53%. As AI shopping assistants increasingly shape which products consumers even consider, retailers that optimize assortments under traditional models may systematically misprice and mis-stock their catalogs, leaving substantial revenue on the table. The paper also shows the deterministic-mediation version of the problem admits a fully polynomial-time approximation scheme for any fixed number of customer segments, making it tractable for practical e-commerce deployment. The empirical pipeline estimates Plackett-Luce ranking models from AI-generated product rankings and evaluates them on held-out assortments, then combines these estimates with synthetic customer preferences. The key caveat is that customer preferences are synthetic rather than observed, so the reported revenue gaps are simulation-based; the deterministic plug-in's strong performance is what motivates the subsequent complexity analysis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Retail assortment planning is the problem of choosing which products from a broader pool to stock in order to maximize expected revenue. The multinomial logit model is a standard discrete-choice tool for predicting which item a customer picks from a set, while the Plackett-Luce model generalizes this to full rankings by modeling how items are sequentially selected. This paper adds a new layer: an AI intermediary that first filters the retailer's catalog into a customer-facing shortlist, so the retailer must optimize against the AI's ranking behavior rather than directly against consumer choice.

## [Language as Supervision boosts neural decoding accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes Language as Supervision (LaS), which aligns neural features with natural-language class descriptions via learned projections and predicts classes using candidate text prototypes. Evaluated across seven encoder architectures for visual, motor, and speech decoding from scalp and intracranial recordings, LaS improves speech-decoding accuracy by up to 17.15 percentage points over matched class-label supervision baselines. This work suggests that natural-language descriptions can serve as general-purpose references for neural decoding, potentially improving brain-computer interfaces for communication in people with paralysis. The ability to reuse candidate sets and adapt across subjects and sessions could reduce calibration burden and make BCI systems more practical. LaS enables conditional decoding of unseen frequencies in steady-state visual evoked potential (SSVEP) responses, and dual-sided attribution visualization shows how neural and textual contributions combine into the same class-matching score. The method supports label-supervised subject and session adaptation, though the paper does not report performance on all possible decoding tasks or clinical populations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Brain-computer interfaces (BCIs) decode neural activity to restore communication or control for people with motor and speech impairments. Traditional decoding relies on class-label supervision (CLS), where each neural sample is mapped to a discrete label, leaving relationships among classes implicit. Language as Supervision instead uses text descriptions of classes as a richer supervisory signal, leveraging advances in multimodal learning where text and other modalities are aligned in a shared space.

## [Uncertainty-Guided Contraction Cuts Reasoning Post-Training Cost 450x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12933) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12933) proposes an uncertainty-guided progressive contraction framework that compresses reasoning supervision for post-training large language models. Experiments show it uses as little as 0.22% of the optimization budget of full-data SFT — over 450x less training exposure — while maintaining or improving reasoning performance. Scaling reasoning post-training with ever-larger corpora has driven up training costs without proportional performance gains, so this method could substantially reduce the compute and data budget needed to train strong reasoning models. It also generalizes across heterogeneous student families, black-box teachers, and weak-to-strong transfer, suggesting broad applicability. The framework builds Monte Carlo reasoning trees around intermediate reasoning states to expose process-level uncertainty, first contracting the corpus into a compact teacher-informed pool and then performing learner-aware online routing during SFT. It generalizes across heterogeneous student families, black-box teachers, and weak-to-strong transfer, indicating it can also enhance reasoning capability rather than merely accelerate training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning post-training teaches large language models to produce step-by-step solutions, but scaling it with ever-larger teacher-generated corpora raises training costs. Monte Carlo tree search is a technique that simulates many possible paths from a given state to estimate which branches are most promising, and here it is used to profile uncertainty across reasoning trajectories. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) is the standard post-training stage in which a model is trained on curated input-output examples.

## [MathVis Benchmark Tests Whether LLMs Can Reason by Drawing Diagrams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12954) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces MathVis, a benchmark of 512 tasks that evaluates LLMs' ability to visualize frontier mathematical problems and perform diagram-assisted reasoning. Each task pairs a mathematical statement with a reference diagram, executable TikZ or SVG code, a Gold Intermediate Representation (IR), atomic rubrics, and error codes; frontier models GPT-6-Astra, Claude Fable 5, and Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview score only 31.25%, 26.56%, and 22.85% pass@1. The results suggest that even the strongest current models cannot yet use diagrams as a genuine reasoning medium, which matters for AI research on mathematical problem solving, geometry, and multimodal reasoning. It also gives the community a rigorous, executable-code-based yardstick for measuring progress toward models that simplify reasoning through visual representations. Although the tasks demand visualization, the models' outputs are dominated by formal languages, symbols, and graphics programs rather than explicit diagram-mediated reasoning, and solutions that contain all necessary elements are often visually confusing to humans. The benchmark's inclusion of executable TikZ/SVG code, a Gold IR, and error codes enables fine-grained, reproducible evaluation beyond simple pass/fail scoring.

SVG is an XML-based vector graphics format standardized by the W3C that can be rendered by browsers and edited as text. An intermediate representation (IR) is a structured intermediate form of a program used in compilers, and here the Gold IR serves as a canonical, checkable representation of a correct diagram.

## [SmartSalesBench: A Resistance-Aware Multi-Turn Benchmark for LLM Sales Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12975) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SmartSalesBench, described as the first multi-turn conversational benchmark explicitly designed to evaluate LLM sales agents against psychologically grounded, resistant buyer personas. It comprises five agent profiles, twelve buyer personas, fifteen smartphone products across six price tiers, and four controlled experiments with three iterations, totaling 13,680 sessions, with both agent and persona generations grounded via retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). Existing task-oriented dialogue benchmarks typically assume cooperative users and measure task completion, so SmartSalesBench fills a gap by testing agents against users who actively resist and challenge them across conversations of up to twenty turns. This could influence how conversational AI and agent evaluation is conducted in commercial settings such as sales, where persuasion and objection handling matter more than simple task completion. The benchmark introduces nine evaluation metrics grouped into three tiers: primary outcome metrics (CR, BRS, RIR), secondary quality metrics (SMS, PFS, ETI), and exploratory metrics planned for future reporting (ITS, ARI, SPI). Evaluation combines automated LLM-as-judge scoring with internal human assessment (n = 50, kappa = 0.71, Pearson r = 0.81 with BRS), indicating moderate-to-good agreement between automated and human judgments.

Sales-agent evaluation is a specialized form of agent evaluation, where metrics typically cover outcomes, interaction quality, and safety.

## [Continuous-Query Generation Removes Finite-Beam Reachability Limits in Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12977) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12977) proposes two continuous-query generation paradigms, CQG-Single and CQG-AR, that replace discrete Semantic ID (SID) decoding in generative recommendation. CQG-Single generates one retrieval query in a single pass, while CQG-AR autoregressively produces continuous residual updates, eliminating the SID item-token vocabulary, code lookup, and discrete beam expansion. Under a unified protocol with valid-prefix constrained decoding, CQG-Single outperforms the TIGER-style baseline in all seven runs while using only 55.3% as many trainable parameters. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of SID-based generative retrieval: finite-width beam search cannot guarantee that all relevant items are reachable in the returned list. By making every encoded item directly scoreable through dense or approximate-nearest-neighbor retrieval, the approach could simplify large-scale recommendation pipelines and decouple output representation, autoregressive modeling, and retrieval budget. It has clear implications for industrial systems that rely on generative recommendation at scale. CQG-AR remains comparable to the baseline on ML-1M and is numerically stronger on the Beauty and Sports datasets. A controlled depth ablation shows that additional continuous refinement does not monotonically improve shallow accuracy, suggesting that more autoregressive steps are not always better. The paper also distinguishes catalog-wide scoreability from empirical top-K support, a nuance often conflated in prior work.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Generative recommendation (GR) is an emerging paradigm in which a model autoregressively generates item identifiers to retrieve recommendations, much like a language model generates tokens. Many systems represent items as Semantic IDs (SIDs), discrete codes derived from item embeddings, which are decoded token by token using beam search. Because beam search keeps only a finite number of candidate sequences, some items may be unreachable in the final returned list, a limitation this paper calls the finite-beam reachability constraint.

## [Work2Skill Turns Human GUI Demos into Reusable Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12984) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released Work2Skill, a framework that captures human GUI experience through WorkRecorder, which synchronizes screen recordings, mouse and keyboard events, and accessibility-tree data, and used it to build WorkDemo-350, described as the first synchronized multimodal dataset of 350 human desktop GUI demonstrations screened for completeness and task success. They also propose an agentic skill-induction method that inspects evidence on demand to produce reusable skills capturing workflow rules, step dependencies, and completion checks, achieving mean score improvements of 4.67-10.54 points on MyPCBench Bounded Action and 3.11-7.50 points on a 286-task subset of OSWorld-Verified over a No Skill baseline across four execution models. Computer-use agents still struggle with unfamiliar software and workflows that require human domain knowledge, so a dataset and method that convert human demonstrations into reusable skills could significantly improve agent generalization across desktop applications. By releasing both the framework and data openly, the work gives the GUI-agent community a shared benchmark and training resource for skill induction, an area where progress has been limited by the lack of synchronized multimodal demonstration data. WorkRecorder aligns three modalities: screen recordings, mouse and keyboard events, and available accessibility-tree information, and the 350 demonstrations in WorkDemo-350 were screened for completeness and successful task execution. The skill-induction method is agentic, meaning it inspects demonstration evidence on demand rather than processing everything upfront, and evaluation used four execution models under shared execution settings, though the abstract itself is incomplete and no community discussion is available to validate the results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Computer-use agents are AI systems that operate graphical user interfaces by clicking, typing, and navigating applications on behalf of a user. The accessibility tree is a hierarchical representation of UI elements that operating systems expose for assistive technologies and automation tools, and it provides structured information about buttons, text fields, and other controls. Skill induction refers to deriving reusable, generalizable procedures from demonstrations so an agent can apply them to new but similar tasks, rather than memorizing a single workflow. Prior GUI datasets such as GUI-World focus on multimodal understanding and video benchmarks, while Work2Skill targets synchronized demonstration capture specifically for skill induction.

## [Hyperbolic VAE Boosts Cross-Category Patent Prior-Art Retrieval by 52%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12997) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.12997) trains a hyperbolic and a matched Euclidean Variational Autoencoder on 45,000 US patents across 96 runs and 6 seeds, finding that the hyperbolic model retrieves genuinely cross-category prior art 52% more often (18.76% vs. 12.34% of top-10 results over 3,000 queries). The authors also show that a domain's classification breadth and patents-per-subgroup predict method performance (Pearson r=-0.642 and r=0.715, respectively), and release a complete 91-test automated implementation. Patent search systems are structurally organized around classification codes, which makes cross-code discovery difficult; this work suggests that unsupervised hyperbolic representations can recover relevant connections that code-restricted search misses. The finding is significant for representation learning and patent search practitioners, though its impact is domain-specific. The hyperbolic model agrees less with official classification (NMI 0.2392 vs. 0.3206), which the authors explain geometrically as the two measures capturing different goals, with only one aligning with prior-art search needs. The study spans 10 technology domains and 50,000 additional patents, and reports all findings with statistical transparency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Prior-art retrieval is the process of finding earlier patents or publications relevant to a new patent claim, and it is typically constrained by classification codes such as CPC. Hyperbolic embeddings map hierarchical or tree-like data into spaces of constant negative curvature, which can represent taxonomies with less distortion than standard Euclidean spaces. A Variational Autoencoder (VAE) is a generative model that learns a compressed latent representation of data, and Normalized Mutual Information (NMI) is a bounded measure of agreement between two clusterings, often used to evaluate clustering quality.

## [HSAD Trains a Small Advisor to Steer Frozen LLM Agents via Hindsight Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13000) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Hindsight Structured Advice Distillation (HSAD), a method that trains a small advisor model to read each input and write structured advice appended to the prompt of a frozen executor agent. On the FrontierFinance benchmark, an 8B advisor raises the qualification rate of Samaya's proprietary research agent from 52.9% to 56.6%, exceeding the agent's own high-effort configuration at 37% lower cost per query. This offers a cheap, modular way to improve specialized agents in domains like finance, healthcare, and law without retraining closed or shared systems, which is often impossible. It also shows generalization to WebShop, BIRD text-to-SQL, and ExpertLongBench, suggesting the approach could become a general pattern for steering frozen agents. The advisor is trained with hindsight: a teacher that sees graded answers and execution traces writes the advice that would have helped, and the student advisor learns by supervised fine-tuning to produce that advice from the input alone. Because the student is a separate small model rather than the executor, training is cheap and adds little latency, though the abstract does not report detailed ablations or failure cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Language-model agents are increasingly deployed for specialized work such as financial research, healthcare, and law, but they often miss task-specific details like which filing reports a figure. Retraining such agents is expensive and often impossible when they are closed or shared systems, while stuffing all missing know-how into a single static prompt makes it long and identical for every input. HSAD builds on distillation with privileged information, a teacher-student paradigm where the teacher uses extra training-only data to guide the student.

## [LQET Trains Language Models to Update Beliefs by Evidence Provenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.13008) introduces Latent Quotient Evidence Training (LQET), a relational objective that combines sparse Bayesian supervision with constraints so language models update beliefs based on report lineage rather than report count. On a controlled binary benchmark, LQET reduced new-evidence update RMSE by 41-44% and log-odds drift after eight relay steps by 89-91% relative to task-only training across Qwen3-4B and Phi-4-mini. In repeated human-AI interaction, a single observation can be quoted and summarized into multiple reports, making repetition look like independent corroboration and causing models to become overconfident. LQET offers a route toward dynamic human-AI systems in which influence follows new evidence rather than the number of times the same evidence returns, which is relevant to AI safety and reliability. The method uses sparse Bayesian supervision plus constraints that encourage stable predictions when reports add no new evidence and consistent updates to the same new evidence across relay histories. Results were validated with three paired training seeds per backbone and extended to a closed-loop simulation where model confidence controls how many relayed reports re-enter the model's context in later rounds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly used in multi-turn human-AI feedback loops, where information is quoted, summarized, and passed along. A core problem is that repeated reports of the same underlying observation can be mistaken for independent corroboration, inflating confidence. Bayesian belief updating provides a principled way to combine evidence, but standard training does not teach models to track where evidence came from. LQET addresses this by training models to use supplied report lineage, or provenance, when updating beliefs.

Researchers introduced EmbodiedCLBench, the first benchmark in embodied simulation for evaluating the continual learning capability of self-evolving agents, using compositional generalization as its core evaluation principle. The benchmark tests whether agents can first learn from basic tasks and then recombine the acquired skills to solve complex, unseen advanced tasks. This work addresses a clear gap in the literature, since existing continual learning research remains largely confined to non-embodied settings. By offering a standardized benchmark and revealing consistent limitations across multiple agent harnesses and learning methods, it provides valuable resources for designing effective self-evolving agents in embodied environments. Experiments show that advanced tasks are hardly solvable under zero-shot settings, but experience from basic tasks yields consistent improvement that remains stable even as task complexity increases. However, this improvement does not scale with the number of learning samples, indicating current agents cannot extract meaningful information from additional experience.

## [ReTRAIL Lets Long-Horizon LLM Agents Rewind Environments While Keeping Learned Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13058) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced ReTRAIL, a framework that lets long-horizon LLM agents rewind the environment to earlier states while carrying forward knowledge gained from abandoned exploration branches. It trains a shared policy with two complementary signals: State-Anchored GRPO, which compares alternative continuations from revisited states, and a branch-advancement reward that guides reflection based on the progress of subsequent exploration. Long-horizon agents often get trapped in dead-end trajectories after early mistakes trigger cascading failures, so the ability to recover proactively is critical for robust real-world deployment. ReTRAIL shows that environment rewinding alone is insufficient and can even hurt performance, highlighting that coupling rewinding with experience reuse is the key to better long-horizon task solving. Experiments across four interactive benchmarks — MineSweeper, Sokoban, ALFWorld and ScienceWorld — show that simply adding a rewind primitive provides limited benefit and can degrade performance, whereas ReTRAIL enables agents to rewind from unproductive states and use insights from abandoned branches. The code is available at https://github.com/whi497/ReTRAIL.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly deployed in long-horizon environments where they must execute extended sequences of decisions, and early errors can cascade into dead-end trajectories. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning method popularized by DeepSeek-R1 that reduces compute requirements by comparing groups of sampled outputs rather than training a separate value model. Benchmarks like ALFWorld and ScienceWorld provide interactive text-based environments for evaluating such agents.

## [LongHarness Enables Agentic Reasoning Over 100M-Token Corpora](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LongHarness is a model-agnostic agent harness that introduces Evidence-Conclusion Coevolution and Evidence Lifecycle Management to make the interaction between acquired evidence and evolving conclusions explicit, enabling reasoning over corpora exceeding 100M tokens. Evaluated on OfficeQA Pro V1/V2, AA-LCR, and LongBench v2 with GLM-5.3-Flash and Qwen3.8-Flash, it boosts GLM-5.3-Flash exact match on OfficeQA Pro from 15.8% to 60.2% on V1 and from 4.4% to 61.1% on V2, surpassing Claude Code by 12.0 points on V1 and Codex by 18.9 points on V2. This work addresses two coupled failure modes in long-context agentic reasoning—unbounded exploration and evidence-state drift—by making state management explicit, which could significantly improve how LLM agents handle large document collections. The model-agnostic design means the harness can be paired with any backbone model, potentially influencing future agent architectures and retrieval-augmented generation systems. LongHarness uses unresolved conclusions to guide exploration toward missing information while newly acquired evidence strengthens or revises those conclusions, and Evidence Lifecycle Management keeps accumulated evidence current and traceable. With Qwen3.8-Flash, it reaches 61.7% on OfficeQA Pro V1, outperforming Claude Code by 8.3 points and Codex by 12.0 points, though the results are from an arXiv preprint without peer review or community validation yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Corpus-level long-context reasoning requires agents to acquire and integrate evidence across document collections that exceed their context windows. Existing agent harnesses enable incremental access to these corpora, but acquired evidence and evolving conclusions often remain entangled in a growing interaction history, leading to implicit state management problems. An agent harness is a framework that provides memory, tools, and structure around a language model to manage long-running tasks, and evidence-state drift refers to previously acquired evidence or corrections failing to inform the current conclusion.

## [Attention Sees, MLP Refuses: Tracing the Safety Pathway in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13078) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13078) reframes LLM safety failures as an attention-to-MLP pathway, showing that harmfulness is decoded more readily from attention updates while harm-selective refusal writing concentrates in MLP updates across 18 Base-Instruct pairs spanning Qwen, Llama, and Gemma. The authors introduce Sentinel, which combines an attention-derived perception score P with an MLP-derived decision score D, and report that conditional routing with both signals achieves a lower aggregate error rate than single-signal routing across eight models. This work offers a measurable, mechanistic account of why safety-aligned models both get jailbroken and over-refuse, which could inform more targeted defenses and interpretability research. If the attention-to-MLP pathway generalizes, it suggests that safety interventions should treat perception and refusal writing as separate, separately manipulable stages rather than a single monolithic behavior. Post-training strengthened MLP refusal writing in all 18 pairs without systematically improving harmfulness decodability, and positive MLP writing became more concentrated in deeper layers after post-training. Across six reasoning-enabled models, harmfulness remained decodable during reasoning while strong refusal writing concentrated at answer onset, and causal interventions showed that resetting downstream MLP updates to clean values substantially reduced the refusal shift caused by upstream attention interventions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Decoder-only Transformers process information through a residual stream, where each layer's attention sublayer and MLP sublayer add updates to a shared hidden representation. Prior interpretability work has shown that refusal behavior in LLMs is mediated by a direction in this residual stream, and that ablating it can bypass safety refusals. This paper builds on that line of research by asking not just whether refusal exists, but which module—attention or MLP—actually writes it, and how that writing relates to the recognition of harmful content.

## [Black-Box Causal Audit Exposes Model-Dependent Audio Grounding in LALM Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13112) introduces a black-box causal test that localizes a claim's evidence region via CLAP similarity, removes it by spectral gating, and compares against a random-window control across a 2x2x2 grid on three large audio-language models (LALMs). The study finds that localized ablation's raw AUROC (0.613) trails the blank-waveform baseline (0.721) on Qwen2-Audio-7B, but this is a duration confound rather than a localization failure, and the same evidence corrects decoding to raise Qwen2-Audio accuracy from 57.1% to 71.9%. This work provides a new evaluation framework for diagnosing object hallucination in LALMs, showing that a unified causal-grounding profile does not transfer between models and that different models operate under different grounding regimes. It also corrects a duration confound in prior Audio-Aware Decoding (AAD) methods, which matters for researchers building more reliable audio-language systems and hallucination mitigation techniques. The audit uses a 2x2x2 grid across three LALMs, and when duration is held fixed, localization beats random removal at every cell on both models, though raw detection value remains model-dependent, especially on Audio Flamingo 3. The corrected decoding approach combines blank and language-prior contrasts, gated and ensembled, and the source code plus a detailed report of findings and limitations are available at https://github.com/khalooei/causa.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large audio-language models (LALMs) take audio and text as inputs and answer questions about the audio, but they frequently exhibit object hallucination, affirming sounds that are absent. The dominant mitigation, Audio-Aware Decoding (AAD), contrasts logits under real audio against an all-zero "blank" waveform, which prior review flagged as out-of-distribution. CLAP (Contrastive Language-Audio Pretraining) is a multimodal model that learns joint audio-text representations and is used here to localize evidence regions, while spectral gating is a noise-reduction technique that masks frequency bands below a threshold and is repurposed here to remove specific audio evidence.

## [DRYLAB-Bench: First Benchmark for LLM Agent Biosecurity Risk in Mutation Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13116) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced DRYLAB-Bench (Design Risk Yielded by LLM Agent in Biodesign), the first comprehensive benchmark that evaluates the high-risk biological mutation design capabilities of LLM agents across 30 hazardous mutation-design scenarios distilled from biosecurity guidelines. The benchmark scores agent designs against 187,695 empirical wet-lab measurements from ProteinGym and ViroGym using a new Biosecurity Risk Score (BRS), and evaluations of 15 frontier models show that 67.6% of one-shot designs exceed the average measured variant without tools, rising further when biological design tools (BDTs) are added (ΔBRS=+0.116, top-decile share 23.5%). This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety and dual-use research by moving beyond assessing whether models possess hazardous knowledge to quantifying whether agentic LLMs can actually design dangerous biological mutations. It provides a scalable, safe ground-truth verification method that could inform governance, model evaluation, and guardrail development for frontier AI systems with biological capabilities. The benchmark uses a multi-round, tool-augmented environment where LLMs orchestrate BDTs to iteratively refine mutation candidates, and it relies on empirical wet-lab measurements from ProteinGym and ViroGym rather than hazardous validation. The authors note that equipping models with BDTs significantly amplifies risk, and the code is available at https://github.com/drylab-bio/DRYLAB-Bench.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Biological design tools (BDTs) are AI- and ML-enabled technologies that accelerate the design-build-test-learn cycle of engineering biology, and they raise dual-use concerns because the same capabilities that enable beneficial research could be misapplied to engineer enhanced pathogens or drug-resistant bacteria. Dual-use research of concern (DURC) refers to life sciences research that is intended to provide clear benefits but could easily be misapplied to cause significant harm. Prior biosecurity evaluations of LLMs mostly tested whether models possess hazardous knowledge rather than whether they can practically apply it, and they often overlooked risk amplification from BDTs.

## [KnowForge Splits Locating and Reading into Specialized Agents for Multimodal Document QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13128) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces KnowForge, a role-specialized tool-agent pipeline built over a hybrid-retrieval knowledge-point index, where a Locator agent can only search, a code-scheduled bridge judge forces a second retrieval hop, and a Deep Reader agent can only read text, images, and tables. Using a self-hosted Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (3B active), KnowForge reaches F1 47.5 on the full open-domain M3DocVQA benchmark (2,441 questions, 3,368 PDFs), which is +11.0 over the published M3DocRAG system and +7.1 over one-shot retrieve-and-answer on the same index and model, and 53.5 with DeepSeek-V4-Flash. The results suggest that separating the locating and reading jobs across specialized agents can substantially outperform a single self-scheduling retrieval-augmented agent, which is the dominant design in current RAG systems. If the gains hold, this could influence how multimodal document QA pipelines are architected for large heterogeneous PDF collections, affecting both research and production systems that must answer questions over many documents. Per-job ablations attribute the gains: the Deep Reader contributes 3.8, 9.8, and 0 points on the three corpora, tracking how visual each is, while merging both agents into one with all tools and twice the rounds loses 1.9 F1 because it keeps searching instead of reading. A round-budget sweep shows a step at four Locator rounds and a plateau beyond, and query latency scales with model calls rather than corpus size; the authors also release all prompts and runner code and serve the three indexed benchmarks as read-only knowledge bases.

Benchmarks like M3DocVQA, MultiHop-RAG, and MMLongBench-Doc measure performance on open-domain, multi-hop, and single-document questions respectively, and are used here to compare KnowForge against prior systems.

## [Foresight Model Reframes Road-Network Trajectory Generation as Behavior Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13131) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2610.13131) introduces Anticipatory Generative Modeling, a graph-constrained generative paradigm for road-network trajectory generation, along with a model called Foresight. Foresight formulates an "anticipate–select–update–re-anticipate" loop that resolves next transition selection at intersections by anticipating how the future route may unfold toward the destination, and the authors report it outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. Trajectory generation underpins urban applications such as traffic management and urban planning, and existing diffusion-based methods often produce trajectories that drift off the road network because they simulate locations rather than decisions. By shifting from location simulation to behavior modeling, this work could yield more realistic, road-consistent synthetic trajectories for downstream urban computing tasks. The paper argues that movement along road segments is largely deterministic, so the critical uncertainty lies in intersection transitions where multiple outgoing paths may appear equally plausible but lead to very different downstream routes. Foresight is a probabilistic model that maintains longer-term foresight while continuing sequential generation along the historical trajectory; as a preprint, it has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Road-network trajectory generation aims to synthesize plausible vehicle paths, typically evaluated by comparing generated trajectories with real ones using distributional metrics such as Jensen-Shannon divergence. Diffusion models have become a mainstream generative approach for this task, as seen in works like DiffTraj, which combines diffusion with spatial-temporal features of real trajectories. Graph-constrained generation, in which outputs must conform to a graph structure, has also been explored in domains such as house-layout generation with House-GAN.

## [DP-MIND enables differentially private million-token LLM inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13132) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced DP-MIND, a memory-guided framework that scales differentially private (DP) inference to million-token contexts by locally perturbing inputs and processing them chunk by chunk with a compact memory. The memory construction policy is optimized with reinforcement learning on perturbed inputs, guided by final answer correctness and memory consistency, plus a local denoising module that recovers perturbation-corrupted answer spans. Existing DP inference methods are designed for short or moderate contexts and degrade severely at million-token scale, so DP-MIND addresses a real gap for privacy-preserving deployment of long-context LLMs. This matters for LLM-as-a-Service providers and users handling sensitive or confidential documents who need formal privacy guarantees without sacrificing utility. DP-MIND provides a theoretical privacy analysis and is evaluated on multiple long-context benchmarks with context lengths up to the million-token scale, consistently outperforming existing DP and long-context inference baselines in scalability and utility. The approach relies on local input perturbation plus a denoising module so the original context is never exposed to the external model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-Service lets users query large models remotely, but sending sensitive or confidential context to an external provider raises privacy concerns. Differential privacy (DP) is a principled technique that adds calibrated noise so an observer cannot reliably infer whether any single input was included, but applying it to inference over very long inputs is hard because noise accumulates and long-context models already struggle with capacity limits. DP-MIND combines DP input perturbation with a memory mechanism that carries task-relevant information across chunks, similar in spirit to memory-augmented and chunked long-context processing pipelines.

## [Competence-Aware Orchestration Adapts LLM Multi-Agent Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13144) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13144) proposes a two-stage competence-aware framework for LLM-based multi-agent systems that first models the backbone's task-conditioned competence for the current query, then uses that signal to adapt role activation and the communication graph. Across six benchmarks the method reaches an average score of 93.81%, and with six LLM backbones it improves high-competence backbones from 87.22 to 89.97 accuracy while cutting token cost by 36.26%, and adds 4.58 points for lower-competence backbones. The work addresses a key inefficiency in LLM-based multi-agent systems: more collaboration is not always better, since richer coordination helps on hard queries but adds token cost and redundant interactions. By tying collaboration intensity to inferred backbone competence, it offers a practical way to cut inference costs without sacrificing accuracy, which matters for anyone deploying multi-agent LLM pipelines in production. The framework is two-stage: it first estimates the backbone's task-conditioned competence for the query, then uses that estimate to decide which roles to activate and how agents should communicate. Experiments span six benchmarks and six LLM backbones of varying competence, and the same trend holds on unseen backbones, though the abstract does not detail the competence-estimation method or its overhead.

**Background**: LLM-based multi-agent systems (MAS) assign different roles to multiple LLM agents that collaborate to solve complex reasoning tasks, aiming to outperform a single agent through role specialization. A communication graph defines which agents exchange information with which others, and richer graphs generally mean more coordination but also more token usage. The paper's central concept, task-conditioned competence, refers to how capable the underlying LLM backbone is judged to be on the specific query at hand, which the framework uses to decide how much collaboration is worthwhile.

## [VIGOR: Verifiable Geometry Optimization for LLM-Generated Industrial Scene Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13145) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.13145) introduces VIGOR, a method that combines supervised scene-program generation with execution-grounded verification and staged geometric refinement to repair industrial scene programs generated by LLMs. The authors also release an execution-grounded dataset of 1,749 constructible scenarios, each with complete executable programs and 94 final-state geometric checks, and report that VIGOR achieves 96.86% Complete Feasibility and an Overall score of 91.20, beating the strongest baseline by 10.42 points. The work highlights that executable LLM-generated layouts can still violate coupled engineering constraints such as boundary, collision, clearance, aisle, and structural rules, a problem the authors call the executability-engineering-feasibility gap. By making this gap measurable and showing that explicit verification plus coordinated repair substantially improves feasibility, the paper points toward more reliable AI-driven industrial design and verification pipelines. VIGOR uses supervised fine-tuning for complete program generation, local projection to fix independently correctable violations, and joint conflict-graph repair to resolve residual multi-object conflicts while preserving structural relations. The evaluation is limited to controlled industrial layout conditions, and the paper is a preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

Execution-grounded verification means checking that a generated change behaves correctly by actually running it in a controlled environment and observing the resulting state. Staged geometric refinement decomposes a hard geometry problem into successive stages, first resolving simple violations and then coordinating more complex, coupled conflicts.

## [TQS: A Structural Scoring Framework for Selecting Coding-Agent Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13152) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.13152) proposes TQS (Trajectory Quality Score), a structural scoring framework that evaluates coding-agent interaction trajectories using process signals such as information focus, intent-action alignment, and policy dynamics, rather than only whether the final patch passes the test suite. Using 155,144 successful trajectories from a public open-license corpus, the authors rank trajectories by TQS and show that models fine-tuned on top-ranked subsets consistently outperform those trained on random or bottom-ranked subsets on SWE-Bench-Verified. This work challenges the common practice of outcome-level filtering in supervised fine-tuning, showing that process quality of trajectories is an important and previously overlooked supervision signal. Because TQS requires no LLM judge and runs in linear time, it offers a scalable, low-cost alternative to LLM-based data selection that could directly improve how open coding agents are trained. TQS operates directly on trajectory structure and organizes quality signals around "structured convergence," covering information focus, intent-action alignment, and policy dynamics. The evaluation is validated on a large corpus of 155,144 successful trajectories and benchmarked on SWE-Bench-Verified, though as a preprint it has not yet undergone peer review and no community discussion is available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents are AI systems that autonomously inspect files, call tools, and edit code to solve software tasks, and they are typically trained by supervised fine-tuning on interaction trajectories. The standard filtering criterion keeps only trajectories whose final patch passes the benchmark test suite, which ignores inefficient patterns like repetitive file inspection or delayed modification that still get imitated. LLM-as-a-judge evaluators are an alternative for assessing agent trajectories, but they are expensive and slow, motivating cheaper structural metrics like TQS.
