## [CoMMa: Contribution-Aware Medical Multi-Agents for Decentralized Oncology Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09159) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose CoMMa, a decentralized LLM-agent framework for oncology decision support in which specialist agents operate on partitioned clinical data streams rather than sharing inputs. It introduces agent-specific finetuning and a contribution-aware aggregation mechanism that uses deterministic embedding projections to approximate each agent's marginal utility, yielding explicit and interpretable credit assignment. Most existing multi-agent medical frameworks assume centralized data access and prompt-based role assignment, which limits their use in privacy-sensitive clinical settings. By enforcing data decentralization and providing interpretable credit assignment, CoMMa could make multi-agent AI decision support more deployable and trustworthy for hospitals handling sensitive patient data. CoMMa replaces stochastic, narrative-based reasoning with deterministic embedding projections to approximate each agent's marginal utility, providing a stable decision pathway aligned with clinical requirements. It is evaluated on multiple oncology benchmarks, including real-world multidisciplinary tumor board datasets from large academic hospitals in North America and East Asia, as well as public datasets, showing strong performance and generalization across heterogeneous clinical settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems powered by large language models (LLMs) assign different roles to separate agents that collaborate on a task, and they have shown promise for clinical decision support such as oncology. However, most such frameworks assume all agents can access a shared, centralized dataset and rely on prompt-based role assignment, which conflicts with privacy regulations and data-siloed hospital workflows. Decentralized agent frameworks, in which agents operate on local data and coordinate without central orchestration, have emerged as an alternative, and contribution-aware aggregation — weighting each participant by its measured value — is a related idea from federated learning used to improve fairness and model quality in heterogeneous settings.

<a id="item-173"></a>
## [NeuronSpark: Selective Spiking State Space Model for Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16148) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NeuronSpark introduces a self-attention-free spiking language model with input-dependent membrane dynamics, where each neuron's decay rate, gain, and firing threshold adapt to the current token content. At 1.1B parameters and 2.1B tokens, it achieves a 40.0 eight-task zero-shot average, exceeds comparable-budget Pythia/Mamba checkpoints, and reduces operation-energy proxy by 90.1%/67.0% versus Transformer/Mamba at 2,048 tokens. This work addresses a central obstacle in spiking neural networks for language modeling — uniform firing sensitivity across tokens — and demonstrates that billion-scale spiking language model pretraining from random initialization is feasible without backbone transfer, conversion, or distillation. If validated, it could open a path toward energy-efficient NLP at scale, an increasingly important goal as large language models grow more expensive to train and serve. The model combines input-dependent spiking membrane dynamics with gated associative memory and adaptive temporal aggregation, and the authors relate the neuron to fixed-parameter spiking models and the memory to linear attention. It retains 95.2%/93.2% of fully trained Pythia/Mamba scores, spontaneously develops a fast–slow hierarchy, and a physical FPGA path reaches 24.000s TTFT and 0.082440 TPS — though this is an arXiv preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Spiking neural networks (SNNs) are brain-inspired models that transmit information via discrete spikes rather than continuous activations, firing only when a neuron's membrane potential crosses a threshold. State space models (SSMs) such as Mamba are sequence models that scale nearly linearly in sequence length and offer an alternative to Transformer attention, but they have historically underperformed attention in language modeling. Adaptive computation lets a network dynamically allocate more or fewer computational steps based on input complexity, and NeuronSpark applies this idea to spiking dynamics so that different tokens trigger different firing behavior.

## [DEAF Benchmark Exposes Audio MLLMs' Reliance on Text Over Sound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18048) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced DEAF (Diagnostic Evaluation of Acoustic Faithfulness), a benchmark of over 2,700 conflict stimuli spanning emotional prosody, background sounds, and speaker identity. Evaluating seven Audio MLLMs, they found a consistent pattern of text dominance: models are sensitive to acoustic variations but their predictions are predominantly driven by textual inputs. This work reveals a critical gap between high performance on standard speech benchmarks and genuine acoustic understanding in Audio MLLMs, challenging the assumption that these models truly process sound. It provides a rigorous diagnostic methodology that could influence how future audio-language models are evaluated and designed. The benchmark uses a controlled multi-level evaluation framework that progressively increases textual influence, from semantic conflicts in content to misleading prompts and their combination, allowing researchers to disentangle content-driven bias from prompt-induced sycophancy. Diagnostic metrics quantify model reliance on textual cues over acoustic signals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Audio Multimodal Large Language Models (Audio MLLMs) are AI systems that combine large language models with audio encoders to process both speech and text, enabling tasks like speech recognition and audio question answering. Benchmarks like DEAF are designed to test whether these models genuinely integrate acoustic information or simply rely on textual patterns, a key concern for building robust multimodal AI.

## [ChemCost Benchmark Tests LLM Agents on Chemical Cost Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07251) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces ChemCost, a benchmark of 1,427 evaluable reactions grounded to a frozen pricing snapshot covering 2,261 chemicals and 230,775 supplier quotes, designed to evaluate LLM agents on chemical procurement cost estimation with exact, judge-free scoring and stage-level diagnosis. Experiments with frontier, open-weight, and chemistry-specialized agents show the strongest models reach only 50.6% accuracy within 25% relative error on clean inputs, degrading substantially under controlled noise injection. This work addresses a gap in scientific tool-use evaluation by providing exact, judge-free ground truth instead of curated demonstrations or LLM-as-judge scoring, which could improve how agentic AI systems are benchmarked in specialized domains like chemistry and cheminformatics. It shows that tool access alone is insufficient for solving practical scientific tasks, highlighting the need for better parsing, evidence integration, and tool-use convergence in LLM agents. The benchmark supports scalar scoring and stage-level diagnosis of grounding, retrieval, procurement, and arithmetic failures, and includes controlled noise-injected views that perturb chemical aliases, quantity expressions, missing fields, and input formatting. Stage-level analysis reveals failures arise from brittle parsing, ineffective evidence integration, invalid pack selection, and non-convergent tool use.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly used for tool-using tasks, including in chemistry where they can plan syntheses and invoke domain-specific tools. However, evaluating these agents rigorously is difficult because many benchmarks rely on curated demonstrations, expert assessment, or LLM-as-judge scoring rather than exact ground truth. Chemical procurement cost estimation is a practical task requiring agents to ground chemical identities, retrieve supplier quotes, select valid purchasable packs, normalize quantities, and compute costs from a reaction description.

## [FactoryBench: A New Benchmark for Industrial Machine Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07675) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced FactoryBench, a benchmark of over 70k Q&A items grounded in roughly 15k normalized episodes from FactoryWave, AURSAD, and voraus-AD, organized along four causal levels (state, intervention, counterfactual, decision) instantiating Pearl's ladder of causation and five answer formats. Zero-shot evaluation of six frontier LLMs shows no model exceeds 50% on structured levels or 18% on decision-making. This benchmark exposes a wide gap between current AI capabilities and operational machine understanding in industrial settings, highlighting that frontier LLMs still struggle with causal reasoning over robotic telemetry. It provides a structured evaluation framework that could guide future research in AI/ML and robotics toward more robust industrial applications. The benchmark uses deterministic scoring for four structured answer formats and an LLM-as-judge voting protocol for free-form answers, with a scalable Q&A generation framework built around structured question templates. The underlying FactoryWave dataset is a dense, multitask, multivariate sensor dataset collected from a UR3 cobot and a KUKA KR10 industrial arm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Pearl's ladder of causation is a three-level framework for causal reasoning: association (observing correlations), intervention (predicting effects of actions), and counterfactual (reasoning about hypothetical scenarios). Industrial robotic telemetry refers to sensor data collected from robots during operation, such as joint positions, forces, and task states. Evaluating whether AI models can understand such data at higher causal levels is important for deploying them in real-world manufacturing and automation.

<a id="item-177"></a>
## [MAGE: Multi-Agent Self-Evolution via Co-Evolutionary Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10064) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MAGE (Multi-Agent Graph-guided Evolution) externalizes self-knowledge into a four-subgraph co-evolutionary knowledge graph that stores both teacher-written failure corrections and the learner's own correct reasoning traces, retrieved as task-conditioned guidance for a frozen execution model. During evolution, the graph, a task-level search bandit, and a skill-level routing bandit are updated from the same reward stream while the learner's backbone stays unchanged, and across nine benchmarks spanning math reasoning, multi-hop QA, spatio-temporal analysis, financial and medical reasoning, an open-world survival game, and web navigation, MAGE outperforms prompt-based frozen-backbone baselines. This work addresses a known pain point in self-evolving agents — how to preserve knowledge across iterations without updating the backbone model — by making the knowledge graph itself the evolving substrate, which could enable continual improvement of weak or frozen models at inference time. It also connects multi-agent systems, knowledge graphs, and reinforcement learning, offering a reusable design pattern for teams that cannot afford to fine-tune large backbones. The paper provides structural analysis showing that append-only memory growth, bounded curriculum coverage, and task-filtered retrieval together support stable improvement of the retrieval substrate, and ablations reveal that self-harvested success traces and teacher-written corrections are complementary — success memories help most on reasoning-template-heavy tasks while corrective memories support harder composition and interaction settings. Code is available at https://github.com/RuiyiYang01/mage-alg.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolving language-model agents must decide what to learn next and how to retain that knowledge across iterations; existing systems typically carry this cross-iteration knowledge as natural-language feedback, flat episodic memory, or implicit reinforcement signals, none of which cleanly supports a frozen weak backbone at inference time. A knowledge graph is a structured representation of entities and relations, and a multi-armed bandit is a classic reinforcement-learning formulation that balances exploration of new options against exploitation of known good ones. MAGE combines these ideas so that the graph and bandit policies co-evolve while the underlying language model remains frozen.

## [Adaptive optimizer picks per-layer geometry between SGD and Muon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19781) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2605.19781v2) introduces a data-driven criterion that dynamically selects proxy-optimal Linear Minimization Oracle (LMO) geometries for individual neural network layers, using closed-form expressions derived from gradient and activation statistics via a single-step random feature regression surrogate. The framework interpolates between SGD and Muon updates and, with parameter-wise preconditioning, recovers SGD, Muon, Adam, and MuAdam as special extrema, achieving only about 3% runtime overhead on highly optimized baselines. Current optimizers like Muon impose fixed matrix-wise geometry constraints chosen by design or empirically, which may not be optimal for a given problem's geometry. This work provides evidence that LMO geometry can be adapted efficiently from runtime data, opening a new pathway for optimizer design beyond static geometries and potentially improving training performance across diverse scenarios. The criterion is derived in closed form from gradient and activation statistics using a single-step random feature regression surrogate model, and the method is paired with efficient computational strategies to keep overhead low. As a proof of concept, the data-driven optimizer beats or remains competitive with the best performing optimizer between Muon and AdamW across three different training scenarios, though the paper is a preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Modern optimizers like Muon impose matrix-wise geometry constraints on their updates, which can be unified under Linear Minimization Oracle (LMO) theory. Muon is an optimizer for hidden layers that uses updates based on the Schatten-infinity norm, while Adam and AdamW use parameter-wise adaptive updates. Schatten-p norms are a family of matrix norms defined via singular values that generalize several fundamental norms used in machine learning and optimization.

## [Latent Reward Steering Adaptively Promotes Cognitive Behaviors in Reasoning LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00726) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Latent Reward Steering (LRS), an adaptive inference-time framework that optimizes sparse-autoencoder (SAE) latent states to implicitly promote cognitive behaviors in reasoning LLMs. LRS trains a latent reward model on reasoning traces using final answer correctness, then uses reward gradients to apply state-specific corrections only to fragile latent states flagged by a reward and confidence gate. This approach addresses a key limitation of explicit behavior-level control, which is insufficiently adaptive when failures and required corrections vary across reasoning states, tasks, and models. It could influence future work in LLM reasoning and interpretability by offering a more flexible, state-aware steering mechanism. LRS does not rely on predefined cognitive behaviors or steering directions derived from them; instead, it learns a reward model on reasoning traces by final answer correctness to estimate the quality of intermediate latent states. Experiments on multiple reasoning LLM backbones and benchmarks show consistent improvements over various baselines, and post-hoc analyses indicate that LRS implicitly promotes good cognitive behaviors that fix original reasoning errors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are a mechanistic interpretability technique that decomposes polysemantic LLM activations into sparse, more interpretable feature representations, helping researchers understand internal model states. Inference-time steering refers to modifying a model's behavior during generation without retraining, often by intervening on activations or latent states. Reward models are commonly used in LLM reasoning to provide training signals or select answers, and here they are adapted to guide intermediate latent corrections.

## [EvoDrive Uses LLM Agents and Pareto Evolution for Safety-Critical Driving Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03678) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoDrive introduces the first automated, LLM-based agentic evolution framework for multi-objective safety-critical scenario generation in autonomous driving. It employs a simulator-grounded actor-critic architecture with a memory-driven actor, filtering critics, and a self-evolving world evaluator, and maintains a Pareto archive to preserve diverse attack-realism trade-offs, significantly expanding the Pareto frontier on MetaDrive and CARLA benchmarks. This work addresses a key trade-off between adversariality and realism in safety-critical scenario generation, which is essential for validating and improving autonomous driving systems. By combining LLM agents with multi-objective Pareto optimization, it could produce more valuable test scenarios for policy training and enhance the robustness of autonomous vehicles. EvoDrive uses a memory-driven actor to iteratively propose improvements to generators, critics to filter implausible candidates, and a self-evolving world evaluator to route promising proposals and optimize simulation budgets. It maintains a Pareto archive of evaluated candidates to guide future evolution via simulation feedback, and benchmark results show it expands the Pareto frontier across various generators and produces valuable scenarios for policy training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Safety-critical scenario generation is a technique for testing autonomous driving systems by creating rare but dangerous driving situations that expose potential failures. Multi-objective optimization, also known as Pareto optimization, deals with problems involving more than one conflicting objective, seeking a set of best-tradeoff solutions rather than a single optimum. Actor-critic architecture is a reinforcement learning approach that combines a policy-based actor, which selects actions, with a value-based critic, which evaluates them. EvoDrive integrates these concepts with large language model (LLM) agents to automate the generation process.

## [Study Finds Intervention Timing for AI Agents Is a Low-Reliability Construct](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04296) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.04296v2) evaluates four intervention trigger families — absolute state thresholds, composite state-action patterns, regex reasoning-feature extraction, and zero-shot LLM-as-judge — against human-annotated intervention points on SWE-bench-Verified debugging traces. It reports three findings: a 'State Saturation Trap' where modeled frustration saturates and threshold triggers fire on 39-83% of actions, a capability-and-context floor for LLM judges (F1 only 0.17-0.40 at up to 90x cost), and poor human inter-rater reliability (location Krippendorff's alpha = +0.047). The findings suggest that single-annotator F1 is an unsuitable optimization target for runtime safety layers, which matters as autonomous agents move from conversational systems to long-horizon software execution where deciding when to interrupt is critical. It implies that current affect-based triggers and LLM judges may not reliably time safety interventions, affecting AI safety research and agent design. The State Saturation Trap converts threshold-on-state triggers from moment detectors into near-constant indicators, and a v2 erratum notes the observer could not register recovery because decay never ran. LLM judges only escape zero-firing with full-trajectory context, and human annotators agree on intervention type not at all for pause (degenerate) and below chance for clarify, with reflect only at alpha = +0.226.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous AI agents increasingly execute long-horizon software tasks, so runtime safety layers must decide when to interrupt them. This paper uses a continuous 18-dimensional affective-dynamics engine called HEART as a diagnostic probe and evaluates triggers on SWE-bench-Verified, a human-validated subset of the SWE-bench coding benchmark. LLM-as-judge is a common technique for scalable evaluation, but its reliability for timing interventions is questioned here.

## [Study Finds Most Multi-Agent LLM Workflows Don't Beat Single-Agent Baselines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05670) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2606.05670v2) introduces BenchAgent, a shared evaluation framework that compares six multi-agent LLM workflow configurations against a single-agent reference called BenchAgent Core across ten reasoning, coding, and tool-use benchmarks. Using GPT-4.1 with 3,302 instances per configuration over three runs, only EvoAgent consistently outperformed the baseline with 79.70% mean pooled accuracy versus 76.65% for Core, while the other five multi-agent systems trailed by 1.71 to 8.76 percentage points. This study challenges the widely held assumption that adding more agents automatically improves LLM task performance, showing that most multi-agent workflows actually increase token costs without accuracy gains. It provides practitioners with concrete evidence for workflow selection and highlights answer preservation and resource allocation as key optimization directions for future multi-agent research. LLM-Debate gained 9.67 points on HumanEval but lost 1.96 points in pooled accuracy while consuming 1.39 times the tokens of Core, and ChatEval gained 22.25 points on IFEval but still trailed Core overall with 4.34 times its token consumption. The study used fixed task sets, shared tool and scoring interfaces, and identical step limits for Core executions to ensure controlled comparison.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM workflows use multiple language model instances that collaborate, debate, or divide labor to solve tasks, and are often assumed to outperform a single agent. BenchAgent is the evaluation framework introduced in this paper, while BenchAgent Core serves as the single-agent reference implementation. EvoAgent is a method that automatically extends expert agents into multi-agent systems using evolutionary algorithms, LLM-Debate structures multiple agents in adversarial debate, and ChatEval uses a multi-agent referee team to evaluate generated responses.

## [SkeMex: Self-Evolving Skill Memory Boosts Medical Agent Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09365) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SkeMex, a post-deployment self-evolution framework that improves medical agents by distilling interaction trajectories into structured skills stored in a multi-branch memory, without updating model weights. Experiments across diverse clinical tasks show it consistently outperforms representative memory-based agents in both offline and online settings, and generalizes across model backbones. This matters because medical agents increasingly need to reuse prior experience across evolving clinical cases, yet existing memory mechanisms retain redundant, noisy raw traces that are hard to govern. SkeMex's utility-aware retrieval and governance could make long-horizon clinical reasoning more reliable and transferable, benefiting researchers in medical AI and agent systems. SkeMex organizes skills into a multi-branch repository spanning general, task-specific, and action-level experience, and estimates context-dependent utility from environment feedback to guide value-aware retrieval and repository governance. A closed-loop "Read--Write--Assess--Govern" lifecycle writes new skills, updates utilities, promotes useful memories, and removes harmful entries; all data and code will be released publicly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Medical agent systems are increasingly expected to support interactive clinical decision making rather than only static question answering, requiring agents to reuse prior experience across evolving cases. Existing memory mechanisms often retain raw historical traces that are redundant, noisy, and difficult to govern, and they rarely distinguish which memories are truly useful for future reasoning. Skill-level memory is a paradigm that structures reusable, compositional behavioral routines with explicit interfaces, converting raw trajectories into actionable skill modules.

## [TelcoAgent: Scalable, Explainable 5G KPM Forecasting via Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19821) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose TelcoAgent, a foundation model-based framework that forecasts seven 5G Key Performance Measurements (KPMs) per cell across 200 cells without site-specific training. It combines a three-agent pipeline that automatically builds a 3GPP knowledge graph from specification documents, a time-series foundation model (TSFM) for zero-shot forecasting, and a reasoning pipeline that delivers domain-grounded explanations and actionable diagnostics. Existing ML approaches for KPM forecasting struggle with scalability and explainability, which limits their real-world deployment in 5G and next-generation network management. By enabling zero-shot, multi-KPM forecasting with 3GPP-grounded explanations, TelcoAgent could help operators move toward proactive, zero-touch network operations without per-site model training. The framework was evaluated on a three-month, real-world, city-scale 5G KPM dataset from a U.S.-based network operator, covering 200 cells and 7 KPMs per cell. It delivers high forecasting accuracy along with explainable insights and actionable instructions for addressing network degradations, though as a preprint it has not yet undergone peer review or community discussion.

## [HExA: Hierarchical Experimentalist Agents for LLM Self-Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29315) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Hierarchical Experimentalist Agents (HExA), an in-context, experiment-centric self-improvement framework that lets LLM agents iteratively design experiments, distill reusable composable skills, and integrate evidence to answer queries without parameter updates or external supervision. The authors also release Interphyre, a set of hard procedural physics tasks, and report that HExA enables frontier models like GPT-6 Astra to reach near 100% success on the most challenging tasks where they otherwise fail. This work addresses a core limitation of current LLM agents that rely on parametric knowledge acquired through imitation, which struggles in novel domains or complex queries requiring information beyond prior knowledge. By enabling active experimentation and hierarchical skill transfer, HExA points toward agents that can discover general principles of unseen environments and generalize from easier to harder tasks, with potential impact on agent design across reasoning, games, and scientific discovery. HExA is training-free and works with any LLM, including frontier black-box models, requiring no oracle solutions or offline data; it improves token efficiency over naive agentic and memory baselines. The evaluation covers multi-step reasoning, interactive partially observable games, and physics law discovery, with Interphyre providing tool-calling and intervention APIs for hard procedural physics tasks.

**Background**: Most LLM agent systems rely on parametric knowledge acquired through imitation learning, optionally improved by post-training, retrieval, or search over fixed data. This paradigm struggles when tasks require information unavailable from prior knowledge, such as reasoning about a complex physical system where knowing the laws of physics alone is insufficient. In-context self-improvement methods aim to let agents learn from experience without updating model parameters, and active experimentation approaches let agents gather targeted evidence by interacting with an environment.

A new arXiv paper (2607.04528v2) introduces a belief-rollout diagnostic that traces how an LLM agent's anticipated world state and future decisions evolve under alternative execution harnesses, even when the task, environment, and base model are held fixed. The authors also propose Belief-Invariant World-Modeling (BIWM), which exposes evidence hidden or compressed by harness execution and aligns belief trajectories across different harnesses. This work shows that harness design, not just the underlying model, systematically shapes how software agents understand and act on their environments, which is critical for anyone building or evaluating production-grade coding agents. It suggests that terminal success rates alone can mask substantial behavioral differences, offering practical guidance for more reliable agent evaluation and design. The diagnostic separates immediate divergence introduced by the interface from divergence that develops over longer horizons, and experiments across HIBench-Code, SWE-bench Verified, and Terminal-Bench reveal substantial cross-harness belief divergence that is often invisible from terminal success alone and can propagate into different downstream actions.

SWE-bench Verified is a human-filtered subset of 500 instances from the SWE-bench benchmark of real GitHub issues, used to measure how well agents resolve software engineering tasks. This paper asks whether changing only the harness, while keeping the model and task fixed, changes what the agent believes about its environment.

## [Who&When Pro: A New Benchmark for AI Agent Failure Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09996) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Who&When Pro, a large-scale benchmark for automated failure attribution in AI agents, built from 12,326 failed trajectories with golden labels spanning 3 modalities and 26 benchmarks. The dataset was generated using a strictly controlled pipeline that injects a failure only after exactly replaying a successful prefix, and the paper reports systematic attribution patterns across modalities, protocols, and model families. As agents grow more capable, their failures become subtler and harder to trace, so automated attribution is becoming essential for debugging, evaluation, and safe deployment of agentic systems. A large, controlled benchmark with golden labels gives the community a shared yardstick for measuring whether LLMs can actually locate the responsible agent and step, rather than relying on anecdotal case studies. The benchmark's key methodological choice is its controlled pipeline: a successful prefix is replayed exactly before a single failure is injected, which isolates the failure cause and yields reliable golden labels. The paper goes beyond benchmarking by analyzing how attribution performance varies across modalities, protocols, and model families, though it is a benchmark and analysis contribution rather than a new attribution method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Failure attribution is the task of automatically identifying which agent and which step in a multi-agent system first caused a task to fail, essentially tracing a multi-agent cascade back to its root cause. Prior work such as the ICML 2025 spotlight paper "Which Agent Causes Task Failures and When?" (Who&When) established this task, and Who&When Pro extends it to a much larger, more controlled scale. Benchmarks like AgentBench evaluate LLMs as agents, but attribution benchmarks focus specifically on diagnosing failures after they occur.

## [MIRROR Uses Best Modality View as Teacher for VLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21552) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced MIRROR (Modality-Informed Reciprocal Reasoning Optimization), a reinforcement learning method that evaluates a vision-language model under text-only, image-only, and combined views of each problem, selects the best-performing view as a teacher, and trains the remaining views with a GRPO task-reward objective plus a reverse-KL regularizer toward that teacher. They also released ODA-Data, a paired multimodal geometry dataset with text-dominant, image-dominant, and combined views for training and evaluating view-dependent reasoning. Vision-language models often give inconsistent answers to the same problem depending on whether it is presented as text or as an image, and this work shows that such view-dependent strengths can be exploited as a free supervision signal rather than treated as noise. If it generalizes, the approach could improve both accuracy and cross-modality consistency in multimodal reasoning systems without requiring external teacher models or human labels. MIRROR derives its cross-view self-distillation signal from the model's own modality-dependent capabilities, so no separate teacher model is needed; the teacher view is chosen per problem by evaluating the model under all views. The evaluation focuses on multimodal mathematics benchmarks, where MIRROR reportedly improves over standard RL and yields more accurate and consistent behavior across input modality representations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret and generate information from both images and text, extending text-only large language models; examples include GPT-4V, Gemini, Claude 3 Opus, and open-source models such as LLaVA. Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards, such as GRPO, has become a common way to post-train such models on reasoning tasks, while self-distillation lets a model use its own outputs as a training signal. Multimodal reasoning research, including multimodal chain-of-thought work, studies how models combine visual and textual information to solve problems step by step.

## [CASE Uses Gemma 3 KV Cache to Give Tabular Data Semantic Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.03565v2) introduces CASE (Context-Aware Semantic Embeddings), a framework that pre-fills the KV cache of a custom-trained Gemma 3-based Tabular Language Model with a representative sample of rows to create a persistent semantic anchor for a dataset. Experiments on CARTE, TextTab, and TabArena benchmarks show substantial improvements for tabular learners on semantically rich datasets, especially in low-data regimes. Most tabular machine learning models treat text in feature names and cells as meaningless discrete symbols, discarding the semantic knowledge that LLMs already possess. CASE offers a way to inject that LLM-derived semantics into statistical tabular learners, which could improve performance on real-world datasets with rich textual columns and reduce the data needed to train effective models. Unlike prior methods that embed each row in isolation, CASE pre-fills the KV cache with a sample of rows so that generated row embeddings are dynamically contextualized, resolving semantic ambiguities and anchoring representations in domain-specific context. The approach relies on a custom-trained Gemma 3-based Tabular Language Model, and the paper reports gains across three benchmarks, though it remains an arXiv preprint without peer review or community validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular learners such as gradient-boosted trees and neural networks are strong at capturing statistical patterns in structured data but generally ignore the meaning of text in column names or cell values. Large language models like Google DeepMind's Gemma 3, released in March 2025, can encode rich semantic knowledge and support a 128K-token context window. The KV cache is a mechanism in transformer inference that stores previously computed key and value tensors so the model can reuse context without recomputing it, and pre-filling it with dataset rows is how CASE supplies persistent context. Tabular Language Models (TLMs) are transformer variants adapted to process rows, columns, and cells of structured tables.

## [CASCADE: Agentic Framework Predicts Gene Perturbation Effects Validated on TCGA Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05359) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CASCADE is an agentic framework that predicts downstream transcriptional effects of gene perturbation from precomputed ARACNe regulatory networks exposed via MCP, and it validates predictions against real TCGA patient tumor data using directional concordance rather than simple gene membership. For MYC knockdown targets, CASCADE achieved 90.0% concordance in BRCA, 72.0% in COAD, and 85.7% in STAD (all p<=0.0013), replicating at 87.2% in the independent METABRIC cohort. This work shifts validation of perturbation-prediction tools from checking whether predicted genes are known cancer genes to testing whether the predicted direction of change matches reality, which is a more rigorous benchmark. It also demonstrates how LLM-based agents can ground natural-language requests into real MCP tool calls, though accuracy does not exceed existing MSigDB baselines. CASCADE's accuracy is not shown to exceed curated MSigDB gene-set baselines for MYC- or E2F-driven biology, and validation across fifteen additional genes proved gene-specific: proliferation-machinery regulators mostly replicate, while lineage-identity transcription factors and CCND2 consistently fail. In a separate LLM grounding benchmark of 35 queries, a documented local model reached 71.4% exact match (85.7% for a larger model), but both models confidently defaulted to the wrong perturbation type on ambiguous queries, a failure a targeted fix could not resolve.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: ARACNe is an information-theoretic algorithm that reverse-engineers transcriptional regulatory networks from gene expression data by using mutual information and the data processing inequality to filter out indirect interactions. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard that connects AI applications to external data sources and tools, allowing LLM agents to call real functions. TCGA is a landmark cancer genomics program that molecularly characterized over 11,000 primary cancer samples, providing the patient tumor data used here for validation.

<a id="item-191"></a>
## [Fast LapSum: Exact Differentiable Top-k at Million Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06912) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Fast LapSum introduces a scalable solver for the LapSum soft top-k formulation that preserves an exact selection mass of k while supporting end-to-end differentiation. It uses probabilistic bracketing with a binomial order-statistic from kernel-noised samples to restrict sorting to a narrow band, followed by a certification pass that verifies the result at the cost of one additional linear pass, with a full-sort fallback for worst-case scenarios. Exact-budget differentiable top-k is a fundamental operation for inducing sparsity in large-scale models, enabling robust expert activation, token routing, and attention pruning. Fast LapSum makes this practical within million-scale optimization loops, with demonstrated order-of-magnitude speedups in megapixel sparse adversarial examples and reduced Gaussian counts in 3D Gaussian splatting. The certified GPU implementation processes 10^6, 10^7, and 10^8 scores in median times of 0.92 ms, 1.39 ms, and 7.24 ms respectively. The certification pass upgrades probabilistic localization to a verified one, and a full-sort fallback covers worst-case scenarios, ensuring exactness without sacrificing average-case efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Top-k selection is widely used to induce sparsity in neural networks, but the hard top-k operation is non-differentiable, blocking gradient-based training. Existing differentiable alternatives, such as soft top-k based on the LapSum formulation (a sum of Laplace distributions), become increasingly expensive as the number of coordinates grows. Fast LapSum builds on the LapSum soft top-k method, which uses a closed-form inverse of the LapSum function to compute soft weights in O(n log n) time, and adds probabilistic bracketing with certification to scale to millions of scores.

## [SSKG Method Makes LLMs Simulate Students at Realistic Mastery Levels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21668) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.21668v2) demonstrates that three LLMs consistently score 96.8–100% accuracy on SAT questions regardless of the mastery level they are instructed to simulate, and proposes a Stochastic Student Knowledge Graph (SSKG) approach that instead achieves a monotonic accuracy gradient of 44.1–85.2% from low- to high-mastery profiles. Faithful student simulation is critical for generating synthetic training data and stress-testing AI tutoring systems, and this work shows that prompt-level role assignment alone is insufficient, offering a structured alternative that could improve the validity of educational AI evaluation. The approach uses human-in-the-loop workflows where LLMs extract a Curriculum Knowledge Graph (CKG) from course materials, decompose solution paths into CKG triples, and map incorrect distractors; a student's mastery profile becomes an SSKG with mastery probabilities on triples, which is sampled to determine the outcome before an LLM generates a matching rationale, and an ablation shows taxonomy awareness contributes most to fidelity gains, especially for low-mastery profiles.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs represent information as triples of entities and relations, and in education a Curriculum Knowledge Graph encodes which skills or concepts a course requires. LLMs are increasingly used as simulated students to produce synthetic data or test tutoring systems, but prior work shows they tend to answer at their full built-in capability rather than degrading to a requested skill level. SSKG addresses this by making the simulated student's knowledge probabilistic at the level of individual curriculum triples rather than relying on prompt instructions alone.

## [GenCoord: Executable Commitments for Embodied LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22055) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose GenCoord, a decentralized protocol in which the division of work itself becomes the message, using a compact Short DSL to pair a sender's skill path with its partner's continuation. In Minecraft experiments, trained local 0.8B agents matched the task success of free-form communication while cutting median commitment time by 68.2%, and multistep commitments raised held-out-template success from 91.3% to 98.1%. The work suggests a design principle for embodied multi-agent cooperation: make the division of work the message and spend inference on choosing and revising work rather than on dialogue. This could substantially reduce token cost and latency in LLM-driven robot teams, where inference before action is a major bottleneck. A proposal pairs the sender's skill path with the partner's continuation in Short DSL, and compatible paths form commitments that link the sender's delivery to the partner's work after verified receipt; fulfillment is checked separately from original-task success. A deterministic receiver can proceed without another model call when a request is complete, and with the same prompted DeepSeek model, dialogue generated 2.8 times as many output tokens as GenCoord.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Embodied language-model agents are AI systems that perceive and act in environments such as Minecraft while coordinating with other agents, and each agent often holds private information the others cannot see. Traditional dialogue-based coordination has models generate and interpret natural-language messages before the team acts, which costs inference time and tokens. GenCoord instead uses a domain-specific language (DSL) — a compact language specialized for expressing joint work — to encode coordination as executable commitments rather than conversation.

## [New Algorithm Aligns Heart Transplant Policy with Human Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28620) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.28620v2) introduces a two-phase preference elicitation algorithm for linear utilities that learns a utility function directly from allocation outcomes rather than from comparisons of algorithm decisions. Applied to heart transplant allocation, the method achieves a competitive ratio of 0.95 against the hindsight optimum, compared to just 0.66 for the status quo policy, and outperforms LLM-based methods supplied with users' natural language explanations. This work shows that eliciting preferences over outcomes rather than over algorithmic decisions can produce policies significantly better aligned with human values in a high-stakes domain where allocation must balance survival, waitlist mortality, geography, and equity. It also provides evidence that learned linear utility functions can match or beat LLM-based preference modeling, which has implications for AI alignment and healthcare policy design more broadly. The algorithm's first phase uses pairwise comparisons to learn cutting planes that rapidly shrink the space of possible attribute weights and warm-starts the second phase by eliminating dominated regions; the second phase then provably converges to the user's utility function. The authors note that prior decision-level elicitation approaches conflate means with ends, and their user study found no LLM-based method more accurate than the learned linear utilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Preference elicitation is the process of learning what a person or group values, often by asking them to make comparisons, and it is central to aligning AI systems with human values. In organ allocation, policies must weigh competing objectives such as post-transplant survival, waitlist mortality, geographic fairness, and equity, which can be modeled as a utility function over allocation outcomes. Linear utility models assume the overall value of an outcome is a weighted sum of its attributes, making them tractable for optimization but dependent on accurately learning the weights. This paper builds on that framework by learning such weights directly from outcome-level preferences and then using them to optimize heart transplant allocation policies.

A new arXiv paper (2609.01038v2) proposes a simulation framework that predicts A/B test outcomes using LLM-powered agents conditioned on data-driven personas built from anonymized real user behavioral signals such as activity patterns, engagement signals, and inferred demographics. On a benchmark of 40 A/B tests spanning two metric types, the best configuration achieves 0.75-0.80 directional accuracy. A/B testing traditionally requires real user traffic, engineering effort, and weeks of measurement, so a simulation approach that pre-screens experiments could save significant time and cost for product teams. This work also advances LLM-based simulation by showing that grounding agents in real behavioral data produces more faithful population modeling than synthetic or rule-based personas. The paper frames A/B test simulation as a structured question task and systematically studies question design formats, the impact of persona data source and domain alignment, the trade-off between per-persona behavioral depth and population diversity, and efficient population subsampling. Directional accuracy varies by test metric, reaching 0.75-0.80 in the best configuration.

## [New 'Convention Gap' Metric Measures Implicit Communication in Human-AI Cooperation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11489) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces the 'convention gap' as a diagnostic for implicit communication in cooperative AI, formally proving it is zero when partner choices convey only literal content and measuring a +26.2 percentage point gap in public human-human Hanabi games. This work argues that AI agents intended for human partners should be evaluated on human-partnered play using the convention gap, not just AI-AI benchmarks, which could reshape how cooperative AI and AI alignment are assessed. The gap was +16.4 pp for human plays with AI partners and -0.6 pp in simulated AI-AI games among rule-based agents; across five levels of Off-Belief Learning agents, the gap rose monotonically from +1.6 to +21.7 pp, though a nonzero gap indicates partner choices conveyed more without measuring how much.

## [JEV-as-a-Judge Cuts LLM Evaluation Cost via Confidence Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26550) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces JEV-as-a-Judge, a decision-only judge that returns label probabilities rather than generated text, using its confidence to decide whether to accept a verdict or escalate to a reasoning judge. With a pre-frozen threshold, the cascade proved 0.9 points more accurate than GPT-6 on 1,610 held-out pairs at 41% of its fee, and matched GPT-6's accuracy exactly in a pre-specified live test on two new workloads. LLM-as-a-judge has become a standard way to scale model evaluation, but reasoning judges are slow and expensive, so a method that preserves accuracy at a fraction of the cost could make large-scale evaluation far more practical. The paper's finding that confidence reliably marks the boundary between readable and derivable verdicts gives the AI evaluation community a concrete routing signal to build on. JEV comes within three points of GPT-6 wherever a verdict can be read off the text, at 0.36% of its fee and a 0.15-second median latency, but falls behind on math, code, and logic where the verdict must be derived. The authors note that confidence routing weakens on style-adversarial pairs and reference-free prose, and they close with a recipe for validating thresholds locally.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-judge is an evaluation paradigm in which a large language model scores or ranks the outputs of another model, serving as a cheaper and more scalable alternative to human annotation and to word-overlap metrics like BLEU and ROUGE. Confidence-gated cascade routing is a related pattern in which a cheap model answers first, a confidence gate scores that answer, and only low-confidence cases escalate to a premium model. JEV is a decision-only model that reads text and structured state but returns only bounded decisions and label probabilities, rather than chatting or generating long explanations.

A new arXiv paper (2609.35328v2) proposes a dual-agent framework that treats MetaBBO's design loop as a coding task, using a task agent to evolve a target MetaBBO codebase and a hyper agent to modify the task agent and itself. Starting from a naive MetaBBO template, the framework automatically discovers novel variants that outperform state-of-the-art human-made MetaBBO baselines and adapt quickly across different optimization domains. ## [DREAM: Dynamic Resolution Assignment for Multimodal Multi-Agent Debate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05615) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DREAM introduces two components for multimodal multi-agent debate: Dynamic Resolution Assignment, a zero-shot probe round where agents test multiple resolutions and use Average Normalized Log-Likelihood (ANLL) with an adaptive threshold to pick each agent's optimal resolution, and Uncertainty-Guided Rollback Aggregation, which tracks uncertainty across rounds and restores early low-uncertainty answers overridden by group pressure. Across six multimodal datasets, DREAM improves the accuracy-token trade-off over multi-agent debate baselines by 1.5-3.2% accuracy without dataset-specific tuning. This work targets two practical bottlenecks in multimodal multi-agent debate — fixed visual inputs and groupthink — and shows measurable gains without dataset-specific tuning, which could make debate-based reasoning more reliable and efficient for multimodal LLM applications. It also connects uncertainty quantification to debate dynamics, an angle that may influence how future multi-agent systems are designed. DREAM uses Average Normalized Log-Likelihood (ANLL) as its uncertainty measure and an adaptive threshold to assign resolutions per agent, while rollback aggregation restores early low-uncertainty answers that were overridden by confident but hallucinated peer responses. The reported 1.5-3.2% accuracy gain comes with an improved accuracy-token trade-off, though the abstract does not specify which six datasets were used or how many debate rounds were required.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent debate (MAD) is a paradigm in which multiple LLM instances argue over a question and a judge or aggregation step produces a final answer, improving reasoning without additional training. Multimodal LLMs extend this to images, but most frameworks feed every agent the same fixed visual input, even though the optimal image resolution can vary by sample and by agent. Groupthink is a known failure mode in MAD, where agents abandon correct deductions to conform with confident but wrong peers; DREAM addresses both issues using uncertainty signals derived from model likelihoods.

<a id="item-200"></a>
## [MAGI Agent Tested on Nine Real Drug Discovery Campaigns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06411) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers developed MAGI, an open modular agent that authors objectives, launches and monitors molecular optimization, interprets structure-activity relationships, and revises its strategy, then evaluated it across nine retrospective lead-optimization campaigns from three pharmaceutical companies replayed under fixed temporal cutoffs. MAGI generates molecules either directly via the LLM or by delegating to REINVENT 4, and the study found that whether a campaign met its objective depended on the predictive models rather than the generation route. This work suggests that the bottleneck in agentic drug discovery is not tool orchestration but the applicability domain of the scoring models, which could redirect effort in the AI-for-science community toward better predictive models rather than more elaborate agent frameworks. It also provides rare empirical evidence from real pharmaceutical campaigns rather than synthetic benchmarks, making the findings directly relevant to medicinal chemists and cheminformatics practitioners. Both generation routes produced valid structures: LLM proposals stayed closer to local chemistry and reached comparable or higher primary activity in fewer operations, while REINVENT explored broader chemical space. In a blinded evaluation, chemists could not discriminate agentic proposals from held-out compounds, though they judged the SAR reasoning broadly plausible yet incomplete.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Lead optimization is a core stage of drug discovery in which researchers refine promising lead compounds to improve potency, selectivity, safety, and drug-like properties before preclinical development. Structure-activity relationship (SAR) analysis links a molecule's chemical structure to its biological activity, guiding medicinal chemists in modifying compounds. REINVENT 4 is a modern open-source generative AI framework that uses recurrent neural networks and transformers with reinforcement learning to design small molecules. Agentic systems that coordinate such tools are increasingly explored, but it has been unclear which layer of the stack limits real project outcomes.

<a id="item-201"></a>
## [Shared Policies Can Learn Silence in Multi-Agent Safety Reporting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09002) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.09002v2) analyzes a multi-agent game where any witness can stop an unsafe task by reporting, and proves that when k witnesses share a policy and draw actions independently, the reward derivative at universal silence counts silence's benefit k times while the comparison between universal reporting and silence counts it only once, so a fully shared policy can be locally attracted to silence even when reporting is optimal. The authors derive an audit condition that is sufficient for exact policy-gradient updates to reach universal reporting and, outside boundary cases, necessary from near universal silence, and validate it with PPO experiments on 24 witness graphs showing witness groups reduce unsafe task completion by 33.59 percentage points over shuffled groups. This work identifies a subtle gradient pathology that can undermine safety reporting in cooperative multi-agent systems, showing that random audits alone do not guarantee that a silent team learns to report. It matters for AI safety and multi-agent reinforcement learning researchers designing audit mechanisms and policy architectures for safety-critical teams, and it offers concrete design guidance such as separating co-witnesses into distinct networks. The audit condition is sufficient for exact policy-gradient updates to reach universal reporting and, outside boundary cases, necessary from near universal silence; in a balanced family, meeting the condition with prescribed margins costs k times as much for a fully shared policy as for one policy per role. In PPO experiments on 24 witness graphs starting from learned silence, witness groups reduced unsafe completion by 33.59 points (95% bootstrap interval 21.03-45.13), but only 9 of 48 witness-group runs achieved below 1% unsafe completion while retaining at least 90% legitimate completion, and a fully shared network met both thresholds in 0/48 runs with independent action draws versus 48/48 with a common draw.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) studies multiple learning agents that coexist in a shared environment and often share rewards, which can create tension between individual incentives and team outcomes. Policy gradient methods directly optimize a parameterized policy by ascending the gradient of expected return, and in shared-policy settings all agents update the same parameters. Audit mechanisms are review controls that independently check whether AI systems behave as intended, and here they are modeled as random penalties for silence that are meant to make reporting optimal.

<a id="item-202"></a>
## [Reasoning-Token Spikes Reveal Prompted Deception in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10405) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.10405v2) reports that three reasoning-capable large language models, when instructed via system prompts to answer falsely or without regard for truth, generated more reasoning tokens than when instructed to answer truthfully across 210 multiple-choice questions spanning analytic, descriptive, and normative reasoning as well as moral and non-moral domains. In one model, untruthful instructions also increased the likelihood that the model would reason at all. This offers a proof-of-concept for a low-bandwidth, content-independent black-box signal for distinguishing untruthful from truthful model behavior, which is valuable as chain-of-thought traces may become illegible, unfaithful, or inaccessible. It addresses a critical AI safety problem—deception detection—without requiring access to the content of reasoning traces. The study only establishes robust group-level differences in test-time reasoning-token use under explicitly prompted untruthful response policies, and the authors caution it does not yet establish reasoning-token count as a detector of spontaneous deception or general misalignment. Future work should test instance-level detection rates, out-of-distribution generalization, learned deceptive policies, hidden objectives, and robustness under adversarial pressure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) monitoring is a key approach to detecting deception in reasoning AI models, but it depends on reasoning traces being legible, faithful to the underlying computations, and accessible—properties that evidence suggests may soon degrade. Cognitive load theory, originally from psychology, posits that lying requires more mental effort than telling the truth, motivating the hypothesis that untruthful responses would consume more reasoning tokens. Reasoning tokens are the intermediate "thinking" tokens that models like reasoning-capable LLMs generate before producing a final answer.

## [Potential-Based Estimator Cuts Variance in Language Model Expectation Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11399) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.11399v2) introduces a potential-based estimator that exploits next-token conditional probabilities—already produced as a by-product of sampling—to estimate expectations under autoregressive language models more efficiently. The authors construct an estimator whose variance depends on a chosen potential function defined on prefixes, derive conditions under which the potential reduces variance, and demonstrate substantial variance reductions across several estimands at comparable computational cost. Many practical applications of language models—such as evaluating model behavior, reinforcement learning from human feedback, and risk assessment—depend on expectations rather than individual samples, and reliable estimation is often computationally expensive. This method offers a principled, low-overhead way to reduce the number of samples needed, which could make such analyses more affordable and accessible for the ML/NLP community. The approach works by decomposing the test functional additively through real-valued potentials on prefixes, and the estimator's variance depends on the chosen potential; the authors derive conditions for variance reduction and develop practical potentials for several estimands. The method achieves these gains at comparable computational cost to standard Monte Carlo estimation, though the effectiveness depends on how well the potential is tailored to the specific estimand.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive language models generate text one token at a time, using previously generated tokens as context to predict a probability distribution for the next token. Monte Carlo methods estimate expectations by repeatedly drawing random samples and averaging the results, but their accuracy improves only slowly as sample count grows, making variance reduction techniques valuable. This paper connects these two areas by using the model's own next-token probabilities as a control mechanism to reduce estimator variance.

## [TRACE Framework and Benchmark Model Emotion as a Causal Process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11410) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes TRACE, a cognition-oriented framework that formalizes an affective episode into three stages — Condition, Affect, and Effect — and introduces TRACE-Bench, a multimodal benchmark with 3,746 structured QA pairs over 646 real-world social videos spanning five tasks. The authors also present TRACER, a cognition-grounded structured reasoning method that outperforms all evaluated model baselines on each of the five tasks. Current affective computing mostly stops at categorical emotion recognition, so this work pushes multimodal models toward modeling emotion as an unfolding causal process, which could improve social AI, mental-health assessment, and human-machine interaction. The matched human-model comparison reveals a substantial performance gap, showing that even strong general-purpose MLLMs still fail at genuine affective understanding. The benchmark covers grounded affect recognition, regulation decoding, cause reasoning, effect reasoning, and full-chain reconstruction, and it finds that affect-specialized models generally lag behind general-purpose MLLMs. Recurring failure modes include treating displayed behavior as genuine feeling and fabricating unsupported events during long-chain generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Affective computing is the study of systems that can recognize, interpret, process, and simulate human affect, and it has recently moved from categorical emotion labels toward open-ended analysis with large multimodal models. Affective science, however, describes emotion as a dynamic process shaped by appraisal, regulation, and social interpretation, an aspect that remains underexplored computationally. Benchmarks such as EmoBench-M and MMAFFBen have begun testing emotional intelligence in multimodal LLMs, but most still emphasize objective perception tasks rather than the causal chain from cause to consequence.

## [MultiWorldBench Tests Whether Multiplayer World Models Share One Consistent World](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11723) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces MultiWorldBench, a diagnostic Minecraft benchmark with 495 case configurations across seven task suites and ten capabilities, designed to test whether independently controlled views in multiplayer world models stay consistent with one shared, persistent world. Evaluating Solaris, Gamma-World, and MineWorld against Engine GT ground truth, Gamma-World leads the generated systems with a ten-capability average of 21.39, followed by Solaris at 20.88 and MineWorld at 1.89, while Engine GT reaches 91.69. This benchmark exposes a critical blind spot in generation-based world models: plausible individual views do not yet add up to a coherent multiplayer world, with all generated systems scoring at most 8.00 on state persistence and 1.33 on structural consistency. It gives the world-model research community a concrete diagnostic target and could steer progress toward shared-state synchronization and persistence, which matter for games, simulation, and embodied AI. Gamma-World performs better on several control, shared-state, and revisit capabilities, while Solaris leads in cross-view motion and race-condition consistency, yet none of the systems succeeds at spatial reasoning or building-identity preservation. Human preference judgments produce the same overall ranking as the automatic evaluation, with a mean dimension-level Spearman correlation of 0.96, suggesting the automated metrics are reliable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: World models are AI systems that learn a compressed, predictive representation of an environment, allowing them to simulate how a world evolves rather than just recognize patterns in data. In multiplayer settings, each player has an independently controlled viewpoint, so the model must maintain a single shared world state that all views agree on. Minecraft is a popular testbed for such models because it offers a rich, persistent, block-based world with clear ground truth from the game engine.

## [Leverage SHAP Achieves Provably Accurate Shapley Value Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01917) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Leverage SHAP, a lightweight modification of Kernel SHAP that provides provably accurate Shapley value estimates with only O(n log n) model evaluations. The method employs leverage score sampling and achieves approximately 50% error reduction compared to the optimized Kernel SHAP implementation in the SHAP library. This work addresses the lack of non-asymptotic complexity guarantees for Kernel SHAP, a widely used method in explainable AI. By providing the first provably accurate algorithm with O(n log n) evaluations, it could significantly improve the reliability and efficiency of feature attribution in machine learning models. Leverage SHAP leverages a connection between Shapley value estimation and agnostic active learning, using leverage score sampling from randomized numerical linear algebra. The theoretical guarantees hold for general models, and empirical results show a 50% error reduction over the optimized Kernel SHAP in the SHAP library.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Shapley values, originally from cooperative game theory, attribute a model's prediction to individual input features by averaging their marginal contributions over all possible feature subsets. Exact computation requires O(2^n) model evaluations for n features, making approximation necessary. Kernel SHAP is a popular model-agnostic approximation method, but until now lacked strong non-asymptotic complexity guarantees. Leverage score sampling is a technique from randomized numerical linear algebra that selects influential data points to reduce problem size while preserving accuracy.

<a id="item-207"></a>
## [Conditional Vendi Score: Prompt-Aware Diversity Evaluation for Generative AI Models and LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02817) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Conditional-Vendi and Conditional-RKE, new diversity metrics derived from the conditional entropy of positive semidefinite matrices that isolate model-induced diversity in prompt-guided generation. Conditional-RKE achieves an O(1/√n) convergence rate, and a truncated-spectrum approximation makes Conditional-Vendi scalable and consistent. Experiments on text-to-image, image-captioning, and LLM tasks show the conditional scores recover ground-truth diversity orderings and can guide diffusion models toward more diverse samples, with code available at https://github.com/mjalali/conditional-vendi. Existing diversity metrics like Vendi and RKE were designed for unconditional models and cannot distinguish prompt-induced variability from model-induced variability, leaving a critical gap in evaluating prompt-guided generative models. This work provides a principled, theoretically grounded tool for AI/ML researchers and practitioners to measure and improve the true diversity of text-to-image models, image-captioning systems, and LLMs. Conditional-RKE enjoys an O(1/√n) convergence rate, while Conditional-Vendi requires a truncated-spectrum approximation to yield scalable and consistent estimates. The metrics are derived from the conditional entropy of positive semidefinite matrices and are validated across text-to-image, image-captioning, and LLM tasks, including the ability to guide diffusion models toward more diverse samples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: The Vendi Score is a kernel-based diversity metric that quantifies effective diversity by computing the entropy of eigenvalues from a trace-normalized similarity matrix, and RKE is a related metric based on the Rényi entropy of kernel matrices. Both were developed for unconditional generative models, where all variation is model-induced. In prompt-guided generation, however, output variability comes from two sources: the prompt itself and the model, so a metric that conditions on the prompt is needed to isolate the model's contribution to diversity.

A new arXiv paper (2412.06534v5) introduces a modular variant of feature inversion via inverse networks, which reconstructs images from intermediate representations of transformer-based vision models more efficiently and, in some cases, with more semantically coherent results. The authors apply the method to DETR, ViT, Swin Transformer, and DeiT, analyzing reconstructions across network depth and comparing DETR and ViT in particular. This work offers an interpretability tool that could help researchers understand what internal features transformer vision models actually encode, which is important as these architectures become dominant in computer vision. The findings on gradual representational changes and differing robustness to color perturbations could inform model design, debugging, and trust in deployment. The method is a modular variant of inverse-network feature inversion, claimed to be computationally more efficient and sometimes more semantically coherent than prior approaches. The analysis focuses on DETR and ViT as the most informative comparison, while Swin and DeiT results support broader applicability; the paper is a preprint and has not yet been peer-reviewed or validated by community discussion.

Transformer-based vision models such as ViT split images into patches and process them with self-attention, while DETR applies transformers to object detection as a set prediction problem, Swin uses shifted windows for hierarchical local attention, and DeiT is a data-efficient ViT variant. ## [CRM: Outcome-Conditioned Retrieval Model Deployed at Kuaishou](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13844) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Controllable Retrieval Model (CRM), which adds watch time as an input to the user tower so that retrieval can be steered at inference time by supplying a desired watch time, without retraining. CRM is implemented as both a DNN-based two-tower model and a Decision Transformer, and is deployed in Kuaishou's short-video recommendation system serving over 400 million users, with online A/B tests showing watch time improvements of 0.372% in Kuaishou and 0.457% in Kuaishou Lite. This work shows that outcome-conditioned retrieval can be achieved at billion-item scale while preserving efficient approximate nearest neighbor (ANN) search, because the item tower remains independent of the condition and item embeddings can still be precomputed and indexed. It offers a practical way for large-scale recommender systems to steer retrieval toward business objectives such as watch time without retraining, which is valuable for platforms optimizing user engagement. The Transformer variant interleaves item tokens with watch-time-to-go condition tokens, using the target watch time as a numerical prompt for next-item prediction, and for each online request the system chooses between the average and maximum watch times in the user's recent history as the condition. The reported gains are modest (0.372% and 0.457% watch time improvements), but they are validated in a production system at massive scale.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval models in recommendation systems must search large item corpora efficiently, typically using a two-tower architecture where the user tower and item tower produce embeddings that are compared via approximate nearest neighbor (ANN) search. The Decision Transformer reframes reinforcement learning as sequence modeling over (state, action, return-to-go) trajectories, and CRM adapts this idea by treating watch time as a return-to-go-like condition. ANN search is a family of algorithms that retrieve vectors close to a query vector in embedding space without exhaustive exact computation.

## [WoundAmbit benchmarks deep learning models for wound segmentation and telehealth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06185) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WoundAmbit benchmarks state-of-the-art deep learning models for wound segmentation, evaluates real-world deployment aspects, and proposes a reference object-based approach to convert AI-generated masks into clinically relevant wound size estimates. The transformer-based TransNeXt showed the highest generalizability, while VWFormer and ConvNeXtS backbone performed best in expert mask approval. Chronic wounds affect a large population, particularly the elderly and diabetic patients, and automated wound monitoring via mobile image capture can reduce in-person physician visits by enabling remote tracking of wound size. This work addresses the underrepresented area of wound segmentation in medical imaging and demonstrates integration into a custom telehealth system, potentially improving remote wound care. The benchmark standardizes training, data augmentation, and evaluation, conducts cross-validation to minimize partitioning bias, and assesses generalization to an out-of-distribution wound dataset, computational efficiency, and interpretability. All models processed at least one image per second on CPU, and size retrieval accuracy was similar across models, closely matching expert annotations.

## [FedACS: Heterogeneity-Aware Client Sampling for Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11304) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2505.11304v4) presents the first unified theoretical analysis of general heterogeneous federated learning, revealing that communication and computation heterogeneity distort optimization through fundamentally distinct mechanisms. It proposes FedACS, a heterogeneity-aware client sampling method that provably converges to the correct optimum at a rate of O(1/√R) even in dynamic heterogeneous environments. Objective inconsistency means the global model can converge to the wrong stationary point, so understanding and eliminating it is critical for deploying federated learning on real-world devices with diverse hardware and network conditions. FedACS reportedly outperforms state-of-the-art and category-specific baselines by 4.3%–36% while cutting communication costs by 22%–89% and computation loads by 14%–105%, which could make FL substantially more practical. The analysis covers arbitrary choices of local solvers, making it general rather than tied to a specific algorithm, and FedACS is described as a universal method that eliminates all types of objective inconsistency. The convergence guarantee holds even in dynamic heterogeneous environments, though the results are based on an arXiv preprint without peer review or community validation yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning (FL) trains a shared global model across many clients—such as phones or IoT devices—without moving their raw data to a central server. In practice, clients differ in data size, communication bandwidth, and computation speed, and this heterogeneity is known to cause objective inconsistency, where the trained model optimizes a different objective than intended. Client sampling, which selects only a subset of clients per round to save communication, is a key lever for controlling this problem, and prior work such as FedNova and HeteroFL has addressed related aspects of heterogeneity.

Researchers released ReasoningShield, a benchmark of 9.2K annotated (Query, CoT) pairs with stepwise risk analysis, plus lightweight 1B/3B moderation models trained in two stages. The models achieve 91.8% F1, beating LlamaGuard-4 by 35.6% and GPT-4o by 15.8%, while existing moderation tools degrade by up to 35.2% F1 on CoT moderation. This exposes a novel safety vulnerability in large reasoning models where harmful content hides in intermediate reasoning steps even when final answers look benign, a gap existing moderation tools miss. It matters for AI safety researchers and deployers of reasoning models who need reliable content moderation that covers the full reasoning process, not just final outputs. The benchmark spans 10 risk categories, 3 safety levels, and 8 LRMs with diverse reasoning paradigms, with a 7K-sample training set and a 2.2K human-annotated test set. The lightweight models generalize across diverse reasoning paradigms and unseen scenarios, and all resources are publicly released on GitHub and Hugging Face.

Researchers introduced C2TU, a training-free and prompt-free method that uses a Chinese homophone graph and toxic lexicon to unveil cloaked toxic content on social media. It achieves up to 71% improvement in F1 score and 35% in accuracy over the best competitor on two Chinese toxic datasets. Chinese cloaked toxicity has been an unsolved problem for content moderation, as existing methods are mostly designed for English. C2TU first uses substring matching to identify candidate toxic words based on a homophone graph and toxic lexicon, then filters non-toxic candidates and corrects cloaks. It offers two model variants for filtering, based on BERT and LLMs, with the LLM variant addressing auto-regressive limitations by using full semantic context.
