## [Physics-inspired quantum diffusion models generate MNIST images on real IBM hardware](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22193) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes and implements two physics-inspired quantum diffusion model protocols: one using quantum stochastic walks to mix quantum and classical dynamics in the forward process, and another that exploits the intrinsic noise of real IBM quantum hardware with only four qubits to generate images. Both approaches produce MNIST images with lower Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) than fully classical baselines. This work demonstrates that quantum noise, typically viewed as a liability in near-term quantum devices, can be repurposed as a useful resource for generative AI, potentially opening new paths for large-scale quantum generative algorithms. It also provides experimental validation on real quantum hardware, strengthening the case for hybrid quantum-classical generative models. The hardware-based approach uses only four qubits on IBM quantum hardware and deliberately does not mitigate or correct quantum noise, instead treating it as a resource; however, the demonstration is limited to small-scale MNIST images, so scalability to larger, more complex datasets remains unproven.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum Diffusion Models (QDMs) are an emerging paradigm that replaces components of classical diffusion models—such as artificial neural networks—with parameterized quantum circuits, aiming to leverage quantum properties for better generative performance. Quantum stochastic walks generalize classical random walks by interpolating between coherent quantum and incoherent classical dynamics via a GKSL master equation. Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) is a standard metric that compares feature distributions of generated and real images, with lower values indicating better quality. Near-term quantum devices are noisy and limited in qubit count, which has hindered the scalability of earlier QDM proposals.

## [UniConFlow Unifies Constrained Flow-Matching for Certified Motion Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02955) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released UniConFlow (arXiv:2506.02955v3), a unified constrained flow-matching framework that systematically incorporates both equality and inequality constraints into robot trajectory generation. It introduces a novel prescribed-time zeroing function that shapes a time-varying guidance field during inference, plus a violation-segment extraction protocol and a trajectory compression method to handle long-horizon, high-dimensional planning. Most existing generative planners handle constraints such as collision avoidance, actuation limits, and dynamic consistency individually or heuristically, so a unified framework could make certified motion planning more reliable for real robots. Empirical results across a double inverted pendulum, a real-to-sim car racing task, and a sim-to-real manipulation task show it outperforming state-of-the-art generative planners and conventional optimization baselines on safety, kinodynamic consistency, and action feasibility. The prescribed-time zeroing function lets the generation process adapt to varying system models and task requirements, while the violation-segment extraction protocol localizes and refines only the constraint-violating portions of trajectories. The trajectory compression method accelerates optimization in a reduced-dimensional space while preserving high-fidelity reconstruction after decoding, and a project page is available at https://uniconflow.github.io.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a generative modeling technique that trains continuous normalizing flows by regressing a neural vector field toward a closed-form target, enabling flexible and multimodal trajectory generation. Constrained motion planning seeks paths that satisfy constraints such as collision avoidance while reaching a goal, and prescribed-time control aims to achieve stability within a finite settling time chosen by the user in advance. UniConFlow combines these ideas to generate certified trajectories that respect both equality and inequality constraints.

A new arXiv paper (2506.11849v3) introduces a hybrid Monte Carlo estimator that combines sampling with flexible regression models, such as XGBoost, to estimate Shapley and other probabilistic values. The method achieves state-of-the-art accuracy, with error up to 11× lower than Permutation SHAP, 6.5× lower than Kernel SHAP, and 3.4× lower than Leverage SHAP on eight datasets. This advance significantly improves the accuracy of feature attribution in explainable AI, benefiting researchers and practitioners who rely on Shapley values for model interpretation. The flexibility to use any efficiently computable function family, like tree-based models, could lead to broader adoption and more reliable explanations. The approach allows replacing linear regression with any function family whose probabilistic values can be computed efficiently, and with cross-fitting it produces unbiased estimates. For general probabilistic values, the error can be 390× lower than the best prior estimator.

A new arXiv paper (2509.00303v5) introduces the LLM ORDER BY semantic operator as a logical abstraction and systematically studies its physical implementations, including improvements to existing semantic sorting algorithms and a new semantic-aware external merge sort algorithm. The authors also design a budget-aware optimizer that uses heuristic rules, LLM-as-Judge evaluation, and consensus aggregation to dynamically select near-optimal access paths, achieving ranking accuracy on par with or superior to the best static methods across all benchmarks. This work addresses a practical gap in integrating large language models with database systems, where semantic, context-aware ranking is increasingly needed for LLM-powered analytics. By showing that no single implementation is universally optimal and providing an optimizer that adapts to budget constraints, it lays foundational groundwork for building robust, large-scale LLM-driven data processing systems. The evaluation reveals a general scaling relationship between sorting cost and ordering quality for comparison-based algorithms, and the optimizer combines heuristic rules, LLM-as-Judge evaluation, and consensus aggregation to pick access paths dynamically. The approach is evaluated across multiple benchmarks, consistently matching or exceeding the best static methods in ranking accuracy.

## [SPS-GAN Learns Hamiltonian Phase Space from Trajectories and Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23003) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SPS-GAN, a conditional generative adversarial model that combines a port-Hamiltonian backbone with adversarial conditional generation to learn structured dynamics from both Cartesian trajectories and video. Using a cyclic-coordinate loss, it infers latent mechanical structure—including system degrees of freedom and canonical coordinates—without access to the true phase space, and improves trajectory accuracy over supervised dynamics baselines and video consistency over generative baselines. Mechanics-inspired neural models typically require a known configuration space and fixed physical parameters, limiting their use on raw real-world observations. SPS-GAN's ability to recover latent mechanical coordinates across modalities and dynamical regimes could make physics-informed machine learning more applicable to video data and systems where the underlying state is unknown. The architecture represents conservative, dissipative, and forced systems within a single port-Hamiltonian framework, and the cyclic-coordinate loss recovers both degrees of freedom and canonical coordinates without supervision. Experiments on simulated and real-world systems show consistent recovery of correct latent mechanical coordinates across observation modalities and dynamical regimes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Hamiltonian mechanics reformulates classical mechanics using generalized momenta, and its phase space—the space of positions and momenta—is central to statistical mechanics and dynamical systems theory. Port-Hamiltonian systems extend this geometric framework to model multi-physics and networked systems with energy ports, making them useful for learning physically consistent dynamics. Generative adversarial networks (GANs) are models that learn data distributions by pitting a generator against a discriminator, and conditional GANs allow generation to be guided by inputs such as trajectories or video.

## [HAFixAgent Adds Repository History to Agentic Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01047) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HAFixAgent injects blame-derived repository history into an agentic program repair loop, evaluated on 854 Defects4J (Java) and 501 BugsInPy (Python) bugs. The best history heuristic improves over non-history baselines by up to +64.3% with weaker models, and HAFixAgent outperforms RepairAgent by +56.6% and BIRCH-feedback by +47.1% on the strongest model. This work shows that repository history is widely available and can substantially boost automated program repair, especially for weaker models, while remaining cost-competitive in agent steps and tokens. It suggests that history-aware context is a practical, complementary signal for LLM-based debugging agents, with implications for how APR systems are designed and benchmarked. The three history heuristics are complementary, each repairing a different subset of bugs, and their hybrid significantly repairs more bugs than non-history (McNemar's test, p < 0.001), an advantage that persists under realistic spectrum-based fault localization. Individual heuristics are generally no more expensive than non-history in agent steps or tokens, while the hybrid costs roughly twice a single run in a cascade setting.

## [COBALT: Offline Trajectories Boost Multi-Turn Code Generation Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03806) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers from OSU-NLP-Group introduce COBALT, a contextual bandit learning method that reuses offline multi-turn trajectories to train LLMs for multi-turn code generation, avoiding costly online RL. On LiveCodeBench, COBALT improves R1-Distill 8B and Qwen3 8B by up to 9.0 and 6.2 absolute Pass@1 points, matching GRPO and VeRPO baselines. This work addresses the high cost and instability of online RL for code generation, offering a more efficient training paradigm that could lower the barrier for researchers and practitioners. The substantial Pass@1 gains on a recognized benchmark suggest COBALT could become a practical alternative to online RL for multi-turn code tasks. COBALT leverages the insight that multi-turn code generation is a one-step recoverable Markov decision process: it collects trajectories from a reference LLM, splits them into partial trajectories, and trains the model to complete each partial trajectory via single-step generation. A perturbation-based analysis also shows COBALT improves robustness to misleading execution feedback, mitigating in-context reward hacking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Online reinforcement learning (RL) for LLMs typically requires repeatedly generating rollouts and receiving feedback, which is computationally expensive and can be unstable. Offline RL instead learns from a fixed dataset of previously collected trajectories, and contextual bandits are a simplified RL setting where an agent chooses actions based on context to maximize reward. COBALT combines these ideas by reusing offline multi-turn trajectories in a bandit framework, treating each partial trajectory as a context for a single-step generation action.

## [Offline RL Theory Extended to Parametric Policies Beyond State-Wise Mirror Descent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23811) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.23811v5) extends theoretical guarantees for offline reinforcement learning from finite, small action spaces to parameterized policy classes over large or continuous action spaces. The authors identify contextual coupling as the core difficulty when extending mirror descent to parameterized policies, and show that connecting mirror descent to natural policy gradient yields novel analyses, guarantees, and algorithmic insights, including a surprising unification between offline RL and imitation learning. Prior computationally tractable offline RL algorithms such as PSPI only worked for finite and small action spaces and required actors to be implicitly induced from critic functions, which does not match how policies are parameterized in practice. This work bridges that gap by providing theoretical guarantees for standalone parameterized policies over large or continuous action spaces, which is important for researchers aiming to deploy offline RL in realistic continuous-control settings. The paper builds on the pessimism-based theoretical foundations of offline RL (e.g., Xie et al., 2021) and focuses on oracle-efficient algorithms, identifying contextual coupling as the key obstacle when moving from state-wise mirror descent to parameterized policies. It leverages a connection to natural policy gradient to derive new analyses and guarantees, and notably establishes a unification between offline RL and imitation learning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning aims to learn a good policy from a fixed dataset without further environment interaction, typically using pessimism to avoid overestimating out-of-distribution actions. Mirror descent is a classical optimization method that has been adapted to RL as state-wise mirror descent, but this formulation implicitly derives the actor from the critic and struggles with large or continuous action spaces. PSPI (pessimistic soft policy iteration) is an example of a computationally tractable offline RL algorithm limited to finite action spaces, and function approximation is needed to handle realistic, high-dimensional problems.

## [GD in Two-Neuron ReLU Networks Converges at Θ(1/ln(t))](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02095) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.02095v2) proves that gradient descent in a minimal binary classification setting — a two-neuron ReLU network trained on just two instances — converges to an optimal robustness margin at a prohibitively slow rate of Θ(1/ln(t)). This is claimed to be the first explicit lower bound on the convergence rate of the robustness margin in a non-linear model, and empirical simulations show the same tight rate across multiple natural network initializations. This result suggests that even in the simplest non-linear architecture, gradient descent may inherently struggle to maximize adversarial robustness margins, implying that slow robustness convergence is not merely an artifact of large models or poor tuning. It has direct implications for the ML theory community studying optimization dynamics and for practitioners who rely on training to improve adversarial robustness. The proof derives tight control over the GD trajectory by analyzing the distinct activation patterns of the two-neuron ReLU model, bounding the decision boundary's movement despite the non-linearity. The Θ(1/ln(t)) rate is a lower bound, meaning no faster convergence is possible in this setting, and the authors verify it empirically across multiple initializations.

**Background**: ReLU (Rectified Linear Unit) is a widely used activation function that outputs the input directly if positive and zero otherwise, making networks non-linear. Adversarial robustness margin refers to the distance between the decision boundary and training points — a larger margin generally means better resistance to adversarial perturbations. Gradient descent is the standard optimization algorithm for training neural networks, and its convergence rate describes how quickly it approaches an optimal solution; prior explicit lower bounds were mostly known for linear or convex settings, not non-linear neural networks.

## [Agentic P2P Networks: Sharing AI Capabilities, Not Just Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03753) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An arXiv paper (arXiv:2603.03753v2) proposes a plane-based reference architecture for Agentic Peer-to-Peer (P2P) Networks, in which Client-Side Autonomous Agents (CSAAs) on edge devices directly delegate subtasks and share dynamic capabilities. It introduces signed, soft-state capability descriptors for intent-aware routing and a three-tier verification spectrum (reputation signals, canary probes, cryptographic evidence), with proxy-level discrete-event simulation showing improved workflow success under poisoning and drift at modest control-plane overhead. As AI inference migrates from centralized cloud APIs to local edge agents, direct agent-to-agent collaboration could form a new decentralized layer for sharing capabilities and actions rather than static files. This reframes classic P2P assumptions—where exchanged objects were static and verifiable and failures only wasted bandwidth—into a setting where unsafe capabilities can cause privacy or safety harm, making the proposed trust and discovery mechanisms relevant to future multi-agent and edge-AI systems. The architecture decouples connectivity, semantic discovery, and execution into separate planes, and the tiered verification spectrum aligns trust costs with task risks, from cheap reputation signals (Tier 1) to canary probes (Tier 2) and cryptographic evidence (Tier 3). The paper is a position/outline work: its evidence comes from a proxy-level discrete-event simulation rather than a full deployment, so empirical validation in real networks remains future work.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Classic P2P systems such as BitTorrent distribute static, hash-indexed content across decentralized overlays, where the exchanged object is easy to verify and failures mainly waste bandwidth. Client-Side Autonomous Agents (CSAAs) are persistent personal agents that run local inference on edge devices, plan, access local context, and invoke tools on behalf of users. When these agents delegate subtasks directly to one another, they form Agentic P2P Networks that exchange dynamic, heterogeneous, and potentially unsafe capabilities, requiring semantic discovery, drift management, and risk-aware execution. A reference architecture in software engineering provides a template solution and common vocabulary for a domain, which is what the paper's plane-based design aims to offer.

<a id="item-225"></a>
## [CORE-Seg: Reasoning-Driven Segmentation for Complex Lesions via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05911) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced ComLesion-14K, described as the first diverse Chain-of-Thought benchmark for reasoning-driven complex lesion segmentation, alongside CORE-Seg, an end-to-end framework that integrates reasoning with segmentation via a Semantic-Guided Prompt Adapter. CORE-Seg uses a progressive training strategy from SFT to GRPO with an adaptive dual-granularity reward mechanism, achieving a mean Dice of 37.06% (14.89% higher than the second-best baseline) and reducing the failure rate to 18.42%. This work addresses a clear gap in medical image analysis: general MLLMs lack specialized visual reasoning for complex lesions, while traditional segmentation models lack logical interpretability. By combining MLLMs with reinforcement learning and releasing a dedicated CoT benchmark, it could inspire further research into interpretable, reasoning-based medical segmentation systems. The framework relies on a Semantic-Guided Prompt Adapter and an adaptive dual-granularity reward mechanism designed to mitigate reward sparsity during GRPO training. The reported mean Dice of 37.06% and failure rate of 18.42% suggest the task remains challenging, and the abstract is truncated, so full architectural and evaluation details are not yet available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Medical image segmentation traditionally relies on visual pattern matching to delineate lesions pixel by pixel, but such models cannot explain their reasoning. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) can process and reason across text and images, yet general-purpose MLLMs lack the specialized visual reasoning needed for complex lesions. Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting encourages models to generate intermediate reasoning steps, and GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm that improves reasoning by normalizing rewards within candidate groups without a value critic.

<a id="item-226"></a>
## [Mixture-Greedy Beats UCB for Diversity-Aware Generative Model Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21716) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.21716v3) shows that a simple Mixture-Greedy strategy without any UCB exploration bonus converges faster and achieves better performance than UCB-based methods for online selection among generative models under diversity-aware metrics such as FID and Vendi. The authors provide theoretical insight showing that diversity-aware objectives induce implicit exploration by favoring interior mixtures, yielding sublinear regret guarantees. This finding challenges the long-standing assumption that explicit optimism (UCB) is necessary for effective exploration in multi-armed bandits, and it has practical implications for AI practitioners who need to efficiently select among multiple costly generative models. It suggests that for diversity-aware objectives, simpler algorithms can be more sample-efficient, potentially reducing computational cost in generative AI pipelines. The paper evaluates across multiple datasets and metrics, observing that the UCB term consistently slows convergence and reduces sample efficiency, especially for metrics like FID and Vendi where tight confidence bounds are difficult to construct. The theoretical analysis identifies structural conditions under which diversity-aware objectives favor interior mixtures, leading to sampling of all arms and sublinear regret.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: The multi-armed bandit (MAB) problem models sequential decision-making under uncertainty, where an agent must balance exploration of unknown options and exploitation of known good ones. The Upper Confidence Bound (UCB) algorithm is a classic solution that adds an optimism bonus to encourage exploration. In generative model selection, diversity-aware metrics like FID (Fréchet Inception Distance) measure both quality and diversity of generated samples, and a mixture of models can outperform any single model.

## [AnomalyAgent: Tool-Augmented RL Agent for Industrial Anomaly Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07900) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AnomalyAgent is a new agentic system that synthesizes realistic industrial anomalies using five tools (Prompt Generation, Image Generation, Quality Evaluation, Knowledge Retrieval, Mask Generation) and a two-stage training framework of supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning. On MVTec-AD, it achieves IS/IC-L of 2.10/0.33, 57.0% classification accuracy with ResNet34, and 99.3%/74.2% image/pixel-level AP with a simple UNet, surpassing all zero-shot state-of-the-art methods. This work addresses the critical data scarcity problem in industrial anomaly detection by generating high-quality synthetic anomalies, potentially reducing the need for costly and rare real defect samples. The agentic, tool-augmented RL approach could inspire broader applications in data synthesis and industrial AI, impacting manufacturing quality control and automated inspection. The system uses a three-part reward mechanism: task rewards for quality and location rationality, reflection rewards for improving prompts, and behavioral rewards for trajectory adherence. It is trained on structured trajectories from real anomaly images, and the code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Industrial anomaly detection aims to identify defects in manufactured products, but collecting sufficient real anomaly samples is often impractical. Anomaly synthesis generates artificial defect images to augment training data, yet most existing methods rely on single-step generation without complex reasoning. AnomalyAgent introduces an agentic approach with self-reflection and tool augmentation, trained via a two-stage process of supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning, a paradigm increasingly used to enhance tool-use capabilities in large language models.

## [IAD-Unify: Unified Interfaces for Industrial Anomaly Understanding, Segmentation, and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12440) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IAD-Unify introduces a unified architecture that connects a multimodal language model (Qwen3.5), a dense visual expert (DINOv2), and a diffusion editor (Stable Diffusion) through task-specific token interfaces. It compresses a dense anomaly field of 1,369 cells into 81 structured evidence tokens for grounded answers and segmentation, while a separate 256-query interface resamples Qwen states into Stable Diffusion's cross-attention context for localized editing. This work addresses a practical industrial inspection need by providing one fixed shared parameter set for understanding, segmentation, and localized generation without conflating their inputs, supervision, or outputs. It achieves 73.02% MMAD Macro_7, the highest listed public segmentation AP average (57.10%) with one reference, and the lowest Controlled masked DINO distance (0.3765), which could benefit researchers and practitioners in anomaly detection and multimodal AI. The architecture uses a multi-reference DINOv2 pathway to form a dense anomaly field and compresses its 1,369 cells into 81 structured evidence tokens, with 32 task tokens converting them into a semantic residual over the dense mask. Evidence tokens are excluded from the generation pathway, and staged optimization initializes dense evidence, aligns it with language, calibrates segmentation, then pretrains and specializes the diffusion editor while preserving earlier capabilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Industrial anomaly inspection requires complementary capabilities: explaining an observed defect, localizing its pixels, and synthesizing a controlled edit. DINOv2 is a self-supervised vision transformer that produces universal features for both image-level and pixel-level tasks, while Qwen3.5 is a native vision-language model and Stable Diffusion is a latent diffusion model that generates images via cross-attention conditioning. IAD-Unify combines these components through task-specific token interfaces to handle understanding, segmentation, and generation in one framework.

## [LensVLM Compresses Text as Images with Selective Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07019) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose LensVLM, an inference framework and post-training recipe built on Qwen3.5-9B-Base that lets vision language models scan compressed rendered text images and selectively expand only relevant regions to their uncompressed form via learned tools. It maintains accuracy comparable to the full-text upper bound at 4.3x effective compression and outperforms retrieval-based, text- and visual-compression baselines up to 10.1x effective compression across seven text QA benchmarks. Tokenizing long text into sequences is a major cost driver for LLM and VLM inference, and this work shows that rendering text as images with selective expansion can cut effective sequence length by roughly 4x without sacrificing accuracy. If it generalizes, it could offer a practical alternative to token-level compression and retrieval for long-document and multimodal workloads. Because VLM image encoders map fixed-size images to a fixed number of visual tokens, varying rendering resolution acts as a fine-grained compression knob, but accuracy degrades as characters shrink below the encoder's effective resolution. LensVLM's analysis shows training makes visual compression robust to rendering choices, and as compression grows the model relies more on expanded content than on unreliable visual reading; text expansion suits rendered text while high-resolution image expansion suits native documents whose layout cues matter.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision language models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret images and text, extending text-only large language models to multimodal inputs such as GPT-4V, Gemini and Claude. Normally, text fed to a model must be tokenized into long sequences, which grows compute and memory costs; an alternative is to render text as an image so the vision encoder compresses it into a fixed number of visual tokens. Prior visual token compression methods reduce visual tokens for efficiency, but aggressive compression makes small characters unreadable, which LensVLM addresses by expanding only the regions that matter.

<a id="item-230"></a>
## [GraphDPO Generalizes DPO to Preference Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08037) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Graph Direct Preference Optimization (GraphDPO), a structured generalization of Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) that operates over directed acyclic preference graphs induced by multiple rollouts per prompt. GraphDPO encodes dominance relations as edges and optimizes a graph-structured Plackett-Luce-inspired objective, recovering standard DPO as a special case while handling tied and incomparable responses through an equivalence-class layered construction. This work addresses a real limitation in multi-rollout alignment settings where collapsing rich preference data into independent pairs discards transitivity and introduces redundant or conflicting supervision, potentially causing unstable optimization. By providing a scalable and robust alternative to pairwise and listwise alignment objectives, GraphDPO could influence future LLM alignment research and improve training stability in practical deployments. GraphDPO maintains linear per-prompt complexity via efficient log-sum-exp aggregation under the layered construction, and it optionally incorporates ground-truth anchoring by inserting verified solutions as dominant nodes with an annealed schedule that stabilizes early training while gradually relaxing oracle supervision. Experiments on reasoning and program synthesis tasks demonstrate superior performance compared to pairwise and listwise baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a method for aligning language models with human preferences that directly optimizes the policy from pairwise comparisons, avoiding the need for a separate reward model or reinforcement learning loop. The Plackett-Luce model is a probabilistic model for ranking data that generalizes the Bradley-Terry paired comparison model to full rankings, assigning a worth parameter to each item. A directed acyclic graph (DAG) is a directed graph with no cycles, often used to represent dominance or ordering relations among items. GraphDPO combines these ideas by modeling preferences as a DAG and optimizing a Plackett-Luce-inspired objective over it.

<a id="item-231"></a>
## [Sampling-Based Safe RL Enforces Safety Across Sampled Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19469) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Sampling-Based Safe Reinforcement Learning (SBSRL), a model-based RL algorithm that enforces safety constraints jointly across a finite set of sampled dynamics, approximating an intractable worst-case optimization over uncertain dynamics. The paper derives high-probability safety guarantees throughout learning and a finite-time sample complexity bound for near-optimal policy recovery, validated in simulation and on real robotic hardware. Safe exploration is a fundamental barrier to deploying RL in the real world, and SBSRL offers a practical path to safety guarantees in continuous domains without explicit exploration bonuses. This could accelerate the adoption of RL in robotics and other safety-critical applications where constraint violations are unacceptable. The algorithm constrains epistemic uncertainty to drive exploration, eliminating the need for explicit exploration bonuses, and extends to deep-ensemble implementations that scale to high-dimensional continuous control. The theoretical guarantees rely on regularity conditions, and the approach approximates rather than exactly solves the worst-case optimization over uncertain dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning trains agents to maximize rewards through trial and error, but exploration can lead to unsafe actions, especially in real-world settings. Model-based RL learns a model of the environment's dynamics to plan actions, while safe RL incorporates constraints to prevent harmful behavior. Epistemic uncertainty reflects the agent's lack of knowledge about the environment and is often used to guide exploration.

## [SMFP: One-Step Generative Policies for Entropy-Regularized RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21282) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Stochastic MeanFlow Policies (SMFP), a one-step generative policy class that combines MeanFlow-based noise-to-action mappings with Gaussian reparameterization to enable entropy-regularized mirror-descent reinforcement learning. Across seven MuJoCo benchmarks, SMFP outperforms Gaussian and generative baselines using only single-step inference, and also demonstrates multimodal action modeling and effective exploration in sparse-reward navigation. This work bridges two active research threads—one-step generative modeling and entropy-regularized policy optimization—showing that expressive multimodal policies can be trained with tractable entropy control and cheap single-step inference. If it generalizes beyond MuJoCo, it could make generative policies practical for real-time control and other domains where iterative sampling is too slow. SMFP uses a learnable conditional Gaussian scale to provide an analytic entropy lower bound, which regulates stochasticity alongside direct value guidance and advantage-weighted MeanFlow regression. The approach relies on MeanFlow's average-velocity formulation, which enables one-step generation without iterative sampling, though evaluation is currently limited to MuJoCo benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: MeanFlow is a recent one-step generative modeling framework that replaces the instantaneous velocity used in Flow Matching with an interval-averaged (mean) velocity, allowing a model to map noise to data in a single step. Mirror descent is a first-order optimization method widely used to analyze and design trust-region reinforcement learning algorithms, and entropy regularization adds an entropy term to the RL objective to encourage exploration and stochasticity. Gaussian policies are standard in continuous control but struggle to represent multimodal action distributions, while generative policies can capture such multimodality but often require iterative sampling or lack tractable entropy estimates.

## [VITAL: Dual-Supervised Latent Reasoning for Medical MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28422) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VITAL introduces a visual-semantic dual supervision framework for latent reasoning in medical multimodal LLMs, where an auxiliary text decoder reconstructs reasoning chains from latent states and a visual projector regresses ROI features from a frozen medical vision encoder. Both modules are discarded at inference for zero overhead but can be re-attached post-hoc for textual and visual interpretability, and the authors built a 61K dataset spanning 9 imaging modalities that is an order of magnitude larger than prior medical visual latent reasoning datasets. This work addresses modality collapse, insufficient visual supervision, and the lack of interpretability that plague latent reasoning in clinical settings, where opaque reasoning is unacceptable. By achieving state-of-the-art results on 7 benchmarks competitive with trillion-parameter proprietary models while adding no inference cost, VITAL could make explainable latent reasoning practical for real-world medical VQA systems. The dual supervision modules are only used during training and are removed at inference, so the method adds zero inference overhead while still allowing post-hoc attachment for interpretability. The 61K dataset spans 9 imaging modalities, and experiments show VITAL consistently outperforms its backbone, all latent reasoning baselines, and medical MLLMs trained on far larger data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Latent reasoning lets a model reason over continuous hidden states instead of explicit tokens, avoiding the language bottleneck and inference overhead of chain-of-thought prompting, but existing methods suffer from modality collapse (where one modality dominates and visual information is diluted) and opaque latent states. Medical MLLMs combine vision encoders with large language models to answer questions about medical images, and region-of-interest (ROI) features are fine-grained visual representations of clinically relevant areas that are important for diagnosis. VITAL builds on this line of work by adding auxiliary supervision that keeps latent reasoning aligned with both text and visual evidence.

<a id="item-234"></a>
## [Teacher Update Schedules and Stability in Self On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03532) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.03532v2) systematically studies teacher update schedules in self on-policy distillation, showing through a controlled schedule sweep on Qwen3-8B that isolation periods — complete teacher freezing between updates — are the key structural property enabling stable learning, rather than teacher age. The authors introduce a diagnostic framework of temporal KL structure, refresh shock, and length-tail risk, uncover a failure mode called state-oblivious collapse, and propose Consolidation-Gated Teacher Refresh (CGTR), which achieves zero collapse and the best final score on Chemistry, Biology, Physics, and ToolUse tasks. Self on-policy distillation is an increasingly popular way to train LLMs without external supervision, so understanding which teacher update schedules remain stable has direct practical value for LLM training pipelines. The identification of state-oblivious collapse and the CGTR remedy could help researchers avoid catastrophic training failures that are invisible under short-horizon evaluation, though the impact is currently confined to a specialized subfield. The diagnostic framework combines temporal KL structure, refresh shock, and length-tail risk to characterize training dynamics, and it reveals that optimal short-horizon fixed schedules can catastrophically fail in long-horizon training because a clock-driven refresh can copy a transiently drifting student into the teacher in one irreversible step. CGTR preserves isolation periods while gating each refresh on joint evidence of reward improvement and length-tail safety, using a single shared parameter set with no per-dataset retuning and self-regulating its refresh frequency to each task's learning dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Self on-policy distillation trains a student policy against a teacher derived from its own parameter history, meaning the teacher is periodically refreshed from the student rather than supplied by a larger external model. The teacher's update schedule governs the temporal coupling between teacher and student, but this schedule had not been systematically studied as a stability variable. Related work on on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) similarly uses the model itself, given privileged information, instead of calling on a larger model.

## [ADE-FEMU learns constitutive neural networks from deformation data via differentiable FEM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05199) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ADE-FEMU, an end-to-end differentiable framework that embeds a constitutive neural network inside a GPU-accelerated JAX-FEM nonlinear solver and trains its parameters by gradient-based minimization of a measurement-mismatch loss. It is demonstrated on two architectures — a grey-box Constitutive Artificial Neural Network (CANN) and a white-box, interpretable CANN — and evaluated on three open experimental datasets, including full DIC of a perforated tensile specimen, a reduced-data one-dimensional stretch scenario, and a heterogeneous matrix-inclusion system generalized to twenty-two unseen samples. This work extends finite element model updating (FEMU) to hyperelastic materials by making the entire simulation pipeline differentiable, removing the need for analytic adjoints or offline surrogate models. It could improve how material models are calibrated from full-field deformation data in computational mechanics and scientific machine learning, benefiting researchers who rely on heterogeneous experimental measurements rather than homogeneous stress-strain tests. Newton tangent stiffness and loss gradients are computed automatically using forward- and reverse-mode automatic differentiation throughout the entire pipeline, and the framework focuses on incompressible isotropic hyperelasticity. The grey-box CANN is a polyconvex, fully connected model with high flexibility, while the white-box CANN is an expert-system network with phenomenologically interpretable strain-energy terms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Finite element model updating (FEMU) is an inverse parameter identification technique that iteratively refines parameters of a finite element model, such as material properties, to minimize discrepancies with measured data. Constitutive artificial neural networks (CANNs) are a machine learning architecture for data-driven modeling of material behavior that can inherently satisfy kinematical, thermodynamical, and physical constraints. JAX-FEM is an open-source differentiable finite element library built on Google JAX that supports GPU-accelerated 2D and 3D simulations and automatic differentiation.

## [LLMs Peer-Review Each Other to Boost Medical QA Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15419) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.15419v2) proposes a multi-agent peer-reviewed reasoning method where multiple LLM agents independently generate chain-of-thought reasoning and then act as peer reviewers to evaluate each other's reasoning for factual correctness and logical soundness. Tested with five models (Llama-3.1-8B, Qwen2.5-7B, Phi-4, DeepSeek-LLM-7B, GPT-oss-20B) on HeadQA, MedQA-USMLE, and PubMedQA, the best combination reached 0.820 average accuracy, beating the strongest single model (0.777) and majority voting ensembles (up to 0.789). This work shows that letting LLMs act as both solvers and evaluators can improve accuracy, interpretability, and robustness in medical question answering, offering a promising direction for trustworthy biomedical AI systems. It also suggests a scalable alternative to simple majority voting, since performance improved as more models participated. The method selects the highest-rated reasoning chain to produce the final answer, and peer assessments reliably distinguished high- from low-quality reasoning chains. Experiments compared against single-model chain-of-thought reasoning and chain-of-thought-based majority voting, with the method scaling effectively as more models participated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting encourages LLMs to produce intermediate reasoning steps, which improves performance on arithmetic, commonsense, and symbolic reasoning tasks. MedQA-USMLE is a benchmark of clinical vignettes and multi-hop reasoning derived from official medical licensure exams, while HeadQA and PubMedQA are other medical QA datasets. Multi-agent peer review, inspired by academic peer review and LLM-as-a-judge, uses multiple autonomous LLM agents to critique and revise each other's solutions.

## [PACT Uses Privileged Expert Traces to Co-Train Multi-Turn Tool-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16215) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose PACT, a Privileged trAce Co-Training framework that uses expert traces only as training-time optimization signals rather than rollout-time hints, keeping rollout generation prompt-only. PACT combines a trace-conditioned RL surrogate and a component-aware SFT loss with prompt-only anchoring, and experiments on FTRL, BFCL, and ToolHop show it consistently outperforms strong SFT- and RL-based baselines. Multi-turn tool-use agents are central to building capable LLM-based assistants, but post-training them is hard because RL suffers from sparse rewards and weak credit assignment while SFT over-constrains models to fixed trajectories. PACT offers a way to get dense process supervision without sacrificing the prompt-only inference setting, which could improve how agentic LLMs are trained across the industry. PACT keeps rollout generation prompt-only and uses expert traces to guide optimization through two complementary signals: a trace-conditioned RL surrogate that evaluates prompt-only rollouts under expert-trace context, and a component-aware SFT loss that provides annealed process supervision over reasoning and tool-call components. To reduce over-reliance on the training-only trace context, it incorporates prompt-only anchoring with standard prompt-only RL updates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-turn tool-use agents are LLM-based systems that alternately reason, call external tools, and adapt to observations across several interaction turns. Post-training such agents typically relies on either reinforcement learning, which matches the prompt-only inference setting but suffers from sparse rewards and weak credit assignment over long horizons, or supervised fine-tuning on expert traces, which provides dense process supervision but can over-constrain the model to fixed trajectories. PACT aims to combine the strengths of both by treating expert traces as privileged information available only during training.

## [CircuitLasso: Scalable Sparse Regression for LLM Circuit Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16939) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced CircuitLasso, a circuit learning method based on sparse linear regression that recovers circuits over sparse autoencoder (SAE) features in large language models with structural accuracy matching state-of-the-art intervention-based methods at a fraction of the computational cost. The work also demonstrates that the learned circuits can guide a domain-generalization task to comparable performance at substantially lower cost. Circuit learning has been a major bottleneck in mechanistic interpretability because intervention-based methods require expensive per-edge ablation over high-dimensional SAE features. By making circuit discovery dramatically cheaper while preserving accuracy, CircuitLasso could make interpretability analysis practical for larger models and broader research groups, accelerating the field's ability to explain LLM behavior. CircuitLasso operates on SAE features rather than raw polysemantic neurons, avoiding the interpretability problems of raw neurons while sidestepping the prohibitive cost of intervention-based circuit learning on high-dimensional SAE features. It efficiently uncovers relationships among SAE features, showing how human-interpretable semantic features propagate through the model and influence predictions; the paper is a preprint (arXiv:2606.16939v2) with no community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer how large language models compute, often by identifying 'circuits' — subgraphs of model components that jointly produce a specific behavior. Raw neurons are polysemantic, meaning a single neuron responds to many unrelated concepts, which makes circuits hard to interpret; sparse autoencoders (SAEs) address this by decomposing activations into sparse, more monosemantic features. However, SAE feature spaces are very high-dimensional, so existing intervention-based circuit learning methods that ablate individual edges become computationally prohibitive, motivating cheaper approaches like CircuitLasso.

## [w²VLA Decouples Declarative and Procedural Knowledge in Robot Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21496) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.21496v2) proposes w²VLA, a Vision-Language-Action model that restructures information flow so that visual, spatial, and skill information modulates the robot state sequence compositionally instead of feeding all multimodal tokens into a large opaque transformer-based action expert. The authors report that this modular design successfully decouples knowledge representations, enabling robust behavior cloning and zero-shot skill transfer to dissimilar, unseen objects. Current state-of-the-art VLA models perform well in-distribution but remain brittle to minor spatial, semantic, and task variations, which makes data collection for new objects intractable. If decoupling declarative concepts from procedural skills truly improves zero-shot transfer, it could reshape how generalist robot policies are designed and trained, reducing the need for object-specific demonstrations. The key architectural change is replacing the common practice of passing all VLM encoder tokens into a single large action expert with a compositional modulation scheme that injects visual, spatial, and skill signals directly into the robot state sequence. The paper is an arXiv preprint (v2, replace-cross) and, as of this writing, has no accompanying community discussion or peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take an image of the robot's surroundings plus a text instruction and directly output low-level robot actions; the paradigm was pioneered by Google DeepMind's RT-2 in 2023 and typically works by fine-tuning a billion-parameter Vision-Language Model (VLM) on large-scale robot trajectory datasets. In AI, declarative knowledge refers to concepts and entity semantics (what things are), while procedural knowledge refers to how to perform actions (how to do something). The paper argues that existing VLAs entangle these two types of knowledge in their parameters, which is why they struggle to transfer skills to novel objects.

## [RoboMME-Interference Benchmarks Robot Memory Under Distraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22338) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced RoboMME-Interference, a cross-session benchmark built on RoboMME that tests robot memory systems over long sessions with controlled numbers of unrelated distractor tasks. Running memory-augmented π_0.5 variants unmodified, they found perceptual memory success decays steadily as distractors accumulate, while subgoal variants retain more improvement, and adding a retrieval step restores no-distractor success at every interference level. Most existing robot-memory benchmarks rely on single episodes or short context, so this work exposes a critical gap in long-context memory and interference robustness for real-world robot deployments. It shows that current memory-augmented VLA systems largely fail on these capabilities, pointing to concrete directions for improving robot memory architectures. The benchmark constructs each query episode's session history from the relevant prior demonstration followed by a controlled number of unrelated sessions, which are fed to the VLA as memory. Perceptual variants improve success without distractors but decay strongly and steadily, subgoal variants improve less at their best but hold more of that improvement, and a retrieval step selecting the most visually similar history section restores no-distractor success at every interference level.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models combine visual perception, language understanding, and robot control, and memory-augmented variants aim to let robots recall information from earlier sessions. RoboMME is a large-scale benchmark for memory-augmented robotic manipulation that evaluates how well models remember, reason, and act across temporal, spatial, and object-related memory demands. π_0.5 is a VLA model from Physical Intelligence built on π_0 that targets open-world generalization, and RoboMME released memory-augmented π_0.5 variants used in this study.

## [Spec Growth Engine Fights AI Context Explosion and Spec-Code Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27045) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2606.27045v2) introduces the Spec Growth Engine, a lightweight framework that uses a machine-readable spec graph, a Spine context assembler, a vertical-slice growth protocol, and a drift gate to address context explosion and silent spec-code drift in AI-assisted software development. As AI coding agents accelerate implementation, the two structural failure modes it targets—context explosion and invisible spec-code drift—increasingly degrade output quality and create costly repair work, so a lean, machine-enforced approach could directly improve agent workflows for practitioners and researchers. The framework separates contract from design within spec graph nodes, scopes agent context to an ownership path via the Spine assembler, enforces hardest-first ordering through a vertical-slice growth protocol, and makes spec-code divergence a blocking merge condition; it synthesizes established principles such as Parnas information hiding, C4, ADRs, Walking Skeleton, Reflexion Models, and Fitness Functions without the overhead of heavyweight frameworks like RUP or MDA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Spec-driven development is a methodology where a formal specification serves as the authoritative source of truth, with code, tests, and other artifacts generated from that shared context. AI coding agents must reason over a repository within a limited context window, and as that window fills, output quality can degrade—a problem known as context explosion. Meanwhile, spec-code drift occurs when code evolves but the specification does not, leaving the divergence invisible until it becomes costly to repair.

## [AD-CERT Combines Adversarial Distillation with IBP for Certified Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31653) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce AD-CERT, a certified training objective that combines adversarial distillation over the logit space with an Interval Bound Propagation (IBP) upper bound. AD-CERT achieves state-of-the-art certified performance on several robustness benchmarks for ReLU networks, and logit-level distillation improves certified accuracy over robust feature-space distillation by up to 5.40 percentage points. Certified training typically forces a painful trade-off between standard accuracy and formally verifiable robustness, so a method that improves both is valuable for safety-critical deployments. By showing that adversarial distillation can serve as an effective lower-bound surrogate, AD-CERT offers a practical recipe for researchers in adversarial ML and formal verification. The method distills adversarial information from an empirically robust teacher at the logit level, using it as a lower-bound surrogate alongside the loose IBP upper bound. Experiments focus on ReLU networks and standard robustness benchmarks, and the code is released at https://github.com/matteo-melis/ad-cert.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Certified robustness aims to produce neural networks whose predictions can be formally verified to remain correct under bounded adversarial perturbations. Certified training methods based on tight relaxation bounds yield certifiable models but hurt standard accuracy, while adversarial training improves empirical robustness yet produces models that are hard to certify. Interval Bound Propagation (IBP) is a loose but efficient over-approximation of the worst-case loss, and prior work showed that combining adversarial objectives with IBP interpolates between lower and upper bounds to improve the accuracy-certification trade-off.

## [105 Language Models Converge on the Same One-Word Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12796) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.12796v4) introduces the "One-Word Census," testing 105 language models from over twenty labs on 96 single-turn prompts that each name a category with many valid one-word answers, asked eight times per model and scored by exact match. The study finds strong convergence: asked to "pick a word," 46% of models chose "serendipity," and in 28 of 96 categories a single answer took at least 80% of all responses. This concentration of answer choices has implications for model diversity, evaluation, and bias, suggesting that heavily post-trained assistants from major labs are the most conformist while lightly post-trained and persona-tuned models are the most divergent. The finding that the field is more concentrated than human category-production norms in 18 of 20 shared categories raises concerns about the homogenization of AI-generated content. The study uses answer-choice surprisal—the average -log2 probability of a model's answers under the pooled answers of all other models, leave-one-out—with no embeddings and no judge. Within major providers' lineages, release order shows no panel-wide trend once model tier is controlled; GPT, Gemini, Grok, and Qwen become more conformist across releases, while Claude's generation-5 releases reverse this trend. On open checkpoints of three post-training pipelines, supervised fine-tuning is the largest step toward the field's answers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Language models are often evaluated on their ability to generate diverse and creative responses, but when faced with open-ended prompts with many equally valid answers, they may default to statistically common choices learned during training. Post-training techniques such as supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning from human feedback shape model behavior, and persona tuning adjusts a model's style or character. This study systematically measures how much models converge on the same answers, providing a new instrument for understanding model behavior and bias.

## [Decoupled RL Framework Boosts Low-Voltage Grid Congestion Management Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16004) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.16004v2) proposes a reinforcement learning framework that decouples congestion detection from control by pairing a random-forest violation pre-classifier with an actor-critic controller for low-voltage distribution grids. Tested on a real low-voltage grid under synthetic future scenarios with sparse observability, the controller reduced total violation magnitude by 98.9% with accurate grid parameters, and performance remained nearly unchanged under measurement noise, though grid-model mismatch proved more challenging. As photovoltaic generation, electric vehicle charging, and heat pumps increasingly stress low-voltage grids, distribution system operators need curative curtailment methods that work despite sparse sensors and imperfect models. This decoupled approach offers a more robust alternative to end-to-end RL, potentially making RL-based congestion management practical for real-world grids with limited observability. The framework separates violation detection (random forest) from control (actor-critic), and was evaluated under measurement noise and grid-parameter mismatch on a real low-voltage grid with low observability and controllability. While measurement noise barely affected performance, grid-model mismatch degraded results more noticeably, though the controller still mitigated most violations under the tested assumptions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Low-voltage distribution grids are increasingly congested due to distributed energy resources like rooftop solar, EV chargers, and heat pumps, which can push voltages or currents beyond operating limits. Curative curtailment—reducing or shifting flexible loads and generation—is a key remedy, but it is hard when the grid has few sensors (sparse observability) and operators only have approximate models. Reinforcement learning, especially actor-critic methods that combine a policy (actor) with a value estimator (critic), has been explored for such control, but end-to-end RL can be brittle to noise and model errors. Random forests are ensemble classifiers that average many decision trees to improve accuracy and robustness.

## [CovGPO Fixes All-Failed Groups in Long-Horizon Agent RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22724) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.22724v2) introduces Coverage-conditioned Group Policy Optimization (CovGPO), which uses first-visit observation coverage to assign relative advantages only when every rollout in a group receives zero outcome reward. Experiments on ALFWorld and WebShop show consistent improvements over group-based baselines, with especially large gains on hard tasks. Group-based policy optimization methods like GRPO are widely used to train LLM agents from sparse outcome rewards, but they stall on difficult long-horizon tasks where every sampled trajectory fails. By breaking this self-perpetuating credit trap, CovGPO offers a practical way to keep learning signal alive in the hardest agentic training regimes, which could benefit researchers and practitioners training LLM agents for multi-step tasks. The method only activates coverage-based advantages when a group's samples all receive zero outcome reward, so it does not corrupt the original reward signal; reaching new observations is treated as a prerequisite for task success. The paper is a preprint without community discussion or independent validation yet, so its broader impact remains to be established.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Group-based policy optimization, such as Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), trains LLMs by comparing trajectories or steps within a sampled group and computing relative advantages, avoiding the need for a separate critic model. In long-horizon agentic tasks, rewards are sparse and delayed, so credit assignment—determining which action caused a future reward—is difficult. When repeated or low-effect actions dominate the policy's high-probability region, useful state-changing actions are under-sampled, producing many all-failed groups whose relative advantages provide no learning direction.

## [LLM Benchmarks Miss Modality, Search, and Citation Effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06202) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.06202v2) audits a widely-used LLM by comparing ChatGPT's chat UI against OpenAI's API, with and without web search, using 401 stratified prompts from BBQ and SafetyBench and 4,812 total responses across three repeated runs per prompt. It finds that chat UI responses were less accurate than API responses with search disabled, that enabling web search reduced accuracy by up to 8 percentage points and even reversed modality performance trends on one benchmark, and that repeated runs produced inconsistent answers in up to 21% of prompts. The results show that standard single-run, API-only accuracy scores can obscure behavioral variation that matters for AI safety, reliability, and deployment-readiness claims. This could push benchmark designers and evaluators to systematically report modality, multi-run consistency, search conditions, and response-level behaviors instead of a single accuracy number. The audit used a stratified sample of 401 prompts from BBQ (a bias benchmark) and SafetyBench (a safety benchmark), collecting 4,812 responses over three repeated runs per prompt, and measured response consistency, text similarity, citation grounding, and abstention behavior in addition to accuracy. The two modalities grounded answers in different citations, and abstention behavior was inconsistent across modalities, illustrating that even within one model family, simple accuracy metrics hide important behavioral variation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: BBQ (Bias Benchmark for QA) is a hand-built dataset of question sets that tests whether QA models rely on harmful social stereotypes across nine social dimensions in U.S. English contexts. SafetyBench is a comprehensive multiple-choice benchmark of 11,435 questions spanning seven categories of safety concerns, used to evaluate how well LLMs identify and refuse unsafe content. Citation grounding refers to whether a model's answers are supported by verifiable sources, and abstention refers to a model declining to answer when it lacks sufficient information. Most current LLM evaluations rely on a single access modality (model APIs), a single run per prompt, and accuracy as the primary metric, which this paper argues is insufficient.

## [STAIR: Agentic Planning Framework Unifies Incident Response with Graph-as-State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09524) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released STAIR, an end-to-end agentic incident response framework that maintains incident evidence, response actions, and execution feedback in a shared Graph-as-State (GAS) representation, using a Stage Router to dispatch planning to stage-specialized agents. On the new STAIR-Bench benchmark of 44 Docker-based cyber-range cases, STAIR achieved an overall defense score of 0.95, roughly 10.47% above the strongest baseline, and cut cumulative penalties by about 28.8% in the independent CAGE4 environment. Existing AIOps methods automate fault diagnosis and remediation but lack a unified decision basis across planning and execution, which limits them during long-horizon, multi-stage security incidents. By tying planning and execution to one evolving incident state and reusing effect-validated experience, STAIR points toward more reliable autonomous response for live software systems, relevant to security operations and AIOps practitioners. STAIR retrieves effect-validated historical experience based on the current state and stage to guide action selection, while an Execution Harness runs actions, feeds results back to update GAS, and validates action effects for future reuse. Evaluation uses 44 Docker-based cyber-range cases in STAIR-Bench plus the independent CAGE4 environment with ongoing attacks and benign activity, though the abstract does not detail failure modes or computational cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Incident response planning aims to restore compromised software systems, and AIOps tools already automate parts of fault diagnosis and remediation using feedback. However, these tools often treat planning and execution separately, making it hard to track incident state and align actions with changing recovery goals across long, multi-stage attacks. Graph-as-State means the incident itself is represented as a graph whose nodes and edges encode evidence, actions, and feedback, so the latest graph serves as the single decision basis. Agentic frameworks use LLM-driven agents that plan and act autonomously, and STAIR specializes them by response stage.

## [DASC Compresses Recurrent State Checkpoints for Hybrid Linear-Attention Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30386) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.30386v2) introduces Decay-Aware State Compression (DASC), which derives per-head and per-channel "retention horizons" from the decay structure of Gated DeltaNet (GDN) and Kimi Delta Attention (KDA), then selects long-horizon state units and packs them into a ragged checkpoint layout. On Kimi-Linear, conservative DASC configurations compress KDA recurrent state checkpoints by 2.63x while staying close to full caching, cutting mean Time to First Token (TTFT) by 42.6% and raising input throughput by 68.4% under fixed memory budgets. Hybrid linear-attention models promise near-constant KV cache growth, but prefix reuse forces serving engines to store recurrent-state checkpoints, which reintroduces memory pressure, evictions, and repeated prefill. DASC attacks exactly this bottleneck, so it matters to anyone deploying long-context or high-throughput inference on models like Kimi-Linear and Qwen with GDN, where TTFT and input throughput directly determine serving cost. DASC derives retention horizons directly from model weights, balances compressed checkpoints across tensor-parallel ranks, and on reuse either zero-fills omitted units or refreshes them from a bounded suffix at extra compute cost. The paper reports that suffix refresh recovers much of the accuracy lost under aggressive omission, and that the same quality-efficiency trend appears for Qwen with GDN, showing the method generalizes from channel-wise KDA to head-wise GDN.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Linear attention replaces the ever-growing key/value (KV) cache of standard transformers with a fixed-size recurrent state, and hybrid architectures such as Kimi-Linear and Qwen3-Next interleave these linear layers with a few full-attention layers. Gated DeltaNet (GDN) is a linear attention mechanism using a gated delta rule for memory management, while Kimi Delta Attention (KDA) refines it with finer-grained, per-channel decay gating. Because linear layers update their state in place, serving systems that support prefix caching must periodically save state checkpoints alongside the full-attention KV cache, and storing all of them in full causes memory pressure, evictions, and repeated prefill work.

## [Defense-as-Skill: Runtime Guard Skill Protects Skill-Augmented Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01487) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Defense-as-Skill, a paradigm that implements a runtime guard as an installable, inspectable, and editable skill called SkillSonar, which checks sensitive actions against the user's task boundary and routes them to allow, replan, or confirmation decisions. It also introduces SCOPE-R, a task-conditioned dataset with 206 attack-confirmed malicious instances and 43 benign tasks across 6 risk families and 21 sub-categories, and uses Monte-Carlo Tree Search to evolve the guard skill, reducing in-distribution attack success rate from 0.482 to 0.104 and out-of-distribution ASR from 0.606 to 0.115 on repeated GLM-5 runs. This work addresses a timely security gap in skill-augmented agents, where malicious skills can persist in runtime context and only trigger unsafe actions after a concrete task makes them appear useful, rendering pre-install vetting insufficient. By making runtime guards skill-native, transparent, and evolvable, it offers a practical defense paradigm for AI agent security that could influence how agent platforms like Claude Code and OpenClaw handle untrusted skills. SkillSonar runs alongside untrusted task skills without modifying the underlying agent runtime, and the guard-skill evolution uses Monte-Carlo Tree Search over rollouts to improve the on-disk guard skill. The gains transfer across victim models, held-out risk families, and external benchmarks, though the guard remains an advisory control rather than a hard enforcement mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Skill-augmented agents load reusable skills as persistent runtime context to improve task performance, but this also gives malicious skills a durable channel for steering future actions, such as leaking secrets, corrupting code, bypassing approvals, or staging data for exfiltration. Pre-install vetting is insufficient because an unsafe action may only appear useful after a concrete user task and workspace state are present, which calls for runtime, task-conditioned protection. The paper's approach makes the guard itself an installable skill, so it can be inspected and edited like any other skill, and it is evaluated on SCOPE-R, a benchmark covering multiple risk families.

## [Test Boundary Choice Inflates Hardware-Trojan Detector Scores on Trust-Hub](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07199) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.07199v2) measures how the choice of test boundary inflates or deflates reported performance of feature-based hardware-Trojan detectors on Trust-Hub data, using 49,124 gates from 16 netlists grouped into five host families. Holding the parser, 36 gate features, class weighting, model settings, threshold, and family-level aggregation fixed, random forest F1/AP falls from 0.914/0.978 with pooled gates to 0.636/0.851 with one netlist held out and 0.460/0.577 with a host family held out, while XGBoost drops from 0.946/0.976 to 0.464/0.544. The results suggest that widely reported hardware-Trojan detection scores on Trust-Hub may be inflated by data leakage, because sibling variants of the same host circuit can appear in both training and test folds. This is a methodological caution for the hardware-security community: benchmarks with multiple variants of one host should report family-aware holdouts and all family results alongside pooled scores. The gap persists across feature removal, repeated model and simulator seeds, score normalization, parser-related exclusions, and a smaller sample, though its size changes; logistic regression loses AP but its fixed-threshold F1 is not monotonic. The authors also note that a gate-weighted average is dominated by the larger ISCAS files, so headline values give each host family one vote, and they treat the five family rows as descriptive evidence rather than independent trials.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Trust-Hub is an NSF-sponsored resource that provides benchmark circuits with hardware Trojans inserted, widely used to compare detection techniques. Many machine-learning detectors extract gate-level features and train classifiers on these benchmarks, but because Trust-Hub reuses host circuits across files that differ mainly in the inserted Trojan, gates from sibling variants can leak host logic into both training and test folds. Data leakage in machine learning occurs when training uses information that would not be available at prediction time, producing misleadingly high metrics.

## [SUN: Jointly Scoring Novelty and Reachability for RL Goal Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08642) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SUN (SUccessor-to-Novelty), a reachability-aware goal-selection framework that scores candidate goals jointly by novelty and reachability using successor value functions, and can be plugged into any off-policy RL algorithm. The authors prove SUN equals the value of a count-scaled goal-reaching reward, bounds short-horizon hitting probabilities, and rejects unreachable goals, then add an adaptive selection strategy with a lightweight pseudocount, backed by benchmarks showing consistent gains over state-of-the-art methods in mazes, obstacle, irreversible-transition, and unbounded-space environments. Goal selection in goal-conditioned RL has long traded off novelty and reachability by hand, in sequence, or by ignoring one signal entirely, which wastes samples on unreachable goals or fails to explore. SUN offers a principled, theoretically grounded way to combine both signals inside any off-policy learner, potentially improving exploration efficiency for sparse-reward, long-horizon tasks such as robot navigation and manipulation. The core indicator SUN is derived from successor value functions and is proven to be the value of a count-scaled goal-reaching reward, with bounds on short-horizon hitting probabilities and explicit rejection of unreachable goals. The method uses an accurate yet lightweight pseudocount to avoid the overhead of classic count-based exploration, and code is released at https://github.com/sparisi/rl-anvil.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning agents learn by trial and error, and exploration—deciding which states or goals to pursue—is especially hard when rewards are sparse. Goal-conditioned RL addresses this by training policies to reach many subgoals, but choosing which subgoals to practice requires balancing novelty (states rarely visited) against reachability (states the agent can actually get to). Successor features/value functions are a value-function representation that decouples environment dynamics from reward, making them useful for reasoning about reachability across goals; off-policy algorithms learn from data collected by a different behavior policy, improving sample efficiency.

## [Study Maps Adversarial Transfer Across 60 Deepfake Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10002) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled arXiv study evaluates adversarial transferability across 60 deepfake detectors spanning six backbones, two pretraining regimes, and five training-data configurations, using AutoAttack (AA) and the Carlini-Wagner attack with Expectation over Transformation (CW-EOT). It finds that transfer success is strongly shaped by source-target compatibility, with mean attack success rates of 7.21% under AA and 19.52% under CW-EOT, while a multi-source oracle combining both attacks reaches 64.48% mean ASR after excluding exact backbone and training-data matches. The findings establish source-target compatibility and source-model selection as central dimensions of credible black-box robustness evaluation, meaning prior deepfake detector benchmarks that average over non-target sources may substantially understate real vulnerability. This affects both attackers designing transfer-based black-box attacks and defenders who need to report robustness against realistic threat models. The compatibility structure is attack-dependent: exact backbone compatibility has the largest effect under AA, whereas shared pretraining regime and training data have the largest effects under CW-EOT. The authors release 240,000 adversarially perturbed images, complete pairwise transfer results, detector configurations, and evaluation code, though the abstract is truncated and no community discussion is available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial examples are inputs perturbed so that machine-learning models misclassify them, and transferability refers to the phenomenon where examples crafted on a source surrogate model also fool a different target model. In black-box attacks the attacker cannot query the target, so transferability is essential; AutoAttack is a widely used ensemble attack and the Carlini-Wagner attack is an optimization-based method often combined with Expectation over Transformation to make perturbations robust. Deepfake detectors are models that distinguish AI-generated or manipulated media from authentic content, and their robustness under such transfer attacks is a growing security concern.
