## [JoyInside-VLM-Agent: Product-Conditioned Multimodal Policy Learning for Embodied Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12957) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced JoyInside-VLM-Agent, a ReAct-style multimodal agent for embodied tasks on robotic platforms and smart hardware, trained through product-conditioned trajectory construction and staged policy learning. The pipeline combines supervised fine-tuning, multi-teacher on-policy distillation, and a novel reinforcement learning method called DPER (Dual-Source Privileged On-Policy Self-Distillation with Token-Level Evidence Routing), achieving a BFCL v4 overall score of 77.44 that exceeds evaluated baselines. The work targets a persistent challenge in embodied AI: translating multimodal observations and user requests into executable actions while adapting to product constraints, changing goals, and execution feedback. If the reported gains on JoyInside-Devices and JoyInside-Robotics hold up, the approach could inform how VLM-based agents are trained for real-world robotic and smart-hardware deployments. A closed-loop sandbox generates multimodal interaction trajectories covering goal revisions and failure recovery, which are checked against execution outcomes and semantic criteria. DPER routes token-level evidence between a frozen privileged teacher and a privileged student sharing the rollout policy's parameters, modulating reward-derived token advantages while preserving their signs; training rewards combine generative process evaluation, multi-judge rankings, and task-queue consistency. While the system leads baselines on all reported JoyInside-Devices and JoyInside-Robotics metrics, results on other public benchmarks are mixed.

**Background**: ReAct-style agents interleave reasoning traces with actions, allowing a model to observe, think, and act in a loop; multimodal versions extend this to images and other sensor inputs. Embodied agents must ground such reasoning in physical execution, where goals can change and actions can fail. Privileged self-distillation is a training technique in which a model that has access to extra information (the privileged teacher) supervises a student that only sees the normal context, and on-policy distillation means the student learns from its own rollouts rather than fixed offline data. Multi-teacher on-policy distillation aggregates several specialized teachers to combine their capabilities into one student model.

## [MotionCoach: Multimodal Agent Guides Professional Video Motion Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12963) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MotionCoach is a trained multimodal agent that uses multi-turn tools and self-verification to produce a grounded prompt and a reference clip, injecting external professional motion knowledge into video editing models. It was cold-started on MotionCoach-SFT-10k and optimized with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) on MotionCoach-RL-2k, and it improves the overall score from 55.1 to 65.6 on Seedance 2.0 and from 51.2 to 64.5 on MiniMax-H3. Professional motions in performance, sports, and martial arts require fine-grained technique and temporal structure that existing video editing models often fail to reproduce, such as incorrectly lifting the front wheel under a Stoppie instruction. By learning to construct multimodal guidance without retraining the video editing models, MotionCoach offers a practical way to extend general-purpose editors to specialized domains. The method relies on a data pipeline that constructs trajectories for training, and evaluation is conducted on VPMEBench, a new benchmark of 314 professional motion editing tasks with fine-grained references and annotations. Rewards during GRPO are applied to the motion guidance prompt, the motion outcome video, and the output format.

A new arXiv paper (2610.13066) proposes Evolving Creatives with Clicks (ECC), which reformulates e-commerce ad creative optimization as fine-grained attribute editing rather than generating images from scratch. ECC uses ANOVA and Random Forest regression to identify 28 attributes significantly correlated with click-through rate (CTR), then instruction-tunes a prompt model and refines it with real click preferences via a new Consistent Preference Optimization (CPO) strategy. Most existing CTR-driven creative methods penalize all attributes of an underperforming image equally, even ones that actually help clicks; ECC's selective editing could make ad optimization more precise and cost-effective for e-commerce platforms and advertisers. The paper claims substantial CTR improvements over state-of-the-art models, and promises to release the full data pipeline, dataset, and source code upon acceptance. ECC extracts attribute values from online ad images using multimodal large language models (MLLMs), then identifies the 28 CTR-sensitive attributes through statistical analysis, turning a vast editing space into a structured set of actionable dimensions. Its CPO strategy enforces consistency across semantically equivalent shuffled suggestions to mitigate DPO overfitting and improve preference generalization.

## [First Black-Box Uncertainty Estimation Method for AMR Parsers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13236) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13236) presents the first study of uncertainty estimation for Abstract Meaning Representation (AMR) parsers in a black-box setting, deriving uncertainty from pairwise similarities among top-k beam-search candidates and evaluating it with the Prediction Rejection Ratio (PRR). The work characterizes the relationship between parsing performance and uncertainty, assesses the reliability of beam candidate distributions, and identifies parsers that best balance performance and reliability. AMR parsers have become increasingly accurate, but knowing when their outputs can be trusted remains largely unexplored, so this work adds a complementary reliability dimension to parser evaluation. It could help downstream applications decide when to accept, reject, or defer an AMR parse, and it extends black-box uncertainty estimation techniques to structured semantic parsing. The method is black-box, requiring only the top-k beam-search candidates rather than internal model states or probabilities, and uncertainty is computed from pairwise candidate similarities. Evaluation uses the Prediction Rejection Ratio, which measures how well uncertainty estimates correlate with actual errors by comparing rejection curves against oracle and random baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Abstract Meaning Representation (AMR) is a semantic formalism that abstracts away from a sentence's syntactic form and represents its meaning as a rooted, labeled graph, and AMR parsing is the task of automatically producing such graphs from natural language. Uncertainty estimation aims to quantify how confident a model is in a prediction, and black-box methods do so using only input-output behavior, which is useful when model internals are unavailable. Beam search is a decoding strategy that keeps the top-k most likely partial outputs, and the Prediction Rejection Ratio is a standard metric for judging whether uncertainty scores can reliably rank predictions by correctness.

## [Sigmoid Label Enables Sub-Degree DOA Estimation in CNN-Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13433) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.13433) by Lifeng Wang, Xintong Li, and Aifei Liu proposes a sigmoid label encoding for CNN-Transformer networks used in direction-of-arrival (DOA) estimation. By encoding source angles as a sum of angle-shifted monotonic sigmoid functions and differentiating the network output, the method achieves sub-degree accuracy and 1-degree resolution with a 0.25-degree grid, outperforming Gaussian, 0-1 hard, and binary cross-entropy (BCE) label baselines. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of classification-based DOA estimation—grid quantization error—which restricts accuracy and resolution in applications like radar, sonar, and wireless localization. By breaking the grid-step limit without drastically refining the grid, it offers a practical path to high-resolution DOA estimation with deep learning, potentially benefiting array signal processing systems that require precise angle estimation. The sigmoid label encodes DOAs as a cumulative distribution function composed of sigmoid functions, and differentiating the CNN-Transformer output yields sharp spectral peaks at the true angles. The method uses a moderately refined grid step of 0.25 degrees, achieving sub-degree accuracy and 1-degree resolution, and is compared against Gaussian, 0-1 hard, and BCE labels under a unified CNN-Transformer backbone. The official code is available on GitHub.

CNN-Transformer networks combine convolutional neural networks for local feature extraction with transformers for long-range dependency modeling, and have been increasingly applied to signal processing tasks.

## [AudioTETRIS: Training-Free Token Elimination for Efficient Audio LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13600) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AudioTETRIS introduces a training-free, two-stage framework that uses the audio encoder's representation geometry as an acoustic prior to score token importance, then applies progressive top-k token selection across LLM decoder layers. Evaluated on Qwen2-Audio-7B, Phi-4-Multimodal, and Audio Flamingo 3, it retains near-uncompressed performance using only 25% of audio tokens and matches or surpasses full-token accuracy at moderate compression rates. Audio encoders produce hundreds of tokens even for short clips, and self-attention's quadratic scaling makes inference costly; this method cuts that cost without retraining or auxiliary models, making long-form speech understanding more practical on existing LALMs. It also challenges the prevailing assumption that audio tokens can be treated as generic, isotropic features like visual tokens. The key insight is that speech information density peaks at acoustic transitions, so treating tokens as isotropic features smooths over spectral transitions and destroys fine-grained phonetic cues; AudioTETRIS instead propagates encoder geometry into the decoder to compute a per-token fused score. It requires no additional training or auxiliary models, and consistently outperforms existing training-free baselines, though results are reported only on three specific models.

**Background**: Large Audio Language Models (LALMs) combine an audio encoder with an LLM decoder to handle speech-text tasks, but the encoder emits hundreds of tokens for even short clips, and self-attention cost grows quadratically with sequence length. Existing training-free compression methods prune or merge tokens based on coarse attention weights or feature similarity, which can erase the fine-grained phonetic transitions needed for accurate speech decoding. AudioTETRIS belongs to this training-free token-compression line of work, alongside methods like SpeechPrune and HeadRouter.

## [Training Budgets, Not Just Architecture, Drive ARC Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13660) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A controlled study on ARC-AGI-1 crossed five pretraining budgets with four test-time training (TTT) budgets for the vision-inspired VARC and recursion-based TRM models. It found that increasing TTT sample presentations improves pass@2 for both models, but increasing the TTT task-variant count consistently helps only TRM, while VARC often performs better with fewer variants. The results suggest that comparisons of ARC architectures are confounded by differing training and evaluation protocols, so claims about architectural superiority may be overstated. This motivates the community to report results at comparable pretraining and TTT budgets, which could change how future ARC models are evaluated and ranked. Each budget specifies a task-variant count and total sample presentations, defining the controlled training budgets. Notably, VARC remains competitive without pretraining, whereas TRM benefits much more from pretraining, indicating that the two architectures have different sensitivities to the pretraining and TTT budget axes.

VARC reframes ARC as an image-to-image visual reasoning problem, while TRM is a recursion-based model; both have been evaluated on ARC but under differing training protocols. ## [SafeDiSTL: Credit Assignment for Joint Constraints in Safe MARL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13737) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SafeDiSTL is a constrained multi-agent reinforcement learning framework that uses Signal Temporal Logic (STL) robustness and differentiable action sensitivities to assign credit for joint constraint violations across agents. It achieves 1.6-5.1x the safe-success rate in cooperative payload transport, improves navigation safe-success by 11.1%, and delivers 50-68% more throughput in networked traffic control compared to constrained baselines. This work addresses a key challenge in safe multi-agent reinforcement learning: when a team-level constraint is violated, it is unclear which agents are responsible, making credit assignment difficult. By enabling per-agent credit assignment from joint constraint measurements, SafeDiSTL could improve safety and coordination in real-world multi-agent systems such as autonomous vehicle fleets and distributed robotics. The method uses STL robustness to measure episode-level violations via running-maximum cost increments, and leverages smooth full-episode robustness and differentiable dynamics to compute action sensitivities that allocate each cost increment across agents at the same decision time, accounting for delayed effects. The abstract is truncated and no community discussion is available to gauge impact.

Signal Temporal Logic (STL) is a formal language for specifying temporal properties over real-valued signals, and its robustness score quantifies how well a signal satisfies a specification, which can be used to measure constraint violations.

## [MCGPO Improves Credit Assignment in Long-Horizon LLM Agent Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13755) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2610.13755) proposes Matched-Context Group Policy Optimization (MCGPO), a method that constructs weighted action preferences from already-collected trajectories by defining comparison contexts with pre-action state summaries and observed rubric progress. Across six environment-backbone settings with three training seeds each, MCGPO improves mean success over matched GraphGPO by 0.47–1.46 percentage points on ALFWorld and WebShop with Qwen2.5-1.5B and 7B, and by 3.96 and 5.16 points on WebShop with Qwen3-4B and 8B. Credit assignment — determining which intermediate decisions in a long trajectory led to a sparse terminal reward — is a central bottleneck in reinforcement learning for LLM agents, and MCGPO offers a critic-free way to extract finer-grained learning signal from rollout data that is already collected. If the reported gains hold up, the approach could make agent training more sample-efficient without requiring additional reward models or value networks. MCGPO reuses rollout data and each response's original history to preserve critic-free training, using two-action support to select among ordered exact and coarse matching rules, fixed folds to separate candidate decisions from terminal outcomes, and support-dependent evidence weights that scale an auxiliary pairwise objective alongside shared GraphGPO task learning. The ALFWorld gains build on a baseline success rate above 92%, where remaining headroom is limited, and three-seed factorial controls support the opposite-fold evidence and evidence-weighting design choices.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning for LLM agents typically relies on sparse, outcome-level rewards, which makes it hard to know which token, reasoning step, or tool call actually caused success or failure — a problem known as credit assignment. GraphGPO is a prior method that aggregates rollout trajectories into a unified state-transition graph to enable finer-grained, structure-aware credit assignment. MCGPO builds on GraphGPO by adding matched-context comparisons between sibling rollouts, so that action preferences are learned from evidence that is comparable across contexts.

<a id="item-308"></a>
## [Quantile Loss Beats MSE in Tabular Deep Regression, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13909) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A systematic re-evaluation of regression objectives across established tabular datasets and multiple neural backbones shows that many proposed alternatives to Mean Squared Error (MSE) provide limited or inconsistent gains, while quantile loss consistently improves aggregate performance. The authors also identify that regions with different target noise levels reach optimal predictive performance at different training epochs, meaning a single global early-stopping checkpoint can force a compromise between uncertainty regions. This work provides a modern, practical guide for practitioners choosing loss functions in tabular deep learning, showing that a well-tuned MSE baseline is hard to beat and that quantile loss is a reliable upgrade. It also highlights a fundamental limitation of global early stopping that affects any model trained on heteroskedastic data, which is common in real-world tabular problems. The study introduces a heteroskedasticity-aware extension of quantile loss that uses input-dependent label smoothing as a remedy and improves over standard quantile loss on several backbones. Code is available at https://github.com/yandex-research/tabular-regression.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular data—structured data organized in rows and columns—remains a common but challenging domain for deep learning, where models like MLPs and Transformer-based architectures compete with gradient-boosted trees. Regression tasks in this setting typically use Mean Squared Error (MSE) as the default loss, but MSE assumes constant noise (homoskedasticity) and can be sensitive to outliers. Quantile loss (also called pinball loss) instead estimates conditional quantiles of the target distribution, making it more robust to heteroskedastic noise and outliers. Early stopping is a standard regularization technique that halts training when validation performance stops improving, but it typically selects one global checkpoint for the entire model.

## [UxSID Uses Semantic IDs for Ultra-Long User Interest Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09040) ⭐️ 6.0/10

UxSID introduces a semantic-aware framework for ultra-long user sequence modeling that uses Semantic IDs (SIDs) and a dual-level attention strategy to capture target-aware preferences without the heavy cost of item-specific models. It achieves state-of-the-art performance and a 0.337% revenue lift in a large-scale advertising A/B test. This work offers a third path between item-specific search and item-agnostic compression, potentially improving efficiency and effectiveness for industrial recommender systems and advertising platforms that must handle extremely long user histories. The measurable revenue lift in a large-scale A/B test suggests practical value for deployment. The framework is end-to-end and balances computational parsimony with semantic awareness, using semantic-group shared interest memory rather than item-specific search or item-agnostic compression. The reported 0.337% revenue lift comes from a large-scale advertising A/B test, though the abstract does not detail dataset scale or latency constraints.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

Existing paradigms typically use item-specific search (e.g., SIM, TWIN) or item-agnostic compression. Semantic IDs are content-aware discrete item representations that encode semantic meaning, helping address cold-start and long-tail issues in recommender systems. Dual-level attention combines two attention processes to capture different levels of importance.

## [MPMMine: A New Benchmark Suite for Constraint Acquisition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26279) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced MPMMine, a benchmark suite, Python package (mpmmine-py), and leaderboard tool designed to systematically evaluate constraint acquisition (CA) algorithms that discover, validate, and enhance Mathematical Programming (MP) models. The suite provides alternative MP model formulations per problem, tens of parameter instantiations per model, and thousands of exemplary solutions and non-solutions across discrete, continuous, and mixed domains, along with natural-language descriptions for text-to-model methods. Existing benchmarks for constraint acquisition are loosely organized, designed for solver benchmarking rather than CA algorithms, and often omit details needed for reproducibility, which hinders fair comparison and algorithmic progress. MPMMine addresses this gap by offering a standardized, extensible, and open benchmark with a leaderboard that runs algorithms in isolated containers, potentially accelerating research in the niche CA community. MPMMine follows design rules for consistency, standardization, completeness, extensibility, openness, and version control, and relies on open formats. The leaderboard leverages open-source implementations and runs them in isolated containers, adapting input/output to their needs to build an interactive scoreboard.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Constraint Acquisition (CA) is the process of automatically learning constraints or models from examples and domain knowledge, often used to build or refine constraint programs. Mathematical Programming (MP) models are mathematical representations of optimization problems, and validating or enhancing them with domain knowledge artifacts is an active research area. Benchmarks are essential for comparing algorithms fairly, but existing ones for CA are inadequate, motivating the development of MPMMine.

## [Agent Step Value Audits Evaluation-Channel Sensitivity in Black-Box Agent Traces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04419) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.04419v5) defines Agent Step Value (ASV) as a channel-indexed before/after target-margin gain and uses a matched 2x2 design to detect state-by-channel interactions in frozen PubMed question-answering transitions. It reports that cross-channel gain signs disagree on 48.3% of comparisons, versus only 0.3% for same-channel retries. The findings suggest that evaluator-derived step rewards can flip sign depending on which evaluation channel is used, which threatens the reliability of reusing such rewards in agent training and reward modeling. This matters for researchers building agent evaluation pipelines, since a reward that looks positive in one channel may be negative in another. Transition interactions are nearly balanced in sign (median +0.014; 10% trimmed mean -0.033), but asymmetric negative magnitudes produce a positive direct mean and a negative generated-view mean under the empirical target mixture. Matched retrieval designs localize the negative component to the generated-view coordinate, and a 512-token source-contract follow-up shows source-only generation restores the positive mean direction on artifact-bearing retrievals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly evaluated by reusing step-level rewards produced by evaluators, but it is unclear whether the direction of those rewards stays stable across different evaluation channels. Black-box agent traces are execution logs of agent runs that cannot be inspected internally, making it hard to tell whether a reward signal reflects real task progress or artifacts of the evaluation setup. This paper introduces a metric and a matched experimental design to audit that sensitivity before step rewards are reused.

## [SLIFT selectively learns from user feedback with dual LoRA adapters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09109) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.09109v2) introduces SLIFT, a selective self-learning framework that decomposes each user feedback message into atomic Fix, Spec, or Null components relative to the original task. It then trains two complementary LoRA adapters on a shared frozen backbone — a Generalist for task-validity fixes via feedback-conditioned self-distillation, and a Specialist for condition-specific refinements — reporting strong results on MemoryBench and WildFB. Most LLM self-improvement pipelines treat each feedback message as a single uniform update signal, which can over-generalize narrow corrections or ignore useful ones. SLIFT's task-relative decomposition and dual-adapter design offer a more granular way to align models with real user interactions, potentially improving persistent personalization and reducing harmful drift in deployed assistants. The framework classifies feedback components as Fix (requirements for task validity), Spec (compatible condition-specific refinements), or Null (no reliable positive update direction), with Null components inducing no update at all. The Specialist adapter observes only the task and the Generalist's response to supply residual guidance, and code is released at an anonymous repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adds small trainable matrices to selected layers while keeping the original model weights frozen, making it cheap to train multiple adapters on one backbone. Self-distillation lets a model learn from its own outputs or privileged traces without an external teacher, and is increasingly used to turn sparse feedback into denser training signals. SLIFT combines these ideas to update LLMs from user interactions at different generalization scopes.

## [H2Table Uses Hierarchical Hypergraphs to Boost LLM Table Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01216) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.01216v2) introduces H2Table, a framework that represents complex tables as hierarchical nested hypergraphs and processes them with a tailored hypergraph encoder plus learnable query vectors that feed structural embeddings into an LLM. On the HiTab dataset, H2Table reports an average improvement of 22.88% over state-of-the-art baselines on highly complex tables with a nesting depth of four, with code released on GitHub. Most current LLM table-reasoning pipelines linearize tables into token sequences, which discards the two-dimensional and hierarchical relationships that make complex tables meaningful. By explicitly modeling these structures, H2Table points toward more reliable question answering over real-world tables such as financial reports and scientific data, an area where LLMs still struggle. The framework models headers as hyperedges and cells as nodes, enabling message passing that captures semantic entailment between them, while learnable query vectors act as a lightweight bridge to extract representative structural embeddings into the LLM. The reported 22.88% average gain is specific to highly complex tables with nesting depth of four on HiTab, and the work remains a preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Tables are common in documents but hard for large language models because text-based models must flatten a two-dimensional grid into a one-dimensional sequence, losing row-column and nested-header relationships. Hypergraph neural networks are a graph-learning technique in which an edge (hyperedge) can connect more than two nodes at once, making them well suited to represent a header that spans multiple cells. H2Table combines these ideas by building a hierarchical hypergraph from a table and encoding it before passing structural information to an LLM.

## [Multi-Harness RL for Coding Agents Shows Modest Gains, Strong Harness Dependence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04518) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A controlled study (arXiv:2609.04518v2) trains two Qwen3-8B RL variants from the same SFT checkpoint using identical replay data, differing only in whether rewards are normalized within each harness (Within) or across harnesses (Cross). On SWE-bench Verified, multi-harness RL yields only modest gains over the SFT baseline: estimated mean improvements of +0.48 and +0.77 percentage points for Within and Cross, respectively, across four training harnesses. The results suggest that simply adding more execution harnesses to RL training does not reliably transfer to unseen interfaces, which matters for teams investing in agentic RL pipelines for coding. They also indicate that the choice of execution harness affects measured performance far more than the RL training itself, so benchmark comparisons across harnesses should be interpreted cautiously. Cross normalization increases the fraction of trajectories with nonzero advantage but provides no consistent advantage over Within on an unseen harness across training seeds and evaluation budgets, and neither rule significantly beats SFT on that unseen harness. After averaging over model variants, the highest and lowest source-harness solve rates differ by 7.13 percentage points, far exceeding the estimated training gains.

**Background**: Coding agents are LLM-based systems that solve software issues by generating patches, and they are typically evaluated on SWE-bench Verified, a benchmark built from real GitHub issues. An execution harness is the interface or scaffolding through which the agent runs commands, edits files, and receives feedback during training or evaluation. Reinforcement learning for such agents often uses reward normalization to stabilize training when reward scales vary across tasks or environments, and multi-harness training aims to make agents robust to different interfaces.

## [IBBench-Light Introduces Paired Evaluation for Task-Conditioned LLM Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13725) ⭐️ 6.0/10

IBBench-Light introduces Paired Exact-Contract Accuracy (PECA), a benchmark that tests whether instruction-tuned models can correctly handle both execute and process directives applied to the same external record. Across 144 matched pairs per model, Qwen succeeded on 132 execute and 109 process prompts but completed only 97 pairs, and in a pinned Phi rerun changing the end-of-sequence (EOS) token set swung exact paired success from 0/144 to 62/144. This work shows that marginal per-task averages can hide paired failures that matter for real applications where the same input must be handled differently depending on the user's intent. It also demonstrates that seemingly minor generation configuration choices, such as the EOS token set, can dramatically change measured instruction-following performance, which has implications for how LLM benchmarks are designed and reported. The benchmark uses twelve semantic bases yielding 144 matched pairs per model, with four quantized instruction models producing 1,152 archived greedy responses, plus 1,722 logged CPU generations for directive-absent controls, additional semantic bases, wording changes, and generation stopping tests. The authors note that task margins and paired counts must be read together with the stopping policy, and they include a bounded IHEval comparison using the same SmolLM2 checkpoint and output budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Instruction-tuned language models are expected to follow user directives, but a single external record can be used in different ways: it may contain a procedure to execute or text to read, depending on the request. Most benchmarks evaluate each task separately and report average accuracy, which can mask cases where a model succeeds on one use but fails on the other for the same record. IBBench-Light addresses this by pairing both uses of the same record and requiring both members to satisfy their output contracts, while also auditing factors like literal-target exposure, case normalization, and generation stopping.

## [Claim-Gated Audit Framework Targets Omitted Source Relationships in Generative Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29145) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.29145v2) specifies a claim-gated audit of the query-source-answer tuple for generative search, where an omission is only resolved when relationship evidence, answer adoption, materiality, and disclosure are all observed. The authors provide a reference checker that reproduces all 81 three-state predicate combinations on an exhaustive synthetic suite and rejects 192 deliberately malformed records. Generative search answers can cite a supported passage while silently omitting a source relationship that changes how the answer should be interpreted, so this formal specification offers a way to separate citation support from omission risk. It matters for AI safety and information retrieval because it defines the annotation, held-out evaluation, and paired utility tests still needed before such auditing can be validated for real deployment. The specification binds decisions to versioned evidence spans and treats incomplete evidence as unresolved rather than as independence, with common-guard baselines and predicate ablations isolating endpoint logic from missing-evidence handling. The authors explicitly note these are finite contract-conformance results, not detector accuracy or evidence of improved user outcomes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Generative search systems, such as AI-powered search assistants, produce answers that cite web sources, but a citation can be technically supported while the answer still omits a relationship between sources that would change its meaning. Information retrieval traditionally focuses on identifying and ranking relevant resources for a query, whereas this work adds a formal audit layer that gates claims on four conditions: relationship evidence, answer adoption, materiality, and disclosure. The paper uses a three-state predicate logic (resolved, unresolved, and presumably a third state) to make the audit record contract executable.

Researchers introduced GraphDecide, a model-independent benchmark that evaluates System One models such as Jev on graph decision tasks using structural task profiles, matched graph-text input contrasts, and heuristic-proposal controls. The abstract notes that joint graph-text input does not consistently improve prediction and that feasible construction does not establish high solution quality; code and aggregate results are available on GitHub.

<a id="item-319"></a>
## [TypedBench: A New Benchmark for Typed Decision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11392) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TypedBench is a new benchmark for evaluating System One decision models that output calibrated probabilities over typed answers, built from seven policy-labelled generators and nine evaluation suites. It reports accuracy as median and range across paraphrases, and calibration error relative to the finite-sample noise floor of a matched, perfectly calibrated predictor. As typed decision models like Jev move into software pipelines where thresholds, cost-weighted choices, and escalation rules act on probabilities, miscalibration or wording sensitivity can trigger unintended actions with no textual rationale for operators to inspect. TypedBench addresses the lack of a clear reference evaluation for these non-generative models, which matters for anyone deploying them in production decision systems. The benchmark evaluates a hosted model, an open encoder, and a family of open decoders spanning 0.8B–9B parameters on identical items, using selective prediction, ordinal proper scoring rules, and realised cost under asymmetric cost matrices. The hosted model follows the stated policy but is wording-sensitive and systematically underconfident, and under asymmetric costs its probabilities can perform worse than simply taking its top answer, while decoders route exactly but slow down as options or questions are added and are least accurate on policy questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: System One models are AI decision models that return typed answers — such as categorical choices, ordinal levels, or binary outcomes — with calibrated confidence through a non-generative interface, rather than producing chat prose. Because software acts on these probabilities via thresholds and cost-weighted rules, the quality of the probabilities themselves, not just accuracy, determines whether the resulting actions are appropriate. Calibration refers to how well predicted probabilities match observed outcome frequencies, and framing sensitivity refers to how much results change when the same question is worded differently.

<details><summary>References</summary>

<a id="item-320"></a>
## [New Measure-Theoretic Approach to Semantic Information Resolves Bar-Hillel-Carnap Paradox](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00525) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper critiques Floridi's theory of strongly semantic information and proposes a measure-theoretic approach based on the unit circle to quantify semantic information without the Bar-Hillel-Carnap paradox. The approach, analogous to von Neumann's quantum probability, assigns zero informativeness to contradictions and tautologies, and finds that mutually contradictory messages are equally informative. This work addresses a long-standing paradox in the quantification of semantic information, potentially offering a more coherent foundation for information theory and philosophy of information. It could influence how researchers model informativeness in logic, AI, and communication. The paper shows that Floridi's distance-metric and parabolic approach fails to fully remove the paradox, while the unit-circle measure space meets all of Floridi's requirements. The analogy to von Neumann's quantum probability provides a rigorous mathematical framework, and the utility of equal informativeness for contradictory messages is illustrated with an example.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Bar-Hillel and Carnap's classic theory of semantic information defines informativeness as inversely related to probability, but this leads to the counterintuitive result that a contradiction is maximally informative—the Bar-Hillel-Carnap paradox. Floridi proposed a theory of strongly semantic information based on truth-values and a distance metric to resolve the paradox, but the new paper argues it does not succeed. The proposed unit-circle approach draws on measure theory and quantum probability to construct a new measure space for informativeness.

## [Preference-Conditioned Multi-Objective RL for Heavy-Duty Vehicle Tactical Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18783) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2601.18783v3) proposes a preference-conditioned multi-objective reinforcement learning framework that explicitly represents safety, energy efficiency, and time efficiency as three competing objectives for tactical decision-making in heavy-duty vehicles. The method combines Generalized Policy Improvement and Proximal Policy Optimization to approximate the Pareto front, and is evaluated in a scalable highway traffic simulation tailored for heavy-duty trucks. Heavy-duty vehicles operate under demanding commercial constraints where safety, fuel/energy cost, and delivery time must be balanced simultaneously, and conventional scalar reward aggregation tends to obscure these trade-offs. A single preference-conditioned policy that can shift between driving behaviors without retraining could make multi-objective RL more practical for adaptive autonomous driving in commercial fleets. The framework quantifies safety via collisions and successful completion, energy efficiency via energy cost, and time efficiency via driver cost, yielding smooth and interpretable Pareto fronts across different traffic densities. Evaluations against an analytical baseline show the learned policies capture meaningful trade-offs, though results are limited to simulation rather than real-world deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-objective reinforcement learning (MORL) seeks policies that balance several often conflicting objectives, and preference-conditioned approaches train a single policy that can adapt to different user-specified trade-off preferences. Generalized Policy Improvement is a technique that reuses previously learned policies to accelerate learning of new ones, while Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) is a widely used, stable policy-gradient algorithm for deep RL. Heavy-duty vehicle tactical decision-making involves higher-level choices such as lane selection and speed planning under commercial constraints, making it a natural testbed for MORL.

## [ITO: Multi-View Alignment and Training-Time Fusion for Image-Text Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02767) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2603.02767) introduces ITO, a framework for image-text contrastive pretraining that combines multimodal multiple alignment from multi-view image augmentations with a lightweight training-time multimodal fusion module. The fusion module is discarded at inference, so the standard dual-encoder efficiency is preserved while the encoders are regularized during training. The work targets a known weakness of CLIP-style pretraining—representations that remain partly organized by modality rather than semantics—and reports consistent gains over CLIP at the 100M–1B image-text pair scale under identical data, backbone, and compute. If the results hold, it could offer a low-cost way to improve multimodal models without changing inference architecture. The paper claims the gains are not explained by extra training compute or image augmentation alone, and that the fusion module's contribution grows with data scale while stabilizing optimization and mitigating late-stage overfitting common in aggressive contrastive learning. Code is released at https://github.com/showstarpro/ITO, though the work is a preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Image-text contrastive pretraining, popularized by CLIP, trains paired image and text encoders to pull matching pairs together and push mismatched pairs apart, enabling zero-shot classification and cross-modal retrieval. Multi-view augmentation creates several augmented versions of each image to provide richer supervision, while fusion modules typically combine modality features—here used only during training as a regularizer.

## [MemReread Boosts Agentic Long-Context Reasoning via Memory-Guided Rereading](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10268) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces MemReread, a memory-guided LLM agent that decomposes tasks into sub-questions and performs targeted rereading when its final memory is insufficient, avoiding intermediate retrieval. It also adds a reinforcement learning framework that improves length extrapolation and adaptively sets the number of rereading passes; MemReread-4B outperforms DeepSeek-V4-Pro by 1.6 percentage points on 2WikiMultiHopQA while keeping linear time complexity. This work addresses two persistent weaknesses in agent-memory systems for long-context reasoning: evidence loss during memory formation and interference from invalid queries. By recovering prematurely discarded indirect evidence and enabling non-linear reasoning, it could improve multi-hop question answering and other tasks where agents must reason over very long documents without quadratic attention costs. MemReread builds on streaming reading and extends the ReAct paradigm by unifying sub-question generation, question-guided rereading, and memory-based sub-answer derivation into a single function call. The reinforcement learning component adaptively controls computational overhead by deciding how many rereading passes are needed based on task complexity, and the method maintains linear time complexity with respect to context length.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context reasoning tasks often exceed the context window of standard transformers, whose attention cost grows quadratically with input length. Agent-memory approaches instead process document chunks sequentially while maintaining a dynamically updated memory, but they can lose evidence when memory is overwritten and may be misled by irrelevant queries. MemReread is a memory-guided LLM agent that decomposes tasks, rereads targeted sections, and updates its memory until sufficient evidence is gathered, similar in spirit to the ReAct paradigm for interleaving reasoning and actions.

## [EchoDistill boosts noisy-audio robustness in large audio language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23954) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose EchoDistill, a noisy-to-clean self-distillation framework that uses clean audio as privileged information during post-training, where a noisy-input student samples candidate responses while a frozen copy of the same backbone processes the corresponding clean audio. Across three LALM backbones and three audio domains at -10dB, EchoDistill improves average noisy-input accuracy by 1.63 percentage points over the strongest baseline, raising Qwen2.5-Omni noisy-input accuracy from 59.33% to 62.94% and clean-audio accuracy from 76.56% to 77.56%. Acoustic noise is a persistent weakness for large audio language models, and EchoDistill improves robustness without adding any inference cost since only the student is retained at inference time. This makes it a practical post-training recipe that could be applied to existing LALM backbones to make real-world speech and audio assistants more reliable in noisy environments. EchoDistill combines masked response-token distillation, task-gated consistency shaping, and teacher-referenced group-relative optimization to align noisy-input generation with clean-conditioned semantics. Replacing matched audio with random, shuffled, or silent inputs reduces accuracy by 3.08-6.42 points, confirming that matched acoustic evidence drives predictions, though gains do not reliably extend to non-additive distortions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Large Audio Language Models (LALMs) are multimodal systems that combine audio encoders with large language model backbones to enable joint audio-text understanding, as seen in models like Qwen-Audio and Audio Flamingo. Self-distillation is a training technique where a model learns from its own predictions or checkpoints rather than a separate, larger teacher model, and here the same backbone serves as both student and frozen teacher. Group-relative policy optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning method that reduces variance in policy gradient updates by comparing groups of sampled responses, and EchoDistill adapts this idea with teacher references.

## [MyoSem aligns EMG signals with natural-language action semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00174) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MyoSem is a new framework that maps hand electromyography (EMG) signals and multi-view natural-language action descriptions into a shared feature space, enabling bidirectional EMG-text retrieval. It builds semantic prototypes from multi-view descriptions, encodes EMG with local activation-aware representations, and uses semantic queries to aggregate local EMG evidence, achieving the strongest overall bidirectional retrieval performance across seven protocols on NinaPro DB2, EMG2Pose, and NinaPro DB2→DB3. This work moves EMG-based hand action understanding from fixed-label gesture recognition toward language-queryable semantic retrieval, which could improve human-computer interaction and prosthetic control by allowing users to query actions in natural language. It also opens the door to more flexible, cross-user and cross-population EMG decoding, including able-bodied-to-amputee transfer. MyoSem is evaluated under seven protocols covering random splits, user shifts, held-out action classes, and able-bodied-to-amputee transfer, and ablations show how semantic prototypes, activation-aware encoding, and semantic queries shape the learned space. As an arXiv preprint with no accompanying community discussion, its immediate broader impact remains limited.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Electromyography (EMG) measures the electrical activity produced by skeletal muscles using electrodes on the skin or inserted in the muscle, and is widely used for hand gesture recognition, prosthetic control, and wearable interaction. Existing EMG methods typically treat hand action understanding as prediction over fixed gesture labels, leaving EMG signals and action-semantic text unaligned in a common feature space. NinaPro DB2 is a standard sEMG dataset with data from intact subjects and transradial amputees, commonly used to benchmark myoelectric control systems.

## [Wall-Clock State Monitors Fail as Moment Detectors on Agent Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19386) ⭐️ 6.0/10

An arXiv paper (2606.19386v2) corrects a prior 'State Saturation Trap' finding, revealing that the original affect engine received dt=0 between actions, making the published trap a pure-accumulator artifact. A pre-registered sweep over dt in {0..600}s on 20 trajectories shows wall-clock-calibrated monitors have only two regimes: constant alarms at dt<=1s and silence at dt>=60s, with every critical dt falling in (1,30]s. This distinction between sample-time and wall-clock calibration is critical for AI safety and runtime verification, as it implies that common leaky-integrator monitors cannot detect meaningful moments in variable-cadence agent streams. The finding affects how researchers design and interpret runtime monitors for autonomous agents, potentially preventing false alarms or missed interventions. A scriptable gpt-5.4-mini agent loop measures median latency of 1.53s (p90 2.33s), placing it inside the trap regime, while a sample-time CUSUM over the identical stream is exactly dt-invariant (20/20). A rising-edge trigger with hysteresis fires only 0-3 times per trajectory in every condition, and transition detection escapes the trap at every cadence but does not recover human intervention timing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime monitors for autonomous agents often threshold an accumulated internal state, such as a behavioural baseline, drift statistic, or modelled affective state. CUSUM is a sequential analysis technique for change detection that accumulates deviations from a target, while exponential moving average (EMA) and affect models use wall-clock half-lives in seconds. On fixed-rate streams, sample-time and wall-clock calibration coincide, but on agent streams where inter-action time varies by orders of magnitude, they diverge.

## [First-Order In-Context Shapley Enables Scalable LLM Data Auditing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22766) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces an inference-only valuation pipeline that computes the first-order term of an in-context cooperative game over semantic k-NN neighborhoods in O(k) forward passes without parameter updates, correlating positively with multi-shot Monte-Carlo in-context Shapley on 100% of targets at 19x less compute. It also projects pairwise log-likelihood shifts onto a directed influence graph to characterize each record via three signals: outgoing Exemplifying Advantage, incoming Exemplified Advantage, and a topological One-of-a-Kind Score. This addresses a real bottleneck in LLM alignment where preference and instruction-tuning corpora accumulate label noise, safety inversions, and inter-annotator contradictions that deduplication misses and full-corpus LLM judging cannot afford. The method's ability to pre-filter 1%-5% of records while achieving 4.5x enrichment in confirmed errors on HelpSteer2 and 2.2x on Anthropic HH-RLHF could make data auditing practical for large-scale alignment pipelines. During DPO training, confirmed contradictions oppose the update far more often than clean records (56.7% vs. 37.0% in LoRA gradients) and stay below chance in reward accuracy. Auditing the HH-RLHF benchmark exposes label errors where aligned models disagree with human labels 73%-83% of the time, and retraining DPO after repairing or filtering flagged records improves reward accuracy by +0.5%-0.6% overall and +3.1%-3.5% on repaired inversions, even without LLM arbitration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Data Shapley is a game-theoretic framework for equitably valuing individual data points, but exact computation requires intractable retraining, and embedding surrogates like KNN-Shapley fail to separate high- from low-utility demonstrations (ρ = 0.0003 with generative utility). Monte Carlo Shapley approximation is a sampling-based method that approximates true Shapley values when exhaustive computation is infeasible, but it remains computationally expensive for large language model alignment datasets.

## [ThinkNet: Validation-Controlled Evolutionary Ensembles for MI-EEG Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33967) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ThinkNet introduces a validation-controlled evolutionary deep ensemble framework for subject-independent motor-imagery EEG classification that explicitly prevents held-out subject information from leaking into preprocessing, model selection, or ensemble selection. On the four-class BCI Competition IV-2a benchmark with nine Leave-One-Subject-Out folds, its validation-selected compact decoder reached 44.35±15.41% accuracy with only 4.9K parameters, 19 KB FP32 weights, and 0.99 ms batch-1 Orin CUDA inference. Held-out-subject performance in MI-EEG decoding is frequently overstated because test-subject data silently influences normalization, hyperparameter tuning, or ensemble selection, so a framework that enforces strict validation control offers a more honest and reproducible evaluation protocol. This matters for assistive and rehabilitative brain-computer interfaces that must generalize to new users under limited target-user data and constrained compute. Across a broader search over ten decoder families, compact models (≤25K parameters) achieved higher mean held-out accuracy than mid-size and large alternatives after selected retraining (40.10% vs. 35.09% and 34.78%), and validation-gated ensembling improved over single-model inference to 43.98±16.25% in the fixed benchmark. A non-deployable oracle analysis exposed a 6.1-point family-selection gap and near-zero validation–test correlation, showing that validation reliability under subject shift remains the key bottleneck.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Motor-imagery EEG (MI-EEG) decoding lets a user control a brain-computer interface by imagining movements, but signals vary greatly across people, so subject-independent decoders are needed. Deep ensembles combine multiple neural networks to improve accuracy and robustness, while evolutionary algorithms search over candidate configurations using mutation, recombination, and selection. ThinkNet applies evolutionary search under strict validation control so that no held-out subject data influences any design decision.

## [EXAQC Evolves Quantum Circuits for Hybrid Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03220) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper extends EXAQC, an evolutionary framework for automated quantum circuit discovery, to hybrid quantum-classical image classification, evolving parameterized quantum circuits (PQCs) as intermediate modules while keeping classical feature-extraction and prediction layers. On MNIST, Fashion-MNIST, and CIFAR-10, EXAQC reaches 98.42%, 90.62%, and 85.47% accuracy respectively, and achieves 85.68% on CIFAR-10 with over 25x fewer trainable parameters than a 10-layer CNN. Most hybrid quantum-classical models still rely on hand-designed or fixed circuit ansatze, which may not suit the task and create a manual design bottleneck. Automating circuit discovery could make compact quantum modules practical drop-in replacements for larger classical components in vision architectures, which matters for near-term quantum machine learning on limited hardware. The evolved hybrid models use comparable gate counts to other quantum architecture-search methods, and encoding choice strongly affects results: rotation-based encodings (RX, RY, U3) outperform amplitude encoding by 22-25 points on CIFAR-10. The work is an incremental extension of EXAQC rather than a fundamentally new method, and it lacks immediate community discussion or independent validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Hybrid quantum-classical neural networks combine parameterized quantum circuits (PQCs) with classical deep learning layers. A PQC, often called an ansatz, is a quantum circuit with adjustable parameters whose structure, gate composition, and qubit connectivity must be chosen in advance. EXAQC (Evolutionary eXploration of Augmenting Quantum Circuits) is a framework that uses evolutionary algorithms to automatically design and optimize such circuits, and this paper applies it to image classification for the first time.

## [AgroGround Builds 794,850-Example Dataset for Grounded Agricultural Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04425) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AgroGround introduces a large-scale dataset and automated pipeline that converts labels from eight agricultural VQA datasets into 794,850 instruction examples for disease lesion and object localization. Fine-tuning a shared vision-language model on combined recognition-and-localization instructions raises recognition accuracy from 51.8% to 72.6% and joint accuracy from 19.2% to 43.3%, while healthy-image negatives lift abstention to 95.0%. Reliable agricultural diagnosis requires both identifying what is present and localizing the evidence, yet existing agricultural VQA datasets rarely link semantic labels to image regions. AgroGround provides a benchmark measuring joint correctness of identity and localization plus abstention on healthy images, which could support more trustworthy precision-agriculture tools. Grounding-only fine-tuning actually reduces recognition accuracy from 51.8% to 29.1%, showing that localization supervision alone can harm classification; the resulting 2B model exceeds its annotation teacher in grounding F1 on the benchmark and on the external PlantSeg test set, and reinforcement learning further improves lesion-level grounding. Evaluation uses 1,480 human-verified images disjoint from all training data.

Agricultural VQA datasets contain rich semantic labels about plant diseases and other targets, but they usually only ask questions and answer them in text without indicating where in the image the evidence lies. AgroGround bridges this gap by automatically deriving region annotations from those existing labels, enabling models to both recognize and localize agricultural targets.

## [LinSlot applies Linear Representation Hypothesis to slot-based attribute discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10722) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces LinSlot, a probabilistic framework that jointly discovers object (slot) and attribute (block) representations from raw images by leveraging the Linear Representation Hypothesis (LRH), rather than assuming a uniform factorization as in prior block-slot attention methods. Experiments on multiple datasets show improved DCI scores over state-of-the-art methods, empirical evidence for LRH in slot space, and disentangled representations that enable image editing. This work advances unsupervised disentangled representation learning by connecting the interpretability-motivated Linear Representation Hypothesis to object-centric slot attention, potentially improving how models discover attributes without labels. It matters for researchers in computer vision and representation learning who care about interpretable, editable, and compositional visual representations. LinSlot formulates a probabilistic graphical model linking images, slots (objects), and blocks (attributes), uses block attention to connect attribute representations to slots, and incorporates LRH in both object and attribute representation spaces while optimizing the Evidence Lower Bound (ELBO). The approach is evaluated via DCI scores, a standard metric for disentanglement, and demonstrated on image editing tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Slot Attention is an object-centric learning module that binds perceptual features into a set of slots, each intended to represent an object in a scene. Block-slot attention extends this by factorizing each object slot into attribute blocks, but typically assumes a uniform factorization that may not match the true structure of attributes. The Linear Representation Hypothesis posits that composable concepts are encoded as linear directions or subspaces in neural representations, an idea popular in mechanistic interpretability research on large language models. LinSlot brings this hypothesis into slot-based vision models to guide attribute discovery.

<a id="item-332"></a>
## [Constitutional Gating and Deterministic Recovery for Multi-Agent LLM Negotiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11542) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.11542v2) introduces a three-part control stack—a 5-Pillar runtime constitution, a 4-tier swarm (Director, three-agent majority vote, Monitor, schema hard gate), and Cognitive Annealing—for multi-agent LLM negotiation against a stateful adversarial Gatekeeper. In 30 runs using Gemini 2.5 Pro agents and a Claude Haiku 4.5 Gatekeeper, atomic purge plus a canonical strike escaped a honeytrap-to-compliance deadlock 5/5 times versus 0/5 for LLM-only steering (Fisher p = 0.008). The results show that deterministic, non-LLM recovery mechanisms can reliably break compliance deadlocks where LLM-only steering fails, which is directly relevant to building robust autonomous agents that must refuse harmful requests while still completing negotiations. The finding that an LLM Monitor approved strikes that then failed a deterministic pre-flight check also highlights the limits of LLM-based oversight in safety-critical control loops. The constitution and Director made an acceptable framing possible but not reliable (0/5 baseline unlocks versus 1/5 and 2/5 with the constitution), and when the swarm unlocked it did so in one turn with 7-8 calls and about 15k tokens (67-73% below baseline), but when it did not it cost 17-38% more. Pre-registered hypotheses on average call and token reduction were not supported, and LLM-written strikes failed the deterministic pre-flight 5 of 5 times although an LLM Monitor had approved four.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems often waste model calls in polite loops, malformed outputs, or compliance deadlocks when negotiating with a stateful counterpart that has hidden acceptance conditions. Constitutional AI is an approach that uses an explicit set of written principles to guide model behavior, and this paper applies a runtime constitution plus a tiered swarm architecture and a deterministic deadlock-recovery routine called Cognitive Annealing. The testbed uses a released adversarial Gatekeeper whose acceptance rules are fixed regular expressions and whose LLM only renders reply text, so the task measures whether the stack executes a constitution-aligned strategy rather than discovering a novel solution.

<a id="item-333"></a>
## [Sera Adds Semantic Degradation Cues to Battery Health Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11567) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Sera, a semantic representation aggregation framework that complements temporal models for battery state-of-health (SoH) forecasting with degradation semantics extracted from time series using both rule-based knowledge and LLM-based interpretation. On a mainstream benchmark across multiple prediction horizons and temporal models, Sera reduced prediction error by up to 37.3% over the temporal baseline and improved generalizability. Battery SoH forecasting underpins battery management, predictive maintenance, and safety planning in electric vehicles and energy storage, where nonlinear degradation and cell-to-cell heterogeneity make accurate prediction hard. By making degradation semantics explicit and interpretable, Sera could help engineers trust and act on forecasts in critical applications. Sera builds two complementary semantic representations — one from rule-based domain knowledge and one from LLM interpretation — encodes them independently, and fuses them with the temporal model's representation via gated aggregation. Counterfactual analysis shows forecast responses align with the meanings of key degradation descriptors across tested horizons, though evaluation is limited to a single mainstream benchmark.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Oct 13, 04:00

**Background**: State of health (SoH) measures how much capacity and performance a battery has lost over its lifetime, and forecasting it means predicting future degradation from past data. Most existing data-driven methods rely on temporal models that learn only from numerical time series, leaving higher-level degradation characteristics implicit. Sera draws on battery domain expertise to turn those characteristics into explicit semantic representations, similar to how semantic representation aggregation has been used in other domains such as land cover classification.
